## 1. Lost on Campus: Evaluating Embodied Spatial Reasoning of Vision-Language Models in the Wild

**中文标题**: Lost on Campus: Evaluating Embodied Spatial Reasoning of Vision-Language Models in the Wild

**作者**: Zehan Zheng, Yanyuan Chen, Deming Li, Yutao Tang, Jianwen Xie et al.

**发布日期**: 2026-11-24

**arXiv ID**: [2611.33145v1](http://arxiv.org/abs/2611.33145v1)

**PDF**: [下载链接](http://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.33145v1)

**类别**: cs.CV

<details>
<summary><b>摘要 (Abstract)</b></summary>

Embodied Spatial Reasoning (ESR) is central to deploying Vision-Language Models (VLMs) in real-world embodied tasks, yet remains inadequately evaluated. Existing benchmarks primarily assess spatial reasoning in static images or synthetic indoor environments, where semantic shortcuts often suffice, making it difficult to faithfully evaluate spatial reasoning capabilities in the wild. In light of this, we present Lost on Campus, a benchmark for evaluating ESR in large-scale real-world outdoor 3D environments reconstructed by 3D Gaussian Splatting. Our benchmark introduces a unified reasoning-action evaluation framework that seamlessly integrates diagnostic QA for isolated reasoning with closed-loop interactive navigation for active reasoning under multimodal instructions. To enable fine-grained diagnosis, we evaluate six capability aspects of ESR: action grounding, spatial foresight, metric awareness, goal-directed planning, ego-allocentric localization, and spatio-temporal consistency. Extensive experiments reveal that: (i) Compared to indoor settings, real-world outdoor environments impose substantially higher demands on spatial reasoning, where existing models exhibit poor performance; (ii) VLMs still struggle with fine-grained visual alignment, with failures in spatial foresight and ego-allocentric localization emerging as primary bottlenecks; (iii) Improving multimodal interaction capabilities and long-term spatial reasoning is crucial for advancing embodied intelligence. These findings highlight that faithful evaluation of ESR demands benchmarks tightly coupling perception, reasoning and action within realistic and continuous environments.

</details>

<details>
<summary><b>中文摘要</b></summary>

Our benchmark introduces a unified reasoning-action evaluation framework that seamlessly integrates diagnostic QA for isolated reasoning with closed-loop interactive navigation for active reasoning under multimodal instructions. To enable fine-grained diagnosis, we evaluate six capability aspects of ESR: action grounding, spatial foresight, metric awareness, goal-directed planning, ego-allocentric localization, and spatio-temporal consistency. Extensive experiments reveal that: (i) Compared to indoor settings, real-world outdoor environments impose substantially higher demands on spatial reasoning, where existing models exhibit poor performance; (ii) VLMs still struggle with fine-grained visual alignment, with failures in spatial foresight and ego-allocentric localization emerging as primary bottlenecks; (iii) Improving multimodal interaction capabilities and long-term spatial reasoning is crucial for advancing embodied intelligence. These findings highlight that faithful evaluation of ESR demands benchmarks tightly coupling perception, reasoning and action within realistic and continuous environments.

</details>

## 2. EventDiffusion: Learning Native Event Stream Distribution from Videos

**中文标题**: EventDiffusion: Learning Native Event Stream Distribution from Videos

**作者**: Karthik Sundar Senthilkumar, Christopher Thirgood, Simon Hadfield

**发布日期**: 2026-11-24

**arXiv ID**: [2611.33096v1](http://arxiv.org/abs/2611.33096v1)

**PDF**: [下载链接](http://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.33096v1)

**类别**: cs.CV, cs.AI

<details>
<summary><b>摘要 (Abstract)</b></summary>

Event cameras capture asynchronous brightness changes at high temporal resolution, but scarce recordings constrain data-driven research. Sparse RGB observations cannot uniquely determine intervening events since identical frames may correspond to streams with different activity, polarities, and timing. Existing simulators largely rely on prescribed sensor dynamics and estimated brightness trajectories, whose assumptions limit the diversity and fidelity of plausible event streams. We introduce EventDiffusion, a hierarchical generative framework that directly learns the conditional distribution of native asynchronous event streams from real recordings. EventDiffusion decomposes generation into three probabilistic processes: Event Activity Diffusion (EAD) learns stochastic discrete event counts; Event Spatial Allocation (ESA) distributes these counts to individual pixels while preserving cardinality; and Event Timing Diffusion (ETD) learns continuous asynchronous timestamp distributions. We also introduce CEFS, an event-stream realism metric combining event activity and polarity with multiscale space-time structure and temporal fidelity. Across all datasets, EventDiffusion achieves double the accuracy of SOTA methods and demonstrates robustness across different cameras at any resolution.

</details>

<details>
<summary><b>中文摘要</b></summary>

Event cameras capture asynchronous brightness changes at high temporal resolution, but scarce recordings constrain data-driven research. Sparse RGB observations cannot uniquely determine intervening events since identical frames may correspond to streams with different activity, polarities, and timing. Existing simulators largely rely on prescribed sensor dynamics and estimated brightness trajectories, whose assumptions limit the diversity and fidelity of plausible event streams. We introduce EventDiffusion, a hierarchical generative framework that directly learns the conditional distribution of native asynchronous event streams from real recordings. EventDiffusion decomposes generation into three probabilistic processes: Event Activity Diffusion (EAD) learns stochastic discrete event counts; Event Spatial Allocation (ESA) distributes these counts to individual pixels while preserving cardinality; and Event Timing Diffusion (ETD) learns continuous asynchronous timestamp distributions. We also introduce CEFS, an event-stream realism metric combining event activity and polarity with multiscale space-time structure and temporal fidelity. Across all datasets, EventDiffusion achieves double the accuracy of SOTA methods and demonstrates robustness across different cameras at any resolution.

</details>

## 3. SHARPnP: Self-Supervised and Plug-and-Play Hyperspectral Super-Resolution from Real Noisy Unaligned Camera Measurements

**中文标题**: SHARPnP: Self-Supervised and Plug-and-Play Hyperspectral Super-Resolution from Real Noisy Unaligned Camera Measurements

**作者**: Mohamad Jouni, Brayan Monroy, Jorge Bacca, Mauro Dalla Mura

**发布日期**: 2026-11-24

**arXiv ID**: [2611.33032v1](http://arxiv.org/abs/2611.33032v1)

**PDF**: [下载链接](http://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.33032v1)

**类别**: cs.CV, eess.IV, eess.SP

<details>
<summary><b>摘要 (Abstract)</b></summary>

Affordable snapshot hyperspectral cameras capture spectral images in a single exposure but suffer from limited spatial resolution and sensor noise. Low-cost RGB cameras provide fine visible details to guide NIR reconstruction, but introduce spatially varying cross-camera misalignment. Supervised fusion methods require relatively large datasets of clean, high-resolution hyperspectral images, are sensitive to sensor noise, and generalize poorly across new sensors. We introduce SHARPnP, an interpretable, flexible framework coupling registration and reconstruction for RGB-guided hyperspectral super-resolution from noisy, unaligned measurements without clean references. We adapt a pretrained registration model via a cross-view loss backpropagated through a fixed plug-and-play fusion module. Temporal and recorrupted pairs enable reference-free self-supervision on noisy inputs. At test time, we calibrate cross-camera radiometry and refine alignment. Plug-and-play fusion employs sequential three-band denoising. On the REAL benchmark, SHARPnP outperforms supervised fusion baselines at $4\times$, $8\times$, and $16\times$ on clean inputs and at $4\times$ under Gaussian noise ($σ=5/255, 10/255$). On native RGB-HSI snapshot measurements, it reconstructs all 51 VIS-NIR bands, beyond the conventional 31-band VIS setting, without high-resolution references.

</details>

<details>
<summary><b>中文摘要</b></summary>

Plug-and-play fusion employs sequential three-band denoising. On the REAL benchmark, SHARPnP outperforms supervised fusion baselines at $4\times$, $8\times$, and $16\times$ on clean inputs and at $4\times$ under Gaussian noise ($σ=5/255, 10/255$). On native RGB-HSI snapshot measurements, it reconstructs all 51 VIS-NIR bands, beyond the conventional 31-band VIS setting, without high-resolution references.

</details>

## 4. RePix-VFI: Re-rendering Observed Pixels for Video Frame Interpolation with Generative Priors from One-Step Diffusion

**中文标题**: RePix-VFI: Re-rendering Observed Pixels for Video Frame Interpolation with Generative Priors from One-Step Diffusion

**作者**: Dayeon Kim, Jeonghyeok Do, Munchurl Kim

**发布日期**: 2026-11-24

**arXiv ID**: [2611.33016v1](http://arxiv.org/abs/2611.33016v1)

**PDF**: [下载链接](http://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.33016v1)

**类别**: cs.CV

<details>
<summary><b>摘要 (Abstract)</b></summary>

Multi-frame video frame interpolation (VFI) aims to reconstruct dense video from sparse keyframes, requiring accurate intermediate motion and faithful appearance. As temporal gaps grow, flow-based methods can retain observed textures but misplace them under ambiguous motion, whereas iterative latent-diffusion models can synthesize plausible content but incur substantial sampling costs and may alter details during reconstruction with a variational autoencoder (VAE). We present RePix-VFI, named for Re-rendering observed Pixels, a multi-frame VFI framework that couples draft-conditioned one-step video diffusion with reconstruction from observed keyframes. A pretrained video diffusion model refines a flow-rendered draft of the whole window in a single forward pass. For each target frame, our generative readback matches its two bounding keyframes to the generated prediction using an optical-flow estimator. The resulting correspondences guide re-rendering of original keyframe RGB pixels outside the VAE. A lightweight appearance fusion head combines these renderings with generated appearance. Experiments on benchmark datasets demonstrate significant improvements in reconstruction and perceptual quality. On SNU-FILM at $\times$8 and $\times$16, RePix-VFI achieves the highest PSNR and the lowest LPIPS, FloLPIPS, and tOF among the compared methods, exceeding the best evaluated diffusion-based baseline by 1.71 and 1.48dB in PSNR, respectively. It also runs 32--42$\times$ faster than the state-of-the-art multi-frame VFI diffusion model.

</details>

<details>
<summary><b>中文摘要</b></summary>

As temporal gaps grow, flow-based methods can retain observed textures but misplace them under ambiguous motion, whereas iterative latent-diffusion models can synthesize plausible content but incur substantial sampling costs and may alter details during reconstruction with a variational autoencoder (VAE). It also runs 32--42$\times$ faster than the state-of-the-art multi-frame VFI diffusion model.

</details>

## 5. Teaching Time Without Losing General Retrieval: Adapting Multimodal Embeddings for Time-Sensitive Video Retrieval

**中文标题**: Teaching Time Without Losing General Retrieval: Adapting Multimodal Embeddings for Time-Sensitive Video Retrieval

**作者**: Toya Oyama, Rainer Lienhart, Shin'ichi Satoh

**发布日期**: 2026-11-24

**arXiv ID**: [2611.32971v1](http://arxiv.org/abs/2611.32971v1)

**PDF**: [下载链接](http://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.32971v1)

**类别**: cs.CV

<details>
<summary><b>摘要 (Abstract)</b></summary>

In text-to-video retrieval, MLLM-based embedding models do well when the text describes the scene (general retrieval), but poorly when it describes actions and their direction (time-sensitive retrieval). TARA, a pioneering work, fine-tunes such a model on text triplets with temporal hard negatives; it improves time-sensitive retrieval but lowers general retrieval (mean R@1 over six benchmarks in our rerun on Qwen3-VL-Embedding-8B: 58.4->44.8), and its released model keeps general retrieval only when ensembled with Qwen3-VL-Embedding. Our goal is strong general and time-sensitive retrieval with one model and one vector per clip. Three findings lead to three simple components. Keep loss: general retrieval follows how far the output embeddings drift from the base model, not how far the weights move; we therefore keep them near the base model, which keeps most general retrieval even without general-domain data. Pair loss: the text embeddings of an action description and its reversed-direction version are well separated, but the video embeddings of a clip and its reversed copy are close; we therefore widen the gap between the video embeddings of each clip and its reversed copy, in the direction of the difference between their two text embeddings. Middle-layer LoRA: frames are combined in the middle layers, and temporal information peaks in or just after them; we therefore train LoRA only on these layers, which nearly matches training all weights on most time-sensitive benchmarks with 0.15% of the parameters and keeps general retrieval better. General R@1 stays at 58.4, while R@1 with each clip's reversed copy in the gallery rises from 55.5 to 63.2; the gains carry over to unseen benchmarks and InternVL2-8B-RA. The keep loss is also a remedy for the TARA recipe (general R@1: 44.8->57.8), and ensembling TARA with Ours gives the highest scores on RTime and unseen CiA among the compared models.

</details>

<details>
<summary><b>中文摘要</b></summary>

The keep loss is also a remedy for the TARA recipe (general R@1: 44.8->57.8), and ensembling TARA with Ours gives the highest scores on RTime and unseen CiA among the compared models.

</details>

## 6. VidForce: Predicting Force-Aware Articulation by Observing Humans

**中文标题**: VidForce: Predicting Force-Aware Articulation by Observing Humans

**作者**: Rafay Mohiuddin, Jiaqi Chen, Marc Pollefeys, Hermann Blum

**发布日期**: 2026-11-24

**arXiv ID**: [2611.32967v1](http://arxiv.org/abs/2611.32967v1)

**PDF**: [下载链接](http://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.32967v1)

**项目主页**: https://rafaymhddn.github.io/vidforce

**类别**: cs.CV, cs.RO

<details>
<summary><b>摘要 (Abstract)</b></summary>

People can anticipate how an object moves and the effort an interaction may require by watching others. How can embodied agents acquire this understanding from video? Force measurements provide direct sensory supervision, but require specialized equipment and remain limited in quantity. We introduce VidForce, which estimates an interaction trajectory together with a force profile from a third-person RGB video; 2D contact clicks and textual object-action prompts are optional. Our approach makes limited instrumented measurements useful for interpreting human interactions through learned physical prototypes as a prior. The prototypes share learned force levels and temporal profiles across interactions of comparable effort, with visual and semantic cues determining their contribution to each observation. Geometric and vision-language features adapt this prior to the observed interaction, while a joint conditional flow refines an initial tracked trajectory together with the force profile. Paired human and instrumented-gripper recordings provide measured force references for supervising human video, and a separately trained torque model demonstrates that the formulation extends to other sensory quantities. We evaluate trajectory recovery and force estimation through baseline comparisons and controlled ablations on held-out interactions from the Hoi! dataset. On this evaluation subset, VidForce achieves 6.6 cm ATE, peak-force MAE and RMSE of 4.81 N and 5.92 N, respectively, and force-profile RMSE of 5.52 N. On real-world videos, VidForce infers motion and force reliably and consistently despite the variability in human execution. We further deploy VidForce on a Spot robot, where trajectories and force profiles estimated from human demonstrations drive real-world articulated-object manipulation, and report success rates across repeated trials. Project page: https://rafaymhddn.github.io/vidforce

</details>

<details>
<summary><b>中文摘要</b></summary>

People can anticipate how an object moves and the effort an interaction may require by watching others. How can embodied agents acquire this understanding from video? Project page: https://rafaymhddn.github.io/vidforce

</details>

## 7. OmniHarness-RSI: Modality-Aware Harness Evolution for Omnimodal Understanding

**中文标题**: OmniHarness-RSI: Modality-Aware Harness Evolution for Omnimodal Understanding

**作者**: Shilin Ma, Xingming Shui, Jinglin Chen, Zetong Zhao, Jingqi Tian et al.

**发布日期**: 2026-11-24

**arXiv ID**: [2611.32951v1](http://arxiv.org/abs/2611.32951v1)

**PDF**: [下载链接](http://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.32951v1)

**类别**: cs.CV

<details>
<summary><b>摘要 (Abstract)</b></summary>

Recent advances in harness-based recursive self-improvement (RSI) have improved video understanding by evolving executable agent workflows without updating foundation model parameters. However, extending this paradigm to omnimodal understanding remains challenging, as the heterogeneous and interdependent nature of multimodal perception and reasoning creates a complex evolution space, making targeted harness optimization difficult. To address these challenges, we propose OmniHarness-RSI, a modality-aware framework for evolving agent harnesses. Specifically, we factorize the evolution space into three capability dimensions: modality routing and collaboration, modality-aware evidence localization, and modality-specific perception. Based on this factorization, we construct a Factorized-Harness Agent integrating specialized perception experts and audio-video retrieval tools. We further introduce modality-guided harness evolution, which identifies deficiencies through structured modality-capability attribution and refines corresponding harness strategies through interactive code revisions and execution feedback. Validation-guided selection and held-out evaluation assess generalization within and across datasets. Experiments on WorldSense, Daily-Omni, and OmniVideoBench demonstrate test accuracy improvements of 8.86, 8.13, and 6.23 percentage points, respectively, consistently outperforming modality-agnostic RSI. The evolved harnesses also demonstrate cross-dataset transfer without adaptation, while reducing planner calls and viewed frames.

</details>

<details>
<summary><b>中文摘要</b></summary>

However, extending this paradigm to omnimodal understanding remains challenging, as the heterogeneous and interdependent nature of multimodal perception and reasoning creates a complex evolution space, making targeted harness optimization difficult. To address these challenges, we propose OmniHarness-RSI, a modality-aware framework for evolving agent harnesses. The evolved harnesses also demonstrate cross-dataset transfer without adaptation, while reducing planner calls and viewed frames.

</details>

## 8. Efficient Video Retrieval Augmented Generation via Token Allocation

**中文标题**: Efficient Video Retrieval Augmented Generation via Token Allocation

**作者**: Ayush Barik, Fan Lai, Francis Y. Yan

**发布日期**: 2026-11-24

**arXiv ID**: [2611.32938v1](http://arxiv.org/abs/2611.32938v1)

**PDF**: [下载链接](http://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.32938v1)

**类别**: cs.CV, cs.AI, cs.LG, eess.IV

<details>
<summary><b>摘要 (Abstract)</b></summary>

Efficient long-video understanding requires identifying relevant evidence while minimizing the tokens processed by vision-language models (VLMs). Existing video retrieval-augmented generation (RAG) methods rely on frame selection and token compression, but overlook how much each additional token is worth. We introduce Lembas, a plug-and-play framework that formulates video RAG as a token allocation problem over frames and subtitles under a shared budget. Our key insight is that the value of a token diminishes within each item and decreases further as similar evidence receives more tokens. Lembas captures both effects in a single objective and solves it with an efficient iterative water-filling algorithm that has a provable convergence guarantee. For multi-turn conversations, Lembas reuses the tokens processed in earlier turns and refines relevance with the VLM's attention at no extra cost. Our evaluations show that Lembas improves accuracy by up to 7.2 percentage points over state-of-the-art baselines at comparable compute. On multi-turn video understanding, Lembas achieves up to 1.5 percentage points higher accuracy with 1.7-17.3x fewer TFLOPs.

</details>

<details>
<summary><b>中文摘要</b></summary>

</details>

## 9. HARI4D: Hand-Articulation Aware 4D Reconstruction of Human-Object Interaction

**中文标题**: HARI4D: Hand-Articulation Aware 4D Reconstruction of Human-Object Interaction

**作者**: Semin Kim, Xianghui Xie, Chuqiao Li, Jongyoo Kim, Gerard Pons-Moll

**发布日期**: 2026-11-24

**arXiv ID**: [2611.32932v1](http://arxiv.org/abs/2611.32932v1)

**PDF**: [下载链接](http://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.32932v1)

**类别**: cs.CV

<details>
<summary><b>摘要 (Abstract)</b></summary>

Reasoning about human-object interaction from RGB cameras is important for gaming, VR environments, and robot learning. Yet 4D reconstruction from a monocular RGB camera is challenging, especially when articulated hands are involved. Prior full-body works neglect hands during reconstruction, causing them to penetrate into, or float from the object. We present HARI4D, a hand-articulation-aware method for reconstructing spatially and temporally consistent 4D human-object interaction from monocular RGB video. We introduce a history-aware object tracker that provides robust and stable tracking. Based on these tracks, we improve the interaction configuration through coarse-to-fine reasoning that follows the order of object manipulation: ReachNet first brings the full body and arms to the object during contact, and TouchNet then finalizes hand articulation according to temporal context and local object geometry. On ARCTIC, unseen in training, our method reduces the object error by 56% and improves hand contact F1 from 0.866 to 0.921 over prior art. Our method recovers accurate full body-object interaction with coherent hand articulations and applies to online videos. Our code and pretrained models will be released.

</details>

<details>
<summary><b>中文摘要</b></summary>

Reasoning about human-object interaction from RGB cameras is important for gaming, VR environments, and robot learning. Yet 4D reconstruction from a monocular RGB camera is challenging, especially when articulated hands are involved. Prior full-body works neglect hands during reconstruction, causing them to penetrate into, or float from the object. We present HARI4D, a hand-articulation-aware method for reconstructing spatially and temporally consistent 4D human-object interaction from monocular RGB video. We introduce a history-aware object tracker that provides robust and stable tracking. Based on these tracks, we improve the interaction configuration through coarse-to-fine reasoning that follows the order of object manipulation: ReachNet first brings the full body and arms to the object during contact, and TouchNet then finalizes hand articulation according to temporal context and local object geometry. On ARCTIC, unseen in training, our method reduces the object error by 56% and improves hand contact F1 from 0.866 to 0.921 over prior art. Our method recovers accurate full body-object interaction with coherent hand articulations and applies to online videos. Our code and pretrained models will be released.

</details>

## 10. Biomechanically Grounded Prediction of Selective Motor Control and Clinical Outcomes from Infant Video

**中文标题**: Biomechanically Grounded Prediction of Selective Motor Control and Clinical Outcomes from Infant Video

**作者**: Divya Joshi, Ruize Yang, Colleen Peyton, R. James Cotton

**发布日期**: 2026-11-24

**arXiv ID**: [2611.32890v1](http://arxiv.org/abs/2611.32890v1)

**PDF**: [下载链接](http://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.32890v1)

**类别**: cs.CV

<details>
<summary><b>摘要 (Abstract)</b></summary>

Early identification of cerebral palsy (CP) relies on observational assessments of spontaneous infant movement, such as the General Movement Assessment (GMA) and the Baby Observational Selective Control AppRaisal (BabyOSCAR), which require trained raters and substantial scoring time. Existing automated approaches derive hand-engineered features from two-dimensional keypoints, which describe image-plane motion rather than the three-dimensional behavior of individual joints. We present Baby Biomechanical 3D Body (BB3D), an infant-adapted, biomechanical pipeline that estimates joint-angle trajectories from single-camera video. Against a multi-view markerless motion capture reference in 17 infants, BB3D's median Procrustes-aligned error was 17.5~mm for joint centers and 20.4~mm for marker sites. A spatio-temporal transformer, pretrained on BB3D joint-angle trajectories from 1k+ single-camera recordings, and fine-tuned on 550 videos from 350 infants, predicted each individual BabyOSCAR item with a mean within-site AUC of 0.79 and the BabyOSCAR total with a Spearman correlation of 0.86 on held-out recordings. Without being trained on diagnosis, the predicted total separated infants with CP from those without CP with an AUC of 0.86, exceeding a replicated two-dimensional feature classifier (0.67) but below the expert clinician rater's total (0.97). Predicted scores also carried information about functional ability and body distribution, though less than expert clinician scores. These results show that biomechanically grounded representations of monocular video can support scalable, joint-level assessment of selective motor control in infancy

</details>

<details>
<summary><b>中文摘要</b></summary>

Early identification of cerebral palsy (CP) relies on observational assessments of spontaneous infant movement, such as the General Movement Assessment (GMA) and the Baby Observational Selective Control AppRaisal (BabyOSCAR), which require trained raters and substantial scoring time. Existing automated approaches derive hand-engineered features from two-dimensional keypoints, which describe image-plane motion rather than the three-dimensional behavior of individual joints. We present Baby Biomechanical 3D Body (BB3D), an infant-adapted, biomechanical pipeline that estimates joint-angle trajectories from single-camera video. Against a multi-view markerless motion capture reference in 17 infants, BB3D's median Procrustes-aligned error was 17.5~mm for joint centers and 20.4~mm for marker sites. A spatio-temporal transformer, pretrained on BB3D joint-angle trajectories from 1k+ single-camera recordings, and fine-tuned on 550 videos from 350 infants, predicted each individual BabyOSCAR item with a mean within-site AUC of 0.79 and the BabyOSCAR total with a Spearman correlation of 0.86 on held-out recordings. Without being trained on diagnosis, the predicted total separated infants with CP from those without CP with an AUC of 0.86, exceeding a replicated two-dimensional feature classifier (0.67) but below the expert clinician rater's total (0.97). Predicted scores also carried information about functional ability and body distribution, though less than expert clinician scores. These results show that biomechanically grounded representations of monocular video can support scalable, joint-level assessment of selective motor control in infancy

</details>

## 11. 3D Tracking Any Point with Events and Frames

**中文标题**: 3D Tracking Any Point with Events and Frames

**作者**: Roberto Pellerito, Zijia Dai, Laurent Kneip, Davide Scaramuzza

**发布日期**: 2026-11-24

**arXiv ID**: [2611.32883v1](http://arxiv.org/abs/2611.32883v1)

**PDF**: [下载链接](http://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.32883v1)

**类别**: cs.CV

<details>
<summary><b>摘要 (Abstract)</b></summary>

Tracking a fast-moving point in 3D is prohibitive when RGB frames are blurred or too far apart to reveal its motion. Event cameras record brightness changes between frames, providing measurements inside these temporal gaps. We introduce TrackAnyMotion, which combines monocular RGB frames and events to track arbitrary points in 2D and 3D, including at times when no RGB frame is available. The motion expected for a static point, given scene geometry and camera motion, provides a reference for identifying independently moving objects. An independent-motion decoder combines geometric motion cues with event guidance and 2D tracks, feeding its predictions back into iterative 2D and 3D refinement. We also introduce HyperEvent, a synthetic RGB--Event dataset with high-frequency 3D trajectories, geometry, and independent-motion labels. At RGB frame timestamps on this benchmark, TrackAnyMotion reduces 3D and 2D endpoint errors by 70\% and 32\%, respectively.

</details>

<details>
<summary><b>中文摘要</b></summary>

Tracking a fast-moving point in 3D is prohibitive when RGB frames are blurred or too far apart to reveal its motion. Event cameras record brightness changes between frames, providing measurements inside these temporal gaps. We introduce TrackAnyMotion, which combines monocular RGB frames and events to track arbitrary points in 2D and 3D, including at times when no RGB frame is available. The motion expected for a static point, given scene geometry and camera motion, provides a reference for identifying independently moving objects. An independent-motion decoder combines geometric motion cues with event guidance and 2D tracks, feeding its predictions back into iterative 2D and 3D refinement. We also introduce HyperEvent, a synthetic RGB--Event dataset with high-frequency 3D trajectories, geometry, and independent-motion labels. At RGB frame timestamps on this benchmark, TrackAnyMotion reduces 3D and 2D endpoint errors by 70\% and 32\%, respectively.

</details>

## 12. MemHand: Memory-Enhanced World-Space Hand Motion Reconstruction from Egocentric Videos

**中文标题**: MemHand: Memory-Enhanced World-Space Hand Motion Reconstruction from Egocentric Videos

**作者**: Junyi Ma, Zhangshuo Qi, Erhang Zhang, Mingjie Li, Hesheng Wang

**发布日期**: 2026-11-24

**arXiv ID**: [2611.32876v1](http://arxiv.org/abs/2611.32876v1)

**PDF**: [下载链接](http://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.32876v1)

**项目主页**: https://irmvlab.github.io/memhand.github.io

**类别**: cs.CV

<details>
<summary><b>摘要 (Abstract)</b></summary>

Recovering the continuous world-space motion of both hands from egocentric video is essential for applications such as human-to-robot policy transfer and augmented reality. Achieving this goes beyond reconstructing the hands from each individual frame, as the context that a frame needs is often spread over time, especially around occlusions or when a hand moves out of sight. Recent works have explored how to exploit temporal context, yet they mostly draw it from fixed-length windows. Such windows cannot guarantee that every frame has the context required to reconstruct the hands in world space with accurate poses and metric scale. To address this limitation, we introduce MemHand, a memory-enhanced framework for world-space hand motion reconstruction. MemHand maintains two complementary forms of memory. The Short-Term Event Memory keeps the context of the well-observed hand around each occlusion or out-of-sight event and supplies it to frames with weak or missing visual evidence. In addition, since the metric scale holds over the whole recording, the Long-Term Scale Memory retains the most reliable observations across the video as scale anchors within a bounded state. Across benchmarks featuring severe occlusions and frequent out-of-sight intervals, MemHand achieves state-of-the-art accuracy and generalizes well to unseen domains. Benefiting from the synergy of both memories, it cuts W-MPJPE by 57\% on ARCTIC and 70\% on HOT3D relative to StableHand, delivering continuous and metrically accurate world-space hand poses and trajectories. We will release our code and models at the project page: https://irmvlab.github.io/memhand.github.io

</details>

<details>
<summary><b>中文摘要</b></summary>

Recovering the continuous world-space motion of both hands from egocentric video is essential for applications such as human-to-robot policy transfer and augmented reality. Achieving this goes beyond reconstructing the hands from each individual frame, as the context that a frame needs is often spread over time, especially around occlusions or when a hand moves out of sight. Recent works have explored how to exploit temporal context, yet they mostly draw it from fixed-length windows. Such windows cannot guarantee that every frame has the context required to reconstruct the hands in world space with accurate poses and metric scale. To address this limitation, we introduce MemHand, a memory-enhanced framework for world-space hand motion reconstruction. MemHand maintains two complementary forms of memory. The Short-Term Event Memory keeps the context of the well-observed hand around each occlusion or out-of-sight event and supplies it to frames with weak or missing visual evidence. In addition, since the metric scale holds over the whole recording, the Long-Term Scale Memory retains the most reliable observations across the video as scale anchors within a bounded state. Across benchmarks featuring severe occlusions and frequent out-of-sight intervals, MemHand achieves state-of-the-art accuracy and generalizes well to unseen domains. Benefiting from the synergy of both memories, it cuts W-MPJPE by 57\% on ARCTIC and 70\% on HOT3D relative to StableHand, delivering continuous and metrically accurate world-space hand poses and trajectories. We will release our code and models at the project page: https://irmvlab.github.io/memhand.github.io

</details>

## 13. CityRecast: Suppressing Persistent Artifacts in Generative Satellite 3D Reconstruction

**中文标题**: CityRecast: Suppressing Persistent Artifacts in Generative Satellite 3D Reconstruction

**作者**: Shuo Cui, Yingyan Hou, Wanxuan Lu, Hongfeng Yu, Daobing Zhang et al.

**发布日期**: 2026-11-24

**arXiv ID**: [2611.32863v1](http://arxiv.org/abs/2611.32863v1)

**PDF**: [下载链接](http://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.32863v1)

**类别**: cs.CV

<details>
<summary><b>摘要 (Abstract)</b></summary>

Satellite-based 3D reconstruction is limited by sparse observations and changes across acquisition dates. Existing methods use generative priors to recover missing visual detail, but ghosting, streaking, and blur persist in their Gaussian scenes. These artifacts become particularly apparent in low-elevation renderings. We introduce CityRecast to suppress these artifacts through Gaussian scene repair and supervision calibration. To correct the Gaussian distributions that produce these artifacts, we first use multi-angle warm-up to constrain their spatial extent. This reduces the initial ghosting carried into progressive refinement. Surface-guided replacement then continues to repair the Gaussian distribution while preserving existing appearance. To provide more reliable supervision for this process, observation calibration mitigates interference from multi-temporal observations. Joint refinement further reduces conflicts among generated views. Experiments on DFC2019 and IARPA demonstrate reduced artifacts in low-elevation renderings while maintaining satellite-view fidelity. Compared with Skyfall-GS, CityRecast reduces FID-CLIP by 25.3% on DFC2019 and 51.8% on IARPA. MUSIQ increases by 14.54 and 24.95 points, respectively.

</details>

<details>
<summary><b>中文摘要</b></summary>

Satellite-based 3D reconstruction is limited by sparse observations and changes across acquisition dates. Existing methods use generative priors to recover missing visual detail, but ghosting, streaking, and blur persist in their Gaussian scenes. These artifacts become particularly apparent in low-elevation renderings. We introduce CityRecast to suppress these artifacts through Gaussian scene repair and supervision calibration. To correct the Gaussian distributions that produce these artifacts, we first use multi-angle warm-up to constrain their spatial extent. This reduces the initial ghosting carried into progressive refinement. Surface-guided replacement then continues to repair the Gaussian distribution while preserving existing appearance. To provide more reliable supervision for this process, observation calibration mitigates interference from multi-temporal observations. Joint refinement further reduces conflicts among generated views. Experiments on DFC2019 and IARPA demonstrate reduced artifacts in low-elevation renderings while maintaining satellite-view fidelity. Compared with Skyfall-GS, CityRecast reduces FID-CLIP by 25.3% on DFC2019 and 51.8% on IARPA. MUSIQ increases by 14.54 and 24.95 points, respectively.

</details>

## 14. DOPER: Deep Object Pose Estimation Revisited

**中文标题**: DOPER: Deep Object Pose Estimation Revisited

**作者**: Hugo Hadfield, Alperen Degirmenci, Jonathan Tremblay

**发布日期**: 2026-11-24

**arXiv ID**: [2611.32846v1](http://arxiv.org/abs/2611.32846v1)

**PDF**: [下载链接](http://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.32846v1)

**类别**: cs.CV, cs.RO

<details>
<summary><b>摘要 (Abstract)</b></summary>

We present DOPER, an RGB pose estimation framework that shares a visual backbone across object detection, segmentation, and keypoint prediction to recover the 6D pose of known objects through Perspective-n-Point. Starting from synthetic training, DOPER adapts to real observations using partial supervision while retaining synthetic keypoint labels. Calibrated multi-view observations provide geometric constraints for keypoint supervision. During inference, optional heatmap refinement fits a rigid pose directly to the predicted keypoint distributions, resolving ambiguous correspondences without depth input or a learned pose refiner. Starting from underspecified CAD models, DOPER progressively refines the object representation and pose estimator. Results on SPEED+ and SHIRT further demonstrate effective adaptation to illumination changes and accurate temporal pose estimation. Across the evaluated single-view and multi-view configurations, DOPER achieves state-of-the-art detection AP results on HANDAL, HOPEv2, HOT3D, and T-LESS while using only RGB and running in real time (35 Hz per object model on one GB300). These results demonstrate the effectiveness of jointly learning detection and pose keypoints, with real observations and geometric constraints.

</details>

<details>
<summary><b>中文摘要</b></summary>

We present DOPER, an RGB pose estimation framework that shares a visual backbone across object detection, segmentation, and keypoint prediction to recover the 6D pose of known objects through Perspective-n-Point. Starting from synthetic training, DOPER adapts to real observations using partial supervision while retaining synthetic keypoint labels. Calibrated multi-view observations provide geometric constraints for keypoint supervision. During inference, optional heatmap refinement fits a rigid pose directly to the predicted keypoint distributions, resolving ambiguous correspondences without depth input or a learned pose refiner. Starting from underspecified CAD models, DOPER progressively refines the object representation and pose estimator. Results on SPEED+ and SHIRT further demonstrate effective adaptation to illumination changes and accurate temporal pose estimation. Across the evaluated single-view and multi-view configurations, DOPER achieves state-of-the-art detection AP results on HANDAL, HOPEv2, HOT3D, and T-LESS while using only RGB and running in real time (35 Hz per object model on one GB300). These results demonstrate the effectiveness of jointly learning detection and pose keypoints, with real observations and geometric constraints.

</details>

## 15. PhysMend: Verification-Guided Program Refinement for Physics-Aware Video Generation

**中文标题**: PhysMend: Verification-Guided Program Refinement for Physics-Aware Video Generation

**作者**: Meilong Xu, Ziyi Zhao, Bhagyashree Puranik, Shayda Moezzi, Yixiao Zhang et al.

**发布日期**: 2026-11-24

**arXiv ID**: [2611.32821v1](http://arxiv.org/abs/2611.32821v1)

**PDF**: [下载链接](http://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.32821v1)

**类别**: cs.CV

<details>
<summary><b>摘要 (Abstract)</b></summary>

Video generative models synthesize realistic scenes, yet their interactions often violate physics. A ball dropped onto sand should displace grains and settle into a crater, whereas a generated video may show it passing through the surface or leaving the sand flat. Post-hoc methods typically score generated videos with a vision-language model (VLM), but such scores offer limited guidance on which object or parameter to modify, and each correction costs another synthesis. Simulation-based pipelines instead run fixed stages before synthesis, so simulation errors reach the video. To address these limitations, we present PhysMend, a training-free framework for verification-guided program refinement. PhysMend describes the target event as an editable physical program of grounded objects, events, and required relations, paired with tunable execution parameters. Our key insight is to verify and repair the event on its simulated rollout and on the depth video that conditions the generator (depth control) rather than on generated videos, enabling quantitative violation measurement, targeted editing, and fast re-verification before synthesis. A hybrid verifier evaluates semantic event consistency, physical plausibility, solver stability, and visibility in the depth control. VLM judgments are confined to event semantics and cannot override failed physical measurements, which limits the impact of VLM hallucinations. Each failed check localizes the program fields eligible for repair, and its measured violation scales a bounded update of their parameters. Only accepted depth control reaches the generator, which runs once per input. Experiments on the commonly used benchmarks Physics-IQ, PhyGenBench, and VBench-2.0 demonstrate substantial gains across different generator backbones; for example, PhysMend raises the overall Physics-IQ score of Wan-VACE from 27.33 to 39.40.

</details>

<details>
<summary><b>中文摘要</b></summary>

Video generative models synthesize realistic scenes, yet their interactions often violate physics. A ball dropped onto sand should displace grains and settle into a crater, whereas a generated video may show it passing through the surface or leaving the sand flat. Post-hoc methods typically score generated videos with a vision-language model (VLM), but such scores offer limited guidance on which object or parameter to modify, and each correction costs another synthesis. Simulation-based pipelines instead run fixed stages before synthesis, so simulation errors reach the video. To address these limitations, we present PhysMend, a training-free framework for verification-guided program refinement. Each failed check localizes the program fields eligible for repair, and its measured violation scales a bounded update of their parameters. </details>

## 16. Residual Guidance for Real-Time Interactive Video Generation

**中文标题**: Residual Guidance for Real-Time Interactive Video Generation

**作者**: NaHyeon Park, Kyeong-Hwan Moon, Youjin Jeon, Hyunjung Shim

**发布日期**: 2026-11-24

**arXiv ID**: [2611.32820v1](http://arxiv.org/abs/2611.32820v1)

**PDF**: [下载链接](http://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.32820v1)

**类别**: cs.MM, cs.AI, cs.CV

<details>
<summary><b>摘要 (Abstract)</b></summary>

Real-time interactive video generation allows users to change the prompt during generation to steer the evolving video. However, existing streaming models, which generate videos chunk by chunk, often keep following the previous prompt after a switch. We trace this failure to context inertia, where the previous chunk acts as a strong prior that suppresses the new prompt. Furthermore, we find that this inertia persists even when the cache retains only the previous chunk, yet discarding the context breaks visual continuity, which limits approaches that edit the cache. Rather than editing the cache, we propose Residual Guidance (RG), a simple, training-free method that counteracts context inertia with a correction measured outside the cache. Specifically, RG denoises the re-noised previous chunk once under the new prompt without the cache and adds the resulting residual to the early denoising steps of the next chunk, where its content is largely decided and the new prompt is most suppressed. Across settings ranging from a single switch to long multi-switch generation, RG substantially improves prompt switching capability on existing streaming models, including those with dedicated switching mechanisms, while largely retaining visual quality. As it requires no training or per-model tuning and adds only one forward pass per chunk, RG can be easily integrated into existing real-time video generation pipelines.

</details>

<details>
<summary><b>中文摘要</b></summary>

Real-time interactive video generation allows users to change the prompt during generation to steer the evolving video. However, existing streaming models, which generate videos chunk by chunk, often keep following the previous prompt after a switch. We trace this failure to context inertia, where the previous chunk acts as a strong prior that suppresses the new prompt. Furthermore, we find that this inertia persists even when the cache retains only the previous chunk, yet discarding the context breaks visual continuity, which limits approaches that edit the cache. Rather than editing the cache, we propose Residual Guidance (RG), a simple, training-free method that counteracts context inertia with a correction measured outside the cache. Specifically, RG denoises the re-noised previous chunk once under the new prompt without the cache and adds the resulting residual to the early denoising steps of the next chunk, where its content is largely decided and the new prompt is most suppressed. Across settings ranging from a single switch to long multi-switch generation, RG substantially improves prompt switching capability on existing streaming models, including those with dedicated switching mechanisms, while largely retaining visual quality. As it requires no training or per-model tuning and adds only one forward pass per chunk, RG can be easily integrated into existing real-time video generation pipelines.

</details>

## 17. Back to Raw Motion: Latent-Free Diffusion Transformers for Text-to-Motion and Exact Conditional Generation

**中文标题**: Back to Raw Motion: Latent-Free Diffusion Transformers for Text-to-Motion and Exact Conditional Generation

**作者**: Yun Chen, Jeonghyeok Do, Munchurl Kim

**发布日期**: 2026-11-24

**arXiv ID**: [2611.32793v1](http://arxiv.org/abs/2611.32793v1)

**PDF**: [下载链接](http://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.32793v1)

**类别**: cs.CV

<details>
<summary><b>摘要 (Abstract)</b></summary>

As generative models tackle larger and more complex datasets, learned codecs enable efficient generation through compact latent representations, but may lose details and cannot guarantee exact preservation of given conditions. Generating directly in the original data space avoids errors introduced by the codec. Recent advances in image generation demonstrate that pixel diffusion can produce high-quality samples at practical computational costs. Inspired by these advances, we conduct a preliminary study of pixel diffusion, demonstrating its potential for adaptation to motion generation. Building on these findings, we present RawMo, a framework that brings text-to-motion generation to raw motion space. Our Motion Diffusion Transformer (MiT) models local skeletal structures and whole-body dynamics, while refining motion across frames to improve temporal consistency. Working directly in raw motion space further allows our high-noise text-motion alignment loss (HiNCE) to improve text consistency without intermediate motion decoding. RawMo supports multiple motion-conditioning tasks with a single conditional model while preserving given motion conditions exactly. Experiments on HumanML3D and KIT-ML show the best text alignment among compared methods and competitive motion quality, with ablations confirming the benefits of MiT and HiNCE.

</details>

<details>
<summary><b>中文摘要</b></summary>

As generative models tackle larger and more complex datasets, learned codecs enable efficient generation through compact latent representations, but may lose details and cannot guarantee exact preservation of given conditions. Generating directly in the original data space avoids errors introduced by the codec. Recent advances in image generation demonstrate that pixel diffusion can produce high-quality samples at practical computational costs. Inspired by these advances, we conduct a preliminary study of pixel diffusion, demonstrating its potential for adaptation to motion generation. Building on these findings, we present RawMo, a framework that brings text-to-motion generation to raw motion space. Our Motion Diffusion Transformer (MiT) models local skeletal structures and whole-body dynamics, while refining motion across frames to improve temporal consistency. Working directly in raw motion space further allows our high-noise text-motion alignment loss (HiNCE) to improve text consistency without intermediate motion decoding. RawMo supports multiple motion-conditioning tasks with a single conditional model while preserving given motion conditions exactly. Experiments on HumanML3D and KIT-ML show the best text alignment among compared methods and competitive motion quality, with ablations confirming the benefits of MiT and HiNCE.

</details>

## 18. Simulator-as-Proxy World Model for Robot Manipulation

**中文标题**: Simulator-as-Proxy World Model for Robot Manipulation

**作者**: Tingting Liao, Mingjia Huo, Hao Li, Zhengzhong Liu, Eric P. Xing et al.

**发布日期**: 2026-11-24

**arXiv ID**: [2611.32786v1](http://arxiv.org/abs/2611.32786v1)

**PDF**: [下载链接](http://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.32786v1)

**类别**: cs.RO, cs.CV, cs.LG

<details>
<summary><b>摘要 (Abstract)</b></summary>

Video world models for robots are increasingly conditioned on a simulated or rendered proxy of the scene, on the assumption that the video will show what the proxy shows. Testing this assumption requires a proxy whose state is explicit and can be perturbed. We present SimPWM (Simulator-as-Proxy World Model): given a calibrated camera, it represents, from the first frame alone, the robot by its 3D model and the objects the gripper may touch as 3D points; a rule-based simulator with no trained parameters moves them under the actions, and a video model renders the result. SimPWM returns the realistic video together with the simulated state. We then freeze the simulated objects while the arm moves as recorded and check whether the video still shows the object lifted. On DROID episodes from unseen setups, the answer differs for the arm and for the objects. With each of three base video models, the arm follows the proxy (robot IoU 0.75-0.78, against at most 0.69 for end-to-end models); rendering the robot alone already gives this. What the simulated objects add is the object state: against a robot-only proxy, they cut missed pick-ups from 54% to 38%, and the video follows them only with some base models: on rigid objects, freezing them lowers the lift rate of MiniMax-H3, fine-tuned on episodes whose simulation agrees with the real video, from 42% to 10%, but that of LTX-2.3, trained on the same episodes, only from 57% to 49%, as if it inferred the grasp from the arm motion; more than half of the remaining lifts of MiniMax-H3 are of cloth and rope, which the simulator carries rigidly. With any base model, the simulated state records what the simulation did; under perturbed actions, a video that disagrees with it reveals that the video ignores the simulated state.

</details>

<details>
<summary><b>中文摘要</b></summary>

Video world models for robots are increasingly conditioned on a simulated or rendered proxy of the scene, on the assumption that the video will show what the proxy shows. Testing this assumption requires a proxy whose state is explicit and can be perturbed. We present SimPWM (Simulator-as-Proxy World Model): given a calibrated camera, it represents, from the first frame alone, the robot by its 3D model and the objects the gripper may touch as 3D points; a rule-based simulator with no trained parameters moves them under the actions, and a video model renders the result. SimPWM returns the realistic video together with the simulated state. We then freeze the simulated objects while the arm moves as recorded and check whether the video still shows the object lifted. On DROID episodes from unseen setups, the answer differs for the arm and for the objects. With each of three base video models, the arm follows the proxy (robot IoU 0.75-0.78, against at most 0.69 for end-to-end models); rendering the robot alone already gives this. What the simulated objects add is the object state: against a robot-only proxy, they cut missed pick-ups from 54% to 38%, and the video follows them only with some base models: on rigid objects, freezing them lowers the lift rate of MiniMax-H3, fine-tuned on episodes whose simulation agrees with the real video, from 42% to 10%, but that of LTX-2.3, trained on the same episodes, only from 57% to 49%, as if it inferred the grasp from the arm motion; more than half of the remaining lifts of MiniMax-H3 are of cloth and rope, which the simulator carries rigidly. With any base model, the simulated state records what the simulation did; under perturbed actions, a video that disagrees with it reveals that the video ignores the simulated state.

</details>

## 19. Beyond Static Semantics: Instance-Adaptive Vision-Language Alignment for Building Damage Assessment

**中文标题**: Beyond Static Semantics: Instance-Adaptive Vision-Language Alignment for Building Damage Assessment

**作者**: Hang Yin, Jiepan Li, Hongruixuan Chen, Guangyi Yang, Wei He

**发布日期**: 2026-11-24

**arXiv ID**: [2611.32777v1](http://arxiv.org/abs/2611.32777v1)

**PDF**: [下载链接](http://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.32777v1)

**代码仓库**: https://github.com/Cutensil/LAMIA-IBDA.

**类别**: cs.CV

<details>
<summary><b>摘要 (Abstract)</b></summary>

Instance-level building damage assessment (IBDA) from bi-temporal remote sensing imagery remains challenging, as the same damage grade may exhibit diverse visual manifestations across individual buildings. Natural language offers explicit descriptions of physical damage, providing complementary semantic cues beyond discrete category labels. However, existing vision--language approaches have not fully addressed how damage semantics should adapt to local bi-temporal evidence and subsequently serve as instance-specific references for severity discrimination. To bridge this gap, we propose LAMIA-IBDA, a unified vision--language framework that progressively grounds shared damage semantics in local observations and individual buildings. Specifically, Adaptive Text-Visual Alignment (ATVA) enables evidence-adaptive semantic interaction by jointly contextualizing multiple damage descriptions and bi-temporal visual features under reliability-aware attention supervision. Dynamic Instance-Prototype Grounding (DIPG) further associates these contextualized semantics with individual building regions to construct instance-adaptive prototypes for fine-grained damage classification. Instance-level description preferences additionally supervise fusion-stage attention, linking semantic adaptation with instance discrimination. Extensive experiments on xBD and BRIGHT demonstrate state-of-the-art performance across optical--optical and optical--SAR settings. The source code is publicly available https://github.com/Cutensil/LAMIA-IBDA.

</details>

<details>
<summary><b>中文摘要</b></summary>

Instance-level building damage assessment (IBDA) from bi-temporal remote sensing imagery remains challenging, as the same damage grade may exhibit diverse visual manifestations across individual buildings. Natural language offers explicit descriptions of physical damage, providing complementary semantic cues beyond discrete category labels. However, existing vision--language approaches have not fully addressed how damage semantics should adapt to local bi-temporal evidence and subsequently serve as instance-specific references for severity discrimination. To bridge this gap, we propose LAMIA-IBDA, a unified vision--language framework that progressively grounds shared damage semantics in local observations and individual buildings. Dynamic Instance-Prototype Grounding (DIPG) further associates these contextualized semantics with individual building regions to construct instance-adaptive prototypes for fine-grained damage classification. Instance-level description preferences additionally supervise fusion-stage attention, linking semantic adaptation with instance discrimination. Extensive experiments on xBD and BRIGHT demonstrate state-of-the-art performance across optical--optical and optical--SAR settings. The source code is publicly available https://github.com/Cutensil/LAMIA-IBDA.

</details>

## 20. MemAnchorWorld: A Sparse World Model with Horizon-Conditioned Memory Retrieval for Autonomous Driving

**中文标题**: MemAnchorWorld: A Sparse World Model with Horizon-Conditioned Memory Retrieval for Autonomous Driving

**作者**: Xinyang Jia, Yuqi Zhang, Jiakai Wang, Yahu Yang, Guandong Pan et al.

**发布日期**: 2026-11-24

**arXiv ID**: [2611.32745v1](http://arxiv.org/abs/2611.32745v1)

**PDF**: [下载链接](http://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.32745v1)

**项目主页**: https://memanchorworld.github.io/

**类别**: cs.CV

<details>
<summary><b>摘要 (Abstract)</b></summary>

Sparse world models for 4D semantic occupancy forecasting represent scenes with a small set of evolving queries to efficiently predict future geometry and semantics for autonomous driving. However, existing sparse world models face a bottleneck in using historical temporal evidence for long-horizon forecasting. They rely on scene states to carry historical information implicitly, allowing errors to propagate and accumulate across successive forecasting steps and causing geometric and semantic deviations. To address this limitation, we present MemAnchorWorld, a sparse occupancy world model that explicitly retrieves relevant historical geometric and semantic evidence for 4D semantic occupancy forecasting. Inspired by the von Restorff effect, we introduce a forecast-time-conditioned hierarchical dual-path memory-anchor retrieval mechanism. Guided by the effective age and semantic compatibility of historical information, it combines query-level candidate selection with point-level memory-anchor retrieval to provide historical evidence for sparse points at different forecast times. To better use memory anchors and existing features, a point-level residual adapter jointly gated by the task and forecast time separately controls residual corrections to semantics, occupancy confidence, and spatial positions. On Occ3D-nuScenes, MemAnchorWorld achieves an average mIoU of 13.55% and an average IoU of 22.84% across the 1-3 s forecasting horizons, attaining state-of-the-art performance on both metrics.Project page: https://memanchorworld.github.io/

</details>

<details>
<summary><b>中文摘要</b></summary>

Sparse world models for 4D semantic occupancy forecasting represent scenes with a small set of evolving queries to efficiently predict future geometry and semantics for autonomous driving. However, existing sparse world models face a bottleneck in using historical temporal evidence for long-horizon forecasting. They rely on scene states to carry historical information implicitly, allowing errors to propagate and accumulate across successive forecasting steps and causing geometric and semantic deviations. To address this limitation, we present MemAnchorWorld, a sparse occupancy world model that explicitly retrieves relevant historical geometric and semantic evidence for 4D semantic occupancy forecasting. Inspired by the von Restorff effect, we introduce a forecast-time-conditioned hierarchical dual-path memory-anchor retrieval mechanism. Guided by the effective age and semantic compatibility of historical information, it combines query-level candidate selection with point-level memory-anchor retrieval to provide historical evidence for sparse points at different forecast times. To better use memory anchors and existing features, a point-level residual adapter jointly gated by the task and forecast time separately controls residual corrections to semantics, occupancy confidence, and spatial positions. On Occ3D-nuScenes, MemAnchorWorld achieves an average mIoU of 13.55% and an average IoU of 22.84% across the 1-3 s forecasting horizons, attaining state-of-the-art performance on both metrics.Project page: https://memanchorworld.github.io/

</details>

## 21. TokenMatte: Unlocking T2V Diffusion Priors for Pixel-Native Video Matting

**中文标题**: TokenMatte: Unlocking T2V Diffusion Priors for Pixel-Native Video Matting

**作者**: Zanyi Wang, Dengyang Jiang, Fengfei Yu, Shilong Liu

**发布日期**: 2026-11-24

**arXiv ID**: [2611.32675v1](http://arxiv.org/abs/2611.32675v1)

**PDF**: [下载链接](http://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.32675v1)

**类别**: cs.CV

<details>
<summary><b>摘要 (Abstract)</b></summary>

High-resolution video matting requires accurate recovery of continuous alpha values, including fine hair, soft boundaries, and motion blur, yet dense alpha supervision remains scarce. Pretrained text-to-video diffusion transformers (DiTs) offer rich spatio-temporal priors, whereas existing generative video matting approaches typically route alpha predictions through RGB-trained VAE latents, which can attenuate fine details and introduce additional decoding overhead. We present TokenMatte, which adapts a pretrained video DiT into a single-pass, auxiliary-free RGB-to-alpha predictor with a pixel-native output interface. At its core is a single shared linear readout that maps each spatio-temporal token directly to its corresponding ($t \times p \times p$) block of alpha pixels, predicting spatial details and temporal variations within the same local video block. Tiling these blocks produces the full-resolution alpha sequence without a convolutional decoder or learned upsampling. The predicted alpha is supervised directly in pixel space, while the ground-truth alpha never enters the VAE, eliminating target-side alpha encoding and output-side VAE decoding. Leveraging the pretrained video prior, TokenMatte is trained using only existing public matting datasets, without additional data curation or auxiliary inputs at inference. Compared with a matched latent-output baseline, our pixel-native interface improves matting accuracy while achieving a 1.51$\times$ inference speedup. TokenMatte establishes a new auxiliary-free state of the art on VideoMatte, YouTubeMatte, and V-HIM60.

</details>

<details>
<summary><b>中文摘要</b></summary>

High-resolution video matting requires accurate recovery of continuous alpha values, including fine hair, soft boundaries, and motion blur, yet dense alpha supervision remains scarce. Pretrained text-to-video diffusion transformers (DiTs) offer rich spatio-temporal priors, whereas existing generative video matting approaches typically route alpha predictions through RGB-trained VAE latents, which can attenuate fine details and introduce additional decoding overhead. We present TokenMatte, which adapts a pretrained video DiT into a single-pass, auxiliary-free RGB-to-alpha predictor with a pixel-native output interface. At its core is a single shared linear readout that maps each spatio-temporal token directly to its corresponding ($t \times p \times p$) block of alpha pixels, predicting spatial details and temporal variations within the same local video block. Tiling these blocks produces the full-resolution alpha sequence without a convolutional decoder or learned upsampling. The predicted alpha is supervised directly in pixel space, while the ground-truth alpha never enters the VAE, eliminating target-side alpha encoding and output-side VAE decoding. Leveraging the pretrained video prior, TokenMatte is trained using only existing public matting datasets, without additional data curation or auxiliary inputs at inference. Compared with a matched latent-output baseline, our pixel-native interface improves matting accuracy while achieving a 1.51$\times$ inference speedup. TokenMatte establishes a new auxiliary-free state of the art on VideoMatte, YouTubeMatte, and V-HIM60.

</details>

## 22. Attack-Inference Aligned Universal Adversarial Attacks on Video Object Segmentation

**中文标题**: Attack-Inference Aligned Universal Adversarial Attacks on Video Object Segmentation

**作者**: Ruiqi Shen, Chang Liu, Henghui Ding

**发布日期**: 2026-11-24

**arXiv ID**: [2611.32658v1](http://arxiv.org/abs/2611.32658v1)

**PDF**: [下载链接](http://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.32658v1)

**代码仓库**: https://github.com/FudanCVL/AUV.

**类别**: cs.CV

<details>
<summary><b>摘要 (Abstract)</b></summary>

Modern video object segmentation (VOS) relies on a two-stage inference paradigm: target initialization and memory-based propagation. However, existing adversarial attacks on VOS often overlook this structure. To bridge this gap, we propose AUV, an adversarial framework that Aligns with the Universal two-stage paradigm of VOS models. Specifically, AUV optimizes a universal adversarial perturbation (UAP) through decoupled training strategies tailored for both initialization and propagation stages. By disrupting the shared memory space, it achieves prompt-agnostic attacks across different modalities, including masks, points, boxes, and language. Extensive experiments show that AUV establishes state-of-the-art VOS attack performance, such as degrading mask-based SAM3 to only 8.4 J&F on DAVIS17-val (compared to 55.4 for the previous SOTA under the same training setup). It also exhibits superior temporal dynamics with the fastest onset, the most enduring persistence, and the support of any-time mid-video attacks. The AUV optimization framework is applicable to different VOS models, revealing vulnerabilities in real-world streaming VOS. Code is available at https://github.com/FudanCVL/AUV.

</details>

<details>
<summary><b>中文摘要</b></summary>

Specifically, AUV optimizes a universal adversarial perturbation (UAP) through decoupled training strategies tailored for both initialization and propagation stages. By disrupting the shared memory space, it achieves prompt-agnostic attacks across different modalities, including masks, points, boxes, and language. Extensive experiments show that AUV establishes state-of-the-art VOS attack performance, such as degrading mask-based SAM3 to only 8.4 J&F on DAVIS17-val (compared to 55.4 for the previous SOTA under the same training setup). It also exhibits superior temporal dynamics with the fastest onset, the most enduring persistence, and the support of any-time mid-video attacks. The AUV optimization framework is applicable to different VOS models, revealing vulnerabilities in real-world streaming VOS. Code is available at https://github.com/FudanCVL/AUV.

## 23. Sol$^{3}$: Accelerating Video Diffusion for Free via Hierarchical Sparse Attention Routing

**中文标题**: Sol$^{3}$: Accelerating Video Diffusion for Free via Hierarchical Sparse Attention Routing

**作者**: Guoxin Fan, Weilun Feng, Chuanguang Yang, Mingqiang Wu, Xiaokun Liu et al.

**发布日期**: 2026-11-24

**arXiv ID**: [2611.32562v1](http://arxiv.org/abs/2611.32562v1)

**PDF**: [下载链接](http://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.32562v1)

**代码仓库**: https://github.com/FofGofx/sol3.

**类别**: cs.CV

<details>
<summary><b>摘要 (Abstract)</b></summary>

Sol-Attn (Sparsifying online attention) combines sparse exact computation with low-cost representative approximation to accelerate training-free inference in diffusion video models. However, intra-block attention can focus on only a few tokens. While preserving these key interactions exactly, the block-level decision incurs the cost of evaluating many low-response members. We propose Sol$^{3}$, a training-free hierarchical sparse attention method that further refines how exact computations are allocated. First, we introduce Gaussian-guided hierarchical routing, which determines the scope of exact computation step by step, from blocks to tokens, using statistical thresholds at each level. Only a subset of members within Mixed blocks that require refinement is selected for exact computation, while representative terms approximate the remaining members. We further design heterogeneous routing statistics that combine block-center correlations with variations in Key and Value within blocks to identify blocks that are difficult to approximate. Among these Mixed blocks, we uniformly compare raw token relevance to select members requiring exact computation. Experiments on video generation, audio-video co-generation, and world simulation tasks demonstrate that Sol$^{3}$ achieves 86% attention sparsity and delivers a $3.07\times$ attention speedup while preserving quality. Our code is available at https://github.com/FofGofx/sol3.

</details>

<details>
<summary><b>中文摘要</b></summary>

Sol-Attn (Sparsifying online attention) combines sparse exact computation with low-cost representative approximation to accelerate training-free inference in diffusion video models. However, intra-block attention can focus on only a few tokens. While preserving these key interactions exactly, the block-level decision incurs the cost of evaluating many low-response members. We propose Sol$^{3}$, a training-free hierarchical sparse attention method that further refines how exact computations are allocated. First, we introduce Gaussian-guided hierarchical routing, which determines the scope of exact computation step by step, from blocks to tokens, using statistical thresholds at each level. Only a subset of members within Mixed blocks that require refinement is selected for exact computation, while representative terms approximate the remaining members. We further design heterogeneous routing statistics that combine block-center correlations with variations in Key and Value within blocks to identify blocks that are difficult to approximate. Among these Mixed blocks, we uniformly compare raw token relevance to select members requiring exact computation. Experiments on video generation, audio-video co-generation, and world simulation tasks demonstrate that Sol$^{3}$ achieves 86% attention sparsity and delivers a $3.07\times$ attention speedup while preserving quality. Our code is available at https://github.com/FofGofx/sol3.

</details>

## 24. MinPair: Measured Minimal Pairs for Fine-Grained Control in Text-to-Motion Generation

**中文标题**: MinPair: Measured Minimal Pairs for Fine-Grained Control in Text-to-Motion Generation

**作者**: Lennart Schiweck, Cristóbal Curio

**发布日期**: 2026-11-24

**arXiv ID**: [2611.32549v1](http://arxiv.org/abs/2611.32549v1)

**PDF**: [下载链接](http://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.32549v1)

**类别**: cs.CV

<details>
<summary><b>摘要 (Abstract)</b></summary>

Text-to-motion generation has made great progress in capturing what action to perform, yet it remains limited in controlling how that action is executed. For example, when only the height word of a kick request changes, none of the tested state-of-the-art methods reaches all three of knee, waist and head height. The other words of a caption usually imply an attribute such as height, so neither the training loss nor the standard metrics require a model to use the word that states it. A model has to use this word only when it alone separates two otherwise identical captions. We introduce MinPair, which creates such caption pairs from real motion clips. It starts from rule-based motion descriptors that measure the 3D body motion of each clip. From these descriptors a language model writes MinPair captions, timed body-part captions that say how each body part moves. The same descriptors show in which attributes two clips of the same action differ. A measured minimal pair joins the true caption of one clip with an altered copy in which only one attribute takes the value measured in the other clip. A contrastive pair loss then requires a diffusion denoiser to reconstruct the clip more accurately from its true caption than from the altered copy. Training needs no synthetic motion and inference stays unchanged. Control is tested on 22 target tasks and on caption edits that each change one attribute. MinPair captions alone help motions reach their targets and improve the standard metrics M2M, R@3 and FID, yet a model trained on them still ignores most single-word edits. The pair loss raises the share of followed template edits from 11% to 60%, while the standard metrics do not improve further. With body-part captions, MinPair reaches more targets than state-of-the-art methods that were trained on up to 99 times more motion data.

</details>

<details>
<summary><b>中文摘要</b></summary>

Text-to-motion generation has made great progress in capturing what action to perform, yet it remains limited in controlling how that action is executed. For example, when only the height word of a kick request changes, none of the tested state-of-the-art methods reaches all three of knee, waist and head height. The other words of a caption usually imply an attribute such as height, so neither the training loss nor the standard metrics require a model to use the word that states it. A model has to use this word only when it alone separates two otherwise identical captions. We introduce MinPair, which creates such caption pairs from real motion clips. It starts from rule-based motion descriptors that measure the 3D body motion of each clip. From these descriptors a language model writes MinPair captions, timed body-part captions that say how each body part moves. The same descriptors show in which attributes two clips of the same action differ. A measured minimal pair joins the true caption of one clip with an altered copy in which only one attribute takes the value measured in the other clip. A contrastive pair loss then requires a diffusion denoiser to reconstruct the clip more accurately from its true caption than from the altered copy. Training needs no synthetic motion and inference stays unchanged. Control is tested on 22 target tasks and on caption edits that each change one attribute. MinPair captions alone help motions reach their targets and improve the standard metrics M2M, R@3 and FID, yet a model trained on them still ignores most single-word edits. The pair loss raises the share of followed template edits from 11% to 60%, while the standard metrics do not improve further. With body-part captions, MinPair reaches more targets than state-of-the-art methods that were trained on up to 99 times more motion data.

</details>

## 25. GeoVLDrive: Geometry-Grounded Vision-Language Reasoning for Autonomous Driving

**中文标题**: GeoVLDrive: Geometry-Grounded Vision-Language Reasoning for Autonomous Driving

**作者**: JiaHui Huang, Songyan Zhang, Hanbing Li, Hanchao Leng, Kuiyuan Yang et al.

**发布日期**: 2026-11-24

**arXiv ID**: [2611.32510v1](http://arxiv.org/abs/2611.32510v1)

**PDF**: [下载链接](http://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.32510v1)

**类别**: cs.CV

<details>
<summary><b>摘要 (Abstract)</b></summary>

Vision-language-action (VLA) planners inherit semantic knowledge from pretrained vision-language models, but their reasoning is rarely grounded in 3D scene structure and its evolution over time. We introduce GeoVLDrive, a geometry-grounded VLA framework for 3D spatiotemporal reasoning and planning. Multi-layer features from a 3D foundation model augment the vision-language backbone through gated cross-attention. A structured latent chain of thought (CoT) organizes future scene geometry, key-agent motion, and meta-actions into ordered, separately supervised latent states. An action expert generates trajectory proposals from these states and multi-layer scene features, and ranks them using scene-grounded, interacting score tokens. We also introduce NAVSIM 3DQA to train and evaluate metric 3D spatial and motion understanding. GeoVLDrive achieves 91.8 PDMS on NAVSIM v1 and 90.9 EPDMS on NAVSIM v2. On NAVSIM 3DQA, the geometry-grounded backbone scores 89.2% overall, 4.6 percentage points above the fine-tuned Qwen3-VL-2B baseline. On LingoQA, the backbone achieves 68.6% Lingo-Judge accuracy.

</details>

<details>
<summary><b>中文摘要</b></summary>

Vision-language-action (VLA) planners inherit semantic knowledge from pretrained vision-language models, but their reasoning is rarely grounded in 3D scene structure and its evolution over time. We introduce GeoVLDrive, a geometry-grounded VLA framework for 3D spatiotemporal reasoning and planning. Multi-layer features from a 3D foundation model augment the vision-language backbone through gated cross-attention. A structured latent chain of thought (CoT) organizes future scene geometry, key-agent motion, and meta-actions into ordered, separately supervised latent states. An action expert generates trajectory proposals from these states and multi-layer scene features, and ranks them using scene-grounded, interacting score tokens. We also introduce NAVSIM 3DQA to train and evaluate metric 3D spatial and motion understanding. GeoVLDrive achieves 91.8 PDMS on NAVSIM v1 and 90.9 EPDMS on NAVSIM v2. On NAVSIM 3DQA, the geometry-grounded backbone scores 89.2% overall, 4.6 percentage points above the fine-tuned Qwen3-VL-2B baseline. On LingoQA, the backbone achieves 68.6% Lingo-Judge accuracy.

</details>
