## 26. An Integrated Remote Sensing and Machine Learning Framework for Mapping Hazard Associated with Leached Metals

**中文标题**: An Integrated Remote Sensing and Machine Learning Framework for Mapping Hazard Associated with Leached Metals

**作者**: Salman Karim, Bahareh Nojabaei, Hosein Foroutan

**发布日期**: 2026-11-24

**arXiv ID**: [2611.32505v1](http://arxiv.org/abs/2611.32505v1)

**PDF**: [下载链接](http://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.32505v1)

**类别**: cs.CV

<details>
<summary><b>摘要 (Abstract)</b></summary>

This study integrates satellite remote sensing, groundwater monitoring, and machine learning to map leached-metal-associated hazards at four coal combustion residual (CCR) sites in Virginia, USA. Constituent-specific groundwater concentrations were expressed as Hazard Quotients (HQ) and combined into a composite Hazard Index (HI). Predictors included Sentinel-2 vegetation indices, biophysical parameters, and spatiotemporal metrics (2019-2025), alongside soil, climate, hydrology, topography, and land cover. Six supervised classifiers were evaluated using spatially separated test blocks, feature-set ablation, and leave-one-site-out testing. Higher HQ coincided with lower vegetation indices in 24 of 33 metal-site combinations near monitoring wells (sign test p = 0.007). Well HI was negatively correlated with CIRE and canopy chlorophyll content at three sites (Spearman rho as low as -0.71). Random Forest achieved within-site HI classification accuracies of 0.768-0.924 and higher-HI-class recall of 0.965-0.999. For 34 constituent-site combinations, HQ classification accuracies ranged from 0.775 to 0.989. Environmental covariates generally contributed more predictive information than vegetation features. Removing vegetation predictors did not reduce performance, and distance to the CCR unit alone performed best at one site. Zero-shot cross-site accuracy declined to 0.734-0.749, no better than the majority-class baseline. These findings demonstrate the potential of integrating satellite observations and environmental data with groundwater measurements for site-calibrated spatial hazard screening. The framework identifies indirect environmental associations rather than directly detecting dissolved metals, highlighting the importance of local calibration and independent validation.

</details>

<details>
<summary><b>中文摘要</b></summary>

This study integrates satellite remote sensing, groundwater monitoring, and machine learning to map leached-metal-associated hazards at four coal combustion residual (CCR) sites in Virginia, USA. Constituent-specific groundwater concentrations were expressed as Hazard Quotients (HQ) and combined into a composite Hazard Index (HI). Predictors included Sentinel-2 vegetation indices, biophysical parameters, and spatiotemporal metrics (2019-2025), alongside soil, climate, hydrology, topography, and land cover. Six supervised classifiers were evaluated using spatially separated test blocks, feature-set ablation, and leave-one-site-out testing. Higher HQ coincided with lower vegetation indices in 24 of 33 metal-site combinations near monitoring wells (sign test p = 0.007). Well HI was negatively correlated with CIRE and canopy chlorophyll content at three sites (Spearman rho as low as -0.71). Random Forest achieved within-site HI classification accuracies of 0.768-0.924 and higher-HI-class recall of 0.965-0.999. For 34 constituent-site combinations, HQ classification accuracies ranged from 0.775 to 0.989. Environmental covariates generally contributed more predictive information than vegetation features. Removing vegetation predictors did not reduce performance, and distance to the CCR unit alone performed best at one site. Zero-shot cross-site accuracy declined to 0.734-0.749, no better than the majority-class baseline. These findings demonstrate the potential of integrating satellite observations and environmental data with groundwater measurements for site-calibrated spatial hazard screening. The framework identifies indirect environmental associations rather than directly detecting dissolved metals, highlighting the importance of local calibration and independent validation.

</details>

## 27. COHERE4D: Training-Free Dense Surface Alignment for Coherent Metric 4D Human-Scene Reconstruction

**中文标题**: COHERE4D: Training-Free Dense Surface Alignment for Coherent Metric 4D Human-Scene Reconstruction

**作者**: Zhumei Wang, Shaofei Wang, Xiang An, Mingtao Pei, Siyuan Huang

**发布日期**: 2026-11-24

**arXiv ID**: [2611.32482v1](http://arxiv.org/abs/2611.32482v1)

**PDF**: [下载链接](http://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.32482v1)

**类别**: cs.CV

<details>
<summary><b>摘要 (Abstract)</b></summary>

Human and scene foundation models offer complementary strengths for monocular 4D reconstruction: human models recover metric-scale body meshes but lack scene grounding, whereas scene models estimate globally consistent 3D geometry and camera motion without reliable metric scale. Crucially, both models reconstruct the same foreground human surface. Yet existing methods do not directly exploit this shared surface: lacking reliable foreground human geometry in scene reconstructions, earlier approaches either resort to sparse contact constraints or learn data-driven alignment modules that generalize poorly to out-of-distribution scenes. We present COHERE4D, a training-free framework that directly registers the human surfaces predicted by independently pretrained human and scene foundation models to reconstruct a coherent, metric 4D world. COHERE4D establishes dense, pixel-aligned 3D correspondences over the visible human surface and employs RANSAC with cross-frame consensus to suppress outliers from occlusion, background contamination, and depth noise. This formulation solves for two complementary geometric quantities: a sequence-level shared scale that calibrates scene geometry and camera motion to metric human size, and per-frame human translations that align camera-space human meshes with the scaled scene geometry to enforce spatial and temporal consistency. Across diverse backbones and benchmarks, COHERE4D substantially outperforms prior methods in human--scene alignment, metric scene and camera pose reconstruction, and global human motion estimation, adding only 4,ms/frame, enabling overall inference at up to 14 FPS.

</details>

<details>
<summary><b>中文摘要</b></summary>

Human and scene foundation models offer complementary strengths for monocular 4D reconstruction: human models recover metric-scale body meshes but lack scene grounding, whereas scene models estimate globally consistent 3D geometry and camera motion without reliable metric scale. Crucially, both models reconstruct the same foreground human surface. Yet existing methods do not directly exploit this shared surface: lacking reliable foreground human geometry in scene reconstructions, earlier approaches either resort to sparse contact constraints or learn data-driven alignment modules that generalize poorly to out-of-distribution scenes. We present COHERE4D, a training-free framework that directly registers the human surfaces predicted by independently pretrained human and scene foundation models to reconstruct a coherent, metric 4D world. COHERE4D establishes dense, pixel-aligned 3D correspondences over the visible human surface and employs RANSAC with cross-frame consensus to suppress outliers from occlusion, background contamination, and depth noise. This formulation solves for two complementary geometric quantities: a sequence-level shared scale that calibrates scene geometry and camera motion to metric human size, and per-frame human translations that align camera-space human meshes with the scaled scene geometry to enforce spatial and temporal consistency. Across diverse backbones and benchmarks, COHERE4D substantially outperforms prior methods in human--scene alignment, metric scene and camera pose reconstruction, and global human motion estimation, adding only 4,ms/frame, enabling overall inference at up to 14 FPS.

</details>

---

## 28. OmniPersist: Persistent Dynamic World Modeling via Coupled Panoramic Evolution

**中文标题**: OmniPersist: Persistent Dynamic World Modeling via Coupled Panoramic Evolution

**作者**: Zhilu Zhang, Xiaoyu Liu, Wenxi Liu, Shengfeng He

**发布日期**: 2026-11-24

**arXiv ID**: [2611.32473v1](http://arxiv.org/abs/2611.32473v1)

**PDF**: [下载链接](http://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.32473v1)

**类别**: cs.CV

<details>
<summary><b>摘要 (Abstract)</b></summary>

Action-conditioned world models play a vital role in robotics and autonomous driving, yet existing video diffusion models struggle to maintain dynamic object persistence. When the camera turns away and returns, out-of-view moving entities fail to evolve plausibly in latent space, often mutating or vanishing entirely due to bounded perspective views. To resolve this issue, we propose OmniPersist, a unified framework that couples perspective video generation with panoramic evolution within a single-stage diffusion model, without relying on auxiliary models or initial panoramic inputs. To maintain efficiency, we model the panoramic stream at a lower frame rate, bridging both views across space and time via Geometric Anchoring (GeoAnch) for spatial correspondence and Temporal Compensation (TemComp) for temporal aggregation. OmniPersist is agnostic to camera-control architectures, supporting both latent-level and attention-level conditioning. Extensive experiments on a newly constructed dynamic benchmark across image-to-video and video continuation tasks demonstrate consistent gains in visual quality, camera controllability, revisit consistency, and dynamic persistence.

</details>

<details>
<summary><b>中文摘要</b></summary>

Action-conditioned world models play a vital role in robotics and autonomous driving, yet existing video diffusion models struggle to maintain dynamic object persistence. When the camera turns away and returns, out-of-view moving entities fail to evolve plausibly in latent space, often mutating or vanishing entirely due to bounded perspective views. To resolve this issue, we propose OmniPersist, a unified framework that couples perspective video generation with panoramic evolution within a single-stage diffusion model, without relying on auxiliary models or initial panoramic inputs. To maintain efficiency, we model the panoramic stream at a lower frame rate, bridging both views across space and time via Geometric Anchoring (GeoAnch) for spatial correspondence and Temporal Compensation (TemComp) for temporal aggregation. OmniPersist is agnostic to camera-control architectures, supporting both latent-level and attention-level conditioning. Extensive experiments on a newly constructed dynamic benchmark across image-to-video and video continuation tasks demonstrate consistent gains in visual quality, camera controllability, revisit consistency, and dynamic persistence.

</details>

## 29. A Mesh to Remember: Persistent Appearance for Interactive Video Worlds

**中文标题**: A Mesh to Remember: Persistent Appearance for Interactive Video Worlds

**作者**: Joonghyuk Shin, Alex Trevithick, Katarína Tóthová, Liyuan Zhu, Shengyu Huang et al.

**发布日期**: 2026-11-24

**arXiv ID**: [2611.32445v1](http://arxiv.org/abs/2611.32445v1)

**PDF**: [下载链接](http://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.32445v1)

**类别**: cs.CV

<details>
<summary><b>摘要 (Abstract)</b></summary>

Video world models synthesize photorealistic observations in response to user actions but struggle to remain consistent over long interactions. Coding agents and text-to-3D models instead generate explicit 3D worlds whose geometry and state persist by construction, but whose appearance falls far short of photorealism. Recent methods combine the strengths of both by rendering such worlds into coarse proxies that guide a video model. However, since these proxies only specify where things are, the renderer must invent what they look like as the world is explored, and anything it forgets is reinvented when a surface reappears. Our key insight is that a mesh identifies which surface each pixel shows, across frames and views, even as objects move, allowing it to serve as an address for what the renderer generates. We propose M2R (Mesh to Remember), a few-step causal video renderer that stores its own features on the surfaces they depict, in each object's frame, and retrieves them by rasterizing the mesh whenever those surfaces come back into view. Appearance thus travels with objects wherever they move, persists across revisits, and is shared by every agent that observes it, while memory grows with the surfaces seen instead of the number of frames. Trained only on synthetic data, M2R transfers zero-shot to real indoor scans and to worlds authored by coding agents. It outperforms state-of-the-art methods in consistency across static, dynamic, and multi-agent benchmarks, more than doubling object permanence and nearly doubling cross-agent consistency, while rendering 720p video in real time (40 FPS).

</details>

<details>
<summary><b>中文摘要</b></summary>

Video world models synthesize photorealistic observations in response to user actions but struggle to remain consistent over long interactions. Coding agents and text-to-3D models instead generate explicit 3D worlds whose geometry and state persist by construction, but whose appearance falls far short of photorealism. Recent methods combine the strengths of both by rendering such worlds into coarse proxies that guide a video model. However, since these proxies only specify where things are, the renderer must invent what they look like as the world is explored, and anything it forgets is reinvented when a surface reappears. Our key insight is that a mesh identifies which surface each pixel shows, across frames and views, even as objects move, allowing it to serve as an address for what the renderer generates. We propose M2R (Mesh to Remember), a few-step causal video renderer that stores its own features on the surfaces they depict, in each object's frame, and retrieves them by rasterizing the mesh whenever those surfaces come back into view. Appearance thus travels with objects wherever they move, persists across revisits, and is shared by every agent that observes it, while memory grows with the surfaces seen instead of the number of frames. Trained only on synthetic data, M2R transfers zero-shot to real indoor scans and to worlds authored by coding agents. It outperforms state-of-the-art methods in consistency across static, dynamic, and multi-agent benchmarks, more than doubling object permanence and nearly doubling cross-agent consistency, while rendering 720p video in real time (40 FPS).

</details>

---

## 30. PVWander: 3D Memory-guided Panoramic Video Wandering with Path-Facing Canonicalization

**中文标题**: PVWander: 3D Memory-guided Panoramic Video Wandering with Path-Facing Canonicalization

**作者**: Chuanxin Song, Xiaodong Wang, Shijie Chen, Junliang Xie, Zhanmi Zhong et al.

**发布日期**: 2026-11-24

**arXiv ID**: [2611.32443v1](http://arxiv.org/abs/2611.32443v1)

**PDF**: [下载链接](http://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.32443v1)

**类别**: cs.CV

<details>
<summary><b>摘要 (Abstract)</b></summary>

Long-horizon panoramic video wandering aims to continuously synthesize scene-consistent $360^\circ$ observations along user-specified camera trajectories from an initial equirectangular projection (ERP) panorama. Existing panoramic video models have achieved promising results in camera-controlled generation, while recent 3D-guided approaches further provide geometric guidance for improving scene consistency. However, stable long-horizon wandering remains challenging due to diverse motion--orientation relationships and imperfect memory guidance induced by accumulated generation artifacts and depth inaccuracies. To address these challenges, we propose \textbf{PVWander}, a 3D-memory-guided framework that accumulates generated observations into an online point-cloud memory and reprojects it to guide subsequent generation, with two complementary designs: \textit{Path-Facing canonicalization} for motion--orientation alignment and \textit{Timestep-aware gating modulation} for adaptive memory guidance. \textit{Path-Facing canonicalization} exploits the horizontal cyclic symmetry of ERP panoramas to uniformly align each clip's ERP orientation with its principal translation direction, yielding more consistent, forward-like relative motions while preserving the prescribed camera path. \textit{Timestep-aware gating modulation} exploits the coarse-to-fine nature of diffusion denoising to adaptively regulate reprojected memory guidance through timestep-dependent channel-wise gates, preserving its structural benefits while mitigating visual artifacts from imperfect projections. Extensive experiments demonstrate that PVWander achieves improved long-horizon generation quality, while ablations validate the effectiveness of both designs.

</details>

<details>
<summary><b>中文摘要</b></summary>

Long-horizon panoramic video wandering aims to continuously synthesize scene-consistent $360^\circ$ observations along user-specified camera trajectories from an initial equirectangular projection (ERP) panorama. Existing panoramic video models have achieved promising results in camera-controlled generation, while recent 3D-guided approaches further provide geometric guidance for improving scene consistency. However, stable long-horizon wandering remains challenging due to diverse motion--orientation relationships and imperfect memory guidance induced by accumulated generation artifacts and depth inaccuracies. To address these challenges, we propose \textbf{PVWander}, a 3D-memory-guided framework that accumulates generated observations into an online point-cloud memory and reprojects it to guide subsequent generation, with two complementary designs: \textit{Path-Facing canonicalization} for motion--orientation alignment and \textit{Timestep-aware gating modulation} for adaptive memory guidance. \textit{Path-Facing canonicalization} exploits the horizontal cyclic symmetry of ERP panoramas to uniformly align each clip's ERP orientation with its principal translation direction, yielding more consistent, forward-like relative motions while preserving the prescribed camera path. \textit{Timestep-aware gating modulation} exploits the coarse-to-fine nature of diffusion denoising to adaptively regulate reprojected memory guidance through timestep-dependent channel-wise gates, preserving its structural benefits while mitigating visual artifacts from imperfect projections. Extensive experiments demonstrate that PVWander achieves improved long-horizon generation quality, while ablations validate the effectiveness of both designs.

</details>

## 31. Video Models are Excellent Motion Models

**中文标题**: Video Models are Excellent Motion Models

**作者**: Kian Izadpanah, Ali Mahdavi Amiri, Daniel Cohen-Or, Yiorgos Chrysanthou, Andreas Aristidou

**发布日期**: 2026-11-24

**arXiv ID**: [2611.32432v1](http://arxiv.org/abs/2611.32432v1)

**PDF**: [下载链接](http://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.32432v1)

**类别**: cs.CV, cs.GR

<details>
<summary><b>摘要 (Abstract)</b></summary>

Video foundation models have emerged as powerful generators of dynamic visual content, exhibiting remarkable capabilities in synthesizing complex actions and interactions. We argue that these models have also become excellent motion generators, with motion priors that are richer and more general than those learned by dedicated motion models. Their advantage extends beyond visual realism and is manifested in two important aspects: motion naturalness, including coherent body coordination, physical plausibility, and realistic interactions with objects and the environment; and motion scope, encompassing a much broader range of activities and semantic coherence, particularly uncommon, complex, and compound actions. To investigate this position, we introduce a common evaluation framework that compares video and dedicated motion models through three complementary settings: text-only motion generation, reconstruction of video-generated motion from sparse keyframes, and video-based enrichment of motion-model outputs. By converting all outputs into a shared 3D skeletal representation, we evaluate motion naturalness, semantic alignment, action coverage, physical motion characteristics, and fidelity to motion constraints. Our results suggest that video foundation models can generate more natural and semantically meaningful movements, particularly for complex and compound actions. Moreover, we investigate motion-conditioned generation through loose skeletal control, finding that dedicated motion models do not consistently improve video-generated motion, while video models can enrich skeletal animations using sparse motion guidance. Our findings suggest a shift in the role of specialized motion models, from generating complete animations to guiding and controlling the richer motion capabilities already embedded in video foundation models.

</details>

<details>
<summary><b>中文摘要</b></summary>

Video foundation models have emerged as powerful generators of dynamic visual content, exhibiting remarkable capabilities in synthesizing complex actions and interactions. We argue that these models have also become excellent motion generators, with motion priors that are richer and more general than those learned by dedicated motion models. Their advantage extends beyond visual realism and is manifested in two important aspects: motion naturalness, including coherent body coordination, physical plausibility, and realistic interactions with objects and the environment; and motion scope, encompassing a much broader range of activities and semantic coherence, particularly uncommon, complex, and compound actions. To investigate this position, we introduce a common evaluation framework that compares video and dedicated motion models through three complementary settings: text-only motion generation, reconstruction of video-generated motion from sparse keyframes, and video-based enrichment of motion-model outputs. By converting all outputs into a shared 3D skeletal representation, we evaluate motion naturalness, semantic alignment, action coverage, physical motion characteristics, and fidelity to motion constraints. Our results suggest that video foundation models can generate more natural and semantically meaningful movements, particularly for complex and compound actions. Moreover, we investigate motion-conditioned generation through loose skeletal control, finding that dedicated motion models do not consistently improve video-generated motion, while video models can enrich skeletal animations using sparse motion guidance. Our findings suggest a shift in the role of specialized motion models, from generating complete animations to guiding and controlling the richer motion capabilities already embedded in video foundation models.

</details>

## 32. Factorized Context Attention: From Emulated to Native Grid Probing

**中文标题**: Factorized Context Attention: From Emulated to Native Grid Probing

**作者**: Mohamed Eltahir, Manar Eyad, Yara Alzahrani, Fatimah Albesher, Duaa M. Tahir et al.

**发布日期**: 2026-11-24

**arXiv ID**: [2611.32423v1](http://arxiv.org/abs/2611.32423v1)

**PDF**: [下载链接](http://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.32423v1)

**类别**: cs.CV, cs.CL

<details>
<summary><b>摘要 (Abstract)</b></summary>

Models read a book, an hour of video, or an image in one forward pass. As the context grows, this pass slows down, loses accuracy, and stops at the context window. Sparse attention is efficient, but keeps the other two limitations. Grid probing, a recent training-free method, recovers the accuracy and extends the reach by asking a frozen model the same question about short local and strided spans of the input, but every question repeats the whole computation, since each span is encoded again. Our insight is that grid probing is emulated attention, an attention pattern reproduced from outside the model through prompts, since each probe only restricts which part of the input the question can see. We introduce Factorized Context Attention (FCA), a training-free block-sparse attention that runs the same pattern natively, as the attention of the model itself. FCA encodes the input once into a KV cache, reads all spans as masked sequences in one batched forward, and uses a stair position schedule to keep every span inside the trained position range. Across long video understanding, long-context text inference and image grounding, FCA matches the accuracy and the reach of grid probing at a fraction of its cost. It gains about 10 points over the single pass on long video and 9 to 14 points on long novels, finds needles in videos of over a thousand frames and in texts four times the length of the window, and answers a later question about the same input in as little as a tenth of the time of grid probing. Native attention thus keeps what emulated attention gains without its cost, all with existing frozen language and vision-language models.

</details>

<details>
<summary><b>中文摘要</b></summary>

Models read a book, an hour of video, or an image in one forward pass. As the context grows, this pass slows down, loses accuracy, and stops at the context window. Sparse attention is efficient, but keeps the other two limitations. FCA encodes the input once into a KV cache, reads all spans as masked sequences in one batched forward, and uses a stair position schedule to keep every span inside the trained position range. Across long video understanding, long-context text inference and image grounding, FCA matches the accuracy and the reach of grid probing at a fraction of its cost. It gains about 10 points over the single pass on long video and 9 to 14 points on long novels, finds needles in videos of over a thousand frames and in texts four times the length of the window, and answers a later question about the same input in as little as a tenth of the time of grid probing. Native attention thus keeps what emulated attention gains without its cost, all with existing frozen language and vision-language models.

</details>

## 33. TESS: Query-Guided Temporal Event-Structured Frame Selection for Long-Video Understanding

**中文标题**: TESS: Query-Guided Temporal Event-Structured Frame Selection for Long-Video Understanding

**作者**: Qi Lu, Shiyan Tong, Shujie Li, Sen Li, Likun shi et al.

**发布日期**: 2026-11-24

**arXiv ID**: [2611.32410v1](http://arxiv.org/abs/2611.32410v1)

**PDF**: [下载链接](http://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.32410v1)

**项目主页**: https://anonymous.4open.science/r/TESS_code.

**类别**: cs.CV

<details>
<summary><b>摘要 (Abstract)</b></summary>

Temporal localization in long videos is challenging because brief, question-relevant events are dispersed among extensive redundant content. Limited visual budgets increase the risk of omitting these events or their temporal context, undermining reliable reasoning. Existing frame selection methods emphasize semantic relevance or temporal coverage, often overlooking the complementary evidence needed to locate related events and establish their temporal relationships. To address this limitation, we propose TESS, a training-free framework for query-guided temporal event-structured frame selection. TESS selects frames that jointly show the reference event and the event asked about, in the required temporal order. Leveraging frozen vision-language representations, TESS integrates soft temporal gating, role-aware budget allocation, and within-role redundancy suppression to preserve complementary event evidence. Extensive experiments across four long-video understanding benchmarks and three multimodal backbones demonstrate that TESS consistently outperforms the strongest compared baseline by 4.0-5.5 percentage points under a fixed frame budget. Further analyses reveal greater improvements on longer videos and robust performance under tighter visual budgets, while independent evidence annotations confirm enhanced event and temporal-relation coverage. These findings establish explicit temporal evidence organization as an effective training-free strategy for preserving event relationships and supporting efficient temporal reasoning over long videos. Our code is available at https://anonymous.4open.science/r/TESS_code.

</details>

<details>
<summary><b>中文摘要</b></summary>

Further analyses reveal greater improvements on longer videos and robust performance under tighter visual budgets, while independent evidence annotations confirm enhanced event and temporal-relation coverage. These findings establish explicit temporal evidence organization as an effective training-free strategy for preserving event relationships and supporting efficient temporal reasoning over long videos. Our code is available at https://anonymous.4open.science/r/TESS_code.

</details>

## 34. Visual-State Graph Memory for Streaming Video Understanding

**中文标题**: Visual-State Graph Memory for Streaming Video Understanding

**作者**: Ailar Mahdizadeh, Pritam Sarkar, Xiangteng He, Leonid Sigal

**发布日期**: 2026-11-24

**arXiv ID**: [2611.32369v1](http://arxiv.org/abs/2611.32369v1)

**PDF**: [下载链接](http://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.32369v1)

**类别**: cs.CV

<details>
<summary><b>摘要 (Abstract)</b></summary>

In streaming video understanding, the evidence needed to answer a question may have already left the recent-frame window. Keeping all frames does not scale with video length, while dropping older frames limits the model's ability to reason about the past. We propose Visual-State Graph (VSG), a training-free memory that stores the state of a stream rather than the raw sequence of frames. VSG represents the stream as a graph of objects and their relations, together with a log of events; similar to a dynamically evolving scene graph. An object observed repeatedly is represented by one persistent node, with a timestamped history of how its state changes. To determine whether a newly mentioned object already exists in the graph, we compare the relations it takes part in with those of stored objects. We build this memory as the video arrives, before the questions are known. When a question arrives, we retrieve relevant objects and events alongside recent frames under a fixed input budget. Our evaluation spans four streaming-video benchmarks and seven open-source vision-language models ranging from 7B to 32B parameters. Under matched input budgets, VSG improves reasoning about the past while preserving real-time understanding.

</details>

<details>
<summary><b>中文摘要</b></summary>

In streaming video understanding, the evidence needed to answer a question may have already left the recent-frame window. VSG represents the stream as a graph of objects and their relations, together with a log of events; similar to a dynamically evolving scene graph. An object observed repeatedly is represented by one persistent node, with a timestamped history of how its state changes. To determine whether a newly mentioned object already exists in the graph, we compare the relations it takes part in with those of stored objects. We build this memory as the video arrives, before the questions are known. When a question arrives, we retrieve relevant objects and events alongside recent frames under a fixed input budget. Our evaluation spans four streaming-video benchmarks and seven open-source vision-language models ranging from 7B to 32B parameters. Under matched input budgets, VSG improves reasoning about the past while preserving real-time understanding.

</details>

## 35. LRQ-Cache: Compressing Feature Caches in Video Diffusion via Low-Rank Approximation and Quantization

**中文标题**: LRQ-Cache: Compressing Feature Caches in Video Diffusion via Low-Rank Approximation and Quantization

**作者**: Ruizhe Yao, Zhen Huang, Songpan Gao, Danyi Liu, Zijian Cao et al.

**发布日期**: 2026-11-24

**arXiv ID**: [2611.32345v1](http://arxiv.org/abs/2611.32345v1)

**PDF**: [下载链接](http://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.32345v1)

**类别**: cs.CV

<details>
<summary><b>摘要 (Abstract)</b></summary>

Video diffusion transformers (DiTs) achieve high-fidelity generation, but inference is expensive because every denoising step evaluates a large transformer over all spatiotemporal tokens of the video. Temporal caching accelerates them by reusing intermediate features across denoising steps. However, retaining dense feature tensors introduces a persistent storage cost that grows with video length, resolution, and cache coverage, reaching 109-346 GiB per video for the predictive caches we evaluate. Such states exceed the memory of a single GPU, so native caches must either be offloaded to the CPU, which slows generation, or be sharded across several GPUs, e.g., with sequence parallelism, so that the cache rather than the compute sets the number of GPUs. We instead shrink the cached state itself, exploiting its redundancy: a few channel patterns shared across tokens capture much of the energy of each cached feature. Based on this observation, we propose LRQ-Cache, a training-free method that combines low-rank approximation and quantization for feature-cache compression. LRQ-Cache represents each cached feature with a small set of factors, quantizes them before storage, and reconstructs the feature at reuse time, leaving cache mechanisms unchanged. Across three caching mechanisms on HunyuanVideo and Wan2.1, LRQ-Cache reduces the persistent state of predictive caches by 42-71x, to 2.3-4.9 GiB per video. VBench stays within 0.25 pp of the native cache for TeaCache, and the larger drops of the Wan2.1 predictive caches, up to 3.5 pp, shrink at higher ranks. As a result, predictive caches that exceed the memory of a single 80 GB H100 GPU fit on it with LRQ-Cache, making generation 2.7-5.9x faster than with CPU-offloaded native caches.

</details>

<details>
<summary><b>中文摘要</b></summary>

LRQ-Cache represents each cached feature with a small set of factors, quantizes them before storage, and reconstructs the feature at reuse time, leaving cache mechanisms unchanged. Across three caching mechanisms on HunyuanVideo and Wan2.1, LRQ-Cache reduces the persistent state of predictive caches by 42-71x, to 2.3-4.9 GiB per video. VBench stays within 0.25 pp of the native cache for TeaCache, and the larger drops of the Wan2.1 predictive caches, up to 3.5 pp, shrink at higher ranks. As a result, predictive caches that exceed the memory of a single 80 GB H100 GPU fit on it with LRQ-Cache, making generation 2.7-5.9x faster than with CPU-offloaded native caches.

</details>

## 36. Get the Next Moment Right: Short-Term Event Planning for Physically Plausible Video Generation

**中文标题**: Get the Next Moment Right: Short-Term Event Planning for Physically Plausible Video Generation

**作者**: Hao Liu, Xiang Tang, Ruotong Li, Xiaopeng Fan, Yujiu Yang

**发布日期**: 2026-11-24

**arXiv ID**: [2611.32337v1](http://arxiv.org/abs/2611.32337v1)

**PDF**: [下载链接](http://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.32337v1)

**类别**: cs.CV

<details>
<summary><b>摘要 (Abstract)</b></summary>

Video generation models synthesize realistic imagery yet still struggle to produce physically plausible evolution. Existing methods guide generation with a motion trajectory predicted for the entire clip in advance, so prediction errors accumulate and degrade later frames. We propose STEP(Short-Term Event Planning), a training-free closed-loop framework that guides generation one short clip at a time. Each round consists of four steps: grounding measures the scene state from the latest frames, event planning predicts the next physical event and renders it into a control clip, latent-guided generation produces the next short clip under this control, and verification checks whether the event occurs and resamples the clip if it does not. Because each round is grounded in the frames actually generated, control relies only on reliable short-range predictions. STEP improves the physical plausibility of three frozen generators on Physics-IQ Verified and PhyGenBench, and can be applied to new generators without training.

</details>

<details>
<summary><b>中文摘要</b></summary>

Video generation models synthesize realistic imagery yet still struggle to produce physically plausible evolution. Existing methods guide generation with a motion trajectory predicted for the entire clip in advance, so prediction errors accumulate and degrade later frames. We propose STEP(Short-Term Event Planning), a training-free closed-loop framework that guides generation one short clip at a time. Each round consists of four steps: grounding measures the scene state from the latest frames, event planning predicts the next physical event and renders it into a control clip, latent-guided generation produces the next short clip under this control, and verification checks whether the event occurs and resamples the clip if it does not. Because each round is grounded in the frames actually generated, control relies only on reliable short-range predictions. STEP improves the physical plausibility of three frozen generators on Physics-IQ Verified and PhyGenBench, and can be applied to new generators without training.

</details>

## 37. SofaSpace: A Benchmark for Executable Visual Spatial Reasoning in Continuous Motion and Environment Construction

**中文标题**: SofaSpace: A Benchmark for Executable Visual Spatial Reasoning in Continuous Motion and Environment Construction

**作者**: Ze Rong, Kun Shi, Zhedong Zheng

**发布日期**: 2026-11-24

**arXiv ID**: [2611.32285v1](http://arxiv.org/abs/2611.32285v1)

**PDF**: [下载链接](http://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.32285v1)

**类别**: cs.CV

<details>
<summary><b>摘要 (Abstract)</b></summary>

A rigid body can fit at its start and goal yet collide with a wall as it moves between them. Inspired by the moving sofa problem, we introduce SofaSpace, a benchmark for visual spatial reasoning through continuous planar rigid-body transport and environment construction. SofaSpace contains 3,000 scenes organized around three challenges: geometric compatibility, nonlocal maneuver planning, and interactive and temporal coordination. Four task views ask models to plan a motion, reconstruct a layout, design an environment that meets transport requirements, or jointly generate a layout and a motion. A separate set of 534 SofaSpace-Hard tasks tests planning and judgment. A geometric verifier checks the entire body throughout each submitted motion. On static planning tasks, endpoint agreement and continuous validity differ substantially within the same submitted plans: Claude drops from 97.2% to 52.8%, DeepSeek from 100.0% to 30.6%, and Kimi from 97.2% to 29.4%, while the strongest configuration retains 98.3% full validity. Environment construction reveals a further gap. Of 295 feasible layouts generated by the strongest configuration, 181 satisfy all evaluated functional requirements; for Kimi, one of 172 does. These results show how executable checks distinguish endpoint agreement from valid motion and geometric feasibility from functional compliance.

</details>

<details>
<summary><b>中文摘要</b></summary>

Environment construction reveals a further gap. Of 295 feasible layouts generated by the strongest configuration, 181 satisfy all evaluated functional requirements; for Kimi, one of 172 does. These results show how executable checks distinguish endpoint agreement from valid motion and geometric feasibility from functional compliance.

</details>

## 38. StreamCoach: Proactive Sports Coaching via Continuous Visual Reasoning

**中文标题**: StreamCoach: Proactive Sports Coaching via Continuous Visual Reasoning

**作者**: Xiaoye Liang, Yiming Huang, Jialu Wu, Yushe Cao, Ruxiang Duan et al.

**发布日期**: 2026-11-24

**arXiv ID**: [2611.32281v1](http://arxiv.org/abs/2611.32281v1)

**PDF**: [下载链接](http://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.32281v1)

**类别**: cs.CV

<details>
<summary><b>摘要 (Abstract)</b></summary>

In fitness and sports training, timely action quality assessment (AQA) is essential for learners to identify and correct technical errors during movement execution. However, most AQA methods assess completed performances, limiting their ability to provide timely feedback. Streaming methods alleviate this latency, but limited reasoning hinders their use of subtle movement cues and sport-specific knowledge. Moreover, their fragmented feedback focuses on one body part at a time, overlooking movement details of other body parts and transitions between action phases. To address these limitations, we propose StreamCoach, a streaming AQA framework under the OnCueCoaching paradigm that couples continuous chain-of-thought (CoT) reasoning with proactive, evidence-gated coaching. Technique-Aware Reasoning tracks body-part movements and action-phase transitions against sport-specific criteria, while Holistic Coaching integrates accumulated evidence to deliver timely, comprehensive feedback on strengths and technical errors. To support training and benchmarking, we construct EE4D-Stream from 1,213 sports video sequences in Ego-Exo4D through LLM-assisted reannotation and manual review of selected claims, pairing dense CoT annotations with evidence-gated speech content. Building on this dataset, we train StreamCoach using Temporal-Semantic Alignment Reinforcement Learning to jointly optimize feedback timing and content. We benchmark 11 offline and streaming baselines on EE4D-Stream, including three proprietary models. StreamCoach achieves state-of-the-art results across intervention timing, action assessment, and coaching quality, surpassing both GPT-6.1 Sol and Claude Opus 5.5.

</details>

<details>
<summary><b>中文摘要</b></summary>

</details>

## 39. ST-SfM: Spatio-Temporal Structure-from-Motion for Initialising 4D Gaussian Splatting

**中文标题**: ST-SfM: Spatio-Temporal Structure-from-Motion for Initialising 4D Gaussian Splatting

**作者**:  Harshit, Sami Brandt

**发布日期**: 2026-11-24

**arXiv ID**: [2611.32231v1](http://arxiv.org/abs/2611.32231v1)

**PDF**: [下载链接](http://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.32231v1)

**类别**: cs.CV

<details>
<summary><b>摘要 (Abstract)</b></summary>

Dynamic Gaussian splatting is usually initialised either with random points or with a structure-from-motion (SfM) cloud of the first frame. Neither says anything about what moves, where and when. We introduce ST-SfM, a sparse spatio-temporal SfM for synchronised, calibrated multi-camera video that splits the scene into two branches. Static structure is detected once per camera on a temporal-median image, gated for temporal stability and matched along epi-polar lines. Motion is detected as space-time interest points (STIPs) in the video volume, described with HOG3D and matched only between candidates that agree both epipolarly and in time. Union-find tracks that refuse inconsistent merges are triangulated with RANSAC over all view pairs, giving static 3D points and dynamic 4D points (x, y, z, t) with sub-frame times. We use ST-SfM derived sparse 4D cloud to initialise 4D Gaussian splatting. In comparison with random initialisation on N3DV scenes we get improvements in PSNR, SSIM and LPIPS.

</details>

<details>
<summary><b>中文摘要</b></summary>

Dynamic Gaussian splatting is usually initialised either with random points or with a structure-from-motion (SfM) cloud of the first frame. Neither says anything about what moves, where and when. We introduce ST-SfM, a sparse spatio-temporal SfM for synchronised, calibrated multi-camera video that splits the scene into two branches. Static structure is detected once per camera on a temporal-median image, gated for temporal stability and matched along epi-polar lines. Motion is detected as space-time interest points (STIPs) in the video volume, described with HOG3D and matched only between candidates that agree both epipolarly and in time. Union-find tracks that refuse inconsistent merges are triangulated with RANSAC over all view pairs, giving static 3D points and dynamic 4D points (x, y, z, t) with sub-frame times. We use ST-SfM derived sparse 4D cloud to initialise 4D Gaussian splatting. In comparison with random initialisation on N3DV scenes we get improvements in PSNR, SSIM and LPIPS.

</details>

## 40. MoGe-4D: Efficient, Flexible, and Unified Dense 4D Reconstruction and Tracking

**中文标题**: MoGe-4D: Efficient, Flexible, and Unified Dense 4D Reconstruction and Tracking

**作者**: Ruicheng Wang, Linlin Xin, Sicheng Xu, Jianfeng Xiang, Zelong Lv et al.

**发布日期**: 2026-11-24

**arXiv ID**: [2611.32198v1](http://arxiv.org/abs/2611.32198v1)

**PDF**: [下载链接](http://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.32198v1)

**类别**: cs.CV

<details>
<summary><b>摘要 (Abstract)</b></summary>

Reconstructing dynamic scenes from RGB video requires reasoning about both 3D geometry and motion across viewpoints, yet existing methods either split reconstruction and tracking into fragmented pipelines or face prohibitive dense spatiotemporal decoding costs. We introduce MoGe-4D, a feed-forward framework that unifies geometry and motion prediction via ordered queries of paired allocentric and egocentric dynamic point maps. Each query maps source pixels to a target time simultaneously in both camera frames, resolving relative camera pose in closed form while flexibly serving depth, dynamic registration, and dense 3D tracking. For efficient dense querying, MoGe-4D decouples scene modeling from query decoding: a high-capacity context encoder caches coarse global spatiotemporal features once, while a lightweight query encoder rapidly decodes dense point maps at flexible resolutions. Furthermore, as supervising dense 4D queries demands continuous 3D correspondences whose raw trajectory storage is prohibitive, we introduce RigIDTrack, a synthetic data pipeline and dataset that compresses surface dynamics into rigidity-regularized affine bodies, recovering exact arbitrary-frame 3D trajectories with $\sim15\%$ storage overhead over RGB-D video. Across diverse benchmarks, MoGe-4D achieves state-of-the-art dense 3D tracking, matches dedicated 3D foundation models in reconstruction, and runs substantially faster than prior 4D baselines.

</details>

<details>
<summary><b>中文摘要</b></summary>

Reconstructing dynamic scenes from RGB video requires reasoning about both 3D geometry and motion across viewpoints, yet existing methods either split reconstruction and tracking into fragmented pipelines or face prohibitive dense spatiotemporal decoding costs. We introduce MoGe-4D, a feed-forward framework that unifies geometry and motion prediction via ordered queries of paired allocentric and egocentric dynamic point maps. Each query maps source pixels to a target time simultaneously in both camera frames, resolving relative camera pose in closed form while flexibly serving depth, dynamic registration, and dense 3D tracking. For efficient dense querying, MoGe-4D decouples scene modeling from query decoding: a high-capacity context encoder caches coarse global spatiotemporal features once, while a lightweight query encoder rapidly decodes dense point maps at flexible resolutions. Furthermore, as supervising dense 4D queries demands continuous 3D correspondences whose raw trajectory storage is prohibitive, we introduce RigIDTrack, a synthetic data pipeline and dataset that compresses surface dynamics into rigidity-regularized affine bodies, recovering exact arbitrary-frame 3D trajectories with $\sim15\%$ storage overhead over RGB-D video. Across diverse benchmarks, MoGe-4D achieves state-of-the-art dense 3D tracking, matches dedicated 3D foundation models in reconstruction, and runs substantially faster than prior 4D baselines.

</details>

## 41. SoccerNarrate: Event-Grounded Streaming Soccer Commentary with Macro-Window Preference Alignment

**中文标题**: SoccerNarrate: Event-Grounded Streaming Soccer Commentary with Macro-Window Preference Alignment

**作者**: Zihan Jia, Zhilin Dai, Zhengming Zhang, Min Yang, Zhenpeng Huang et al.

**发布日期**: 2026-11-24

**arXiv ID**: [2611.32153v1](http://arxiv.org/abs/2611.32153v1)

**PDF**: [下载链接](http://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.32153v1)

**代码仓库**: https://github.com/MCG-NJU/SoccerNarrate.

**类别**: cs.CV

<details>
<summary><b>摘要 (Abstract)</b></summary>

Existing clip-based soccer commentary models often produce delayed, repeated, or poorly synchronized narration on untrimmed full-match videos. Recent streaming video-language models make low-latency full-match narration feasible, but they often favor general descriptions rather than professional, event-grounded soccer commentary. They may describe nearby actions while failing to mention key events such as goals, cards, substitutions, and offsides correctly and on time. In this paper, we present SoccerNarrate to bridge low-latency streaming narration with event-grounded professional soccer commentary across data, training, and evaluation. First, we construct SoccerNarrate-Data, a large-scale time-anchored dataset with word-level ASR alignment, quality filtering, and entity calibration. Second, we perform macro-window preference alignment (MWPA), which aligns the model with complete event-level commentary semantics by comparing causal multi-step rollouts from the same streaming prefix while keeping second-level inference unchanged. We further use event-mismatched human commentaries as counterfactual negatives to emphasize event semantics over commentary style. Third, we introduce SoccerNarrate-Eval, an event-centric benchmark based on temporally constrained event entailment. Full-match experiments show that SoccerNarrate improves event coverage and event-aligned precision over strong offline and streaming baselines. Code and models are released at https://github.com/MCG-NJU/SoccerNarrate.

</details>

<details>
<summary><b>中文摘要</b></summary>

Existing clip-based soccer commentary models often produce delayed, repeated, or poorly synchronized narration on untrimmed full-match videos. Recent streaming video-language models make low-latency full-match narration feasible, but they often favor general descriptions rather than professional, event-grounded soccer commentary. They may describe nearby actions while failing to mention key events such as goals, cards, substitutions, and offsides correctly and on time. In this paper, we present SoccerNarrate to bridge low-latency streaming narration with event-grounded professional soccer commentary across data, training, and evaluation. First, we construct SoccerNarrate-Data, a large-scale time-anchored dataset with word-level ASR alignment, quality filtering, and entity calibration. Second, we perform macro-window preference alignment (MWPA), which aligns the model with complete event-level commentary semantics by comparing causal multi-step rollouts from the same streaming prefix while keeping second-level inference unchanged. We further use event-mismatched human commentaries as counterfactual negatives to emphasize event semantics over commentary style. Third, we introduce SoccerNarrate-Eval, an event-centric benchmark based on temporally constrained event entailment. Full-match experiments show that SoccerNarrate improves event coverage and event-aligned precision over strong offline and streaming baselines. Code and models are released at https://github.com/MCG-NJU/SoccerNarrate.

</details>

## 42. RoboProbe: Benchmarking Physical Plausibility of Robot World Models

**中文标题**: RoboProbe: Benchmarking Physical Plausibility of Robot World Models

**作者**: Zhuoying Yang, Jiachen Ni, Yichen Guo, Xiaoshan Wu, Mengyu Wang

**发布日期**: 2026-11-24

**arXiv ID**: [2611.32152v1](http://arxiv.org/abs/2611.32152v1)

**PDF**: [下载链接](http://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.32152v1)

**类别**: cs.CV, cs.RO

<details>
<summary><b>摘要 (Abstract)</b></summary>

Evaluating the physical plausibility of generated videos is critical for reliable robotic planning with world models. These videos inform action proposal and assessment before real-world execution, so undetected physical violations can lead to erroneous assessments of action feasibility and safety. Yet current video generators often produce physically implausible interactions. Detecting such violations directly with vision-language models (VLMs) remains challenging as physical verification requires evidence-grounded spatiotemporal reasoning beyond high-level semantic understanding. We develop five law-specific metrics for evaluating physical plausibility by combining spatiotemporal evidence from segmentation, tracking, 3D geometry, or appearance comparisons with targeted VLM reasoning: Contact fidelity, Object persistence, Solidity, Robot deformation, and Object deformation. Building on this metric suite, we introduce RoboProbe, an agentic evaluation framework that coordinates metric execution and selectively refines evaluations through constrained actions. To evaluate the physical plausibility of robot world models, we further introduce RP-Bench, with 761 human-labeled generated videos and 130 real demonstrations of 239 manipulation tasks from 12 robot datasets. On RP-Bench, all five metrics achieve AUROCs above 0.85 and outperform the evaluated learned judges, prior evaluators, and a frames-only Gemini baseline. Applying these metrics to five video generators reveals distinct patterns of physical violations across models, in both violation types and frequencies.

</details>

<details>
<summary><b>中文摘要</b></summary>

Evaluating the physical plausibility of generated videos is critical for reliable robotic planning with world models. These videos inform action proposal and assessment before real-world execution, so undetected physical violations can lead to erroneous assessments of action feasibility and safety. Yet current video generators often produce physically implausible interactions. Detecting such violations directly with vision-language models (VLMs) remains challenging as physical verification requires evidence-grounded spatiotemporal reasoning beyond high-level semantic understanding. We develop five law-specific metrics for evaluating physical plausibility by combining spatiotemporal evidence from segmentation, tracking, 3D geometry, or appearance comparisons with targeted VLM reasoning: Contact fidelity, Object persistence, Solidity, Robot deformation, and Object deformation. Building on this metric suite, we introduce RoboProbe, an agentic evaluation framework that coordinates metric execution and selectively refines evaluations through constrained actions. To evaluate the physical plausibility of robot world models, we further introduce RP-Bench, with 761 human-labeled generated videos and 130 real demonstrations of 239 manipulation tasks from 12 robot datasets. On RP-Bench, all five metrics achieve AUROCs above 0.85 and outperform the evaluated learned judges, prior evaluators, and a frames-only Gemini baseline. Applying these metrics to five video generators reveals distinct patterns of physical violations across models, in both violation types and frequencies.

</details>

## 43. LiteSTAR: Low-Rank Channel Modulation for Temporally Consistent CVD Video Compensation

**中文标题**: LiteSTAR: Low-Rank Channel Modulation for Temporally Consistent CVD Video Compensation

**作者**: Peiyang Lin, Sichen Lin, Yipeng Liao, Liqun Lin, Xu Wang et al.

**发布日期**: 2026-11-24

**arXiv ID**: [2611.32140v1](http://arxiv.org/abs/2611.32140v1)

**PDF**: [下载链接](http://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.32140v1)

**类别**: cs.CV

<details>
<summary><b>摘要 (Abstract)</b></summary>

Color Vision Deficiency (CVD) affects a large population worldwide, yet practical recoloring must run continuously on-device. Frame-wise video recoloring produces color flickering, while existing methods rely on costly cross-frame alignment in pixel or feature space. We observe that color flickering correlates with variations in channel-wise responses, with color-sensitive variations concentrated in a low-rank subspace. Based on this observation, we propose Lightweight Spatio-Temporal Adaptive Recoloring (LiteSTAR), built around LiCaD, a plug-and-play Lightweight Channel-Adaptive Decoder. LiCaD performs temporal filtering in a low-rank state space with learned process and observation uncertainties. It then modulates feature channels to achieve temporal consistency without explicit spatial alignment. LiteSTAR further employs adaptive patch routing to improve color discrimination for CVD users while avoiding unnecessary over-enhancement. We also introduce EgoCVDSet, the first egocentric video benchmark for CVD recoloring. Extensive quantitative and qualitative experiments demonstrate that LiteSTAR achieves superior performance with only 0.05M parameters. LiCaD further demonstrates strong generalization across different tasks and network architectures. The code, models, and dataset will be released after review.

</details>

<details>
<summary><b>中文摘要</b></summary>

Color Vision Deficiency (CVD) affects a large population worldwide, yet practical recoloring must run continuously on-device. Frame-wise video recoloring produces color flickering, while existing methods rely on costly cross-frame alignment in pixel or feature space. We observe that color flickering correlates with variations in channel-wise responses, with color-sensitive variations concentrated in a low-rank subspace. Based on this observation, we propose Lightweight Spatio-Temporal Adaptive Recoloring (LiteSTAR), built around LiCaD, a plug-and-play Lightweight Channel-Adaptive Decoder. LiCaD performs temporal filtering in a low-rank state space with learned process and observation uncertainties. It then modulates feature channels to achieve temporal consistency without explicit spatial alignment. LiteSTAR further employs adaptive patch routing to improve color discrimination for CVD users while avoiding unnecessary over-enhancement. We also introduce EgoCVDSet, the first egocentric video benchmark for CVD recoloring. Extensive quantitative and qualitative experiments demonstrate that LiteSTAR achieves superior performance with only 0.05M parameters. LiCaD further demonstrates strong generalization across different tasks and network architectures. The code, models, and dataset will be released after review.

</details>

## 44. The Old Scene Lives in the Cache: Diagnosing and Shedding Stale History in Streaming Video Generation

**中文标题**: The Old Scene Lives in the Cache: Diagnosing and Shedding Stale History in Streaming Video Generation

**作者**: Hao Zhou, Ziyang Xu, Chenhan Jin, Hao Qin, Shengze Xu et al.

**发布日期**: 2026-11-24

**arXiv ID**: [2611.32116v1](http://arxiv.org/abs/2611.32116v1)

**PDF**: [下载链接](http://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.32116v1)

**类别**: cs.CV

<details>
<summary><b>摘要 (Abstract)</b></summary>

Switching scenes while preserving the original subject in streaming video generation exposes a conflict: cached history helps maintain the subject but also sustains old-scene objects that should disappear. Through controlled interventions on text conditioning, denoising steps, the local window, and the attention sink, we identify how cached visual history keeps this obsolete content. When the original frames remain in the cache, KV flush and sink recomputation still leave old-scene objects in the generated videos. Replacing this history with target-scene history largely removes those objects, but also changes the subject, reducing face similarity to the pre-switch subject. We then propose Molt Forcing, a training-free method that rebuilds scene history from a cold-start target scene while selectively retaining subject tokens from the original cache. Across 16 scene families, Molt Forcing removes old-scene objects at least as reliably as an Echo-Forcing hard cut while keeping the subject much closer to its pre-switch appearance.

</details>

<details>
<summary><b>中文摘要</b></summary>

Switching scenes while preserving the original subject in streaming video generation exposes a conflict: cached history helps maintain the subject but also sustains old-scene objects that should disappear. Through controlled interventions on text conditioning, denoising steps, the local window, and the attention sink, we identify how cached visual history keeps this obsolete content. When the original frames remain in the cache, KV flush and sink recomputation still leave old-scene objects in the generated videos. Replacing this history with target-scene history largely removes those objects, but also changes the subject, reducing face similarity to the pre-switch subject. We then propose Molt Forcing, a training-free method that rebuilds scene history from a cold-start target scene while selectively retaining subject tokens from the original cache. Across 16 scene families, Molt Forcing removes old-scene objects at least as reliably as an Echo-Forcing hard cut while keeping the subject much closer to its pre-switch appearance.

</details>

## 45. SurgGenesis: A Generative Surgical World Model for Future-Aware Surgical Understanding

**中文标题**: SurgGenesis: A Generative Surgical World Model for Future-Aware Surgical Understanding

**作者**: Qirui Zhong, Quande Liu, Yang Chen, Cheng Xue

**发布日期**: 2026-11-24

**arXiv ID**: [2611.32054v1](http://arxiv.org/abs/2611.32054v1)

**PDF**: [下载链接](http://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.32054v1)

**项目主页**: https://zh-qr.github.io/SurgGenesis

**类别**: cs.CV

<details>
<summary><b>摘要 (Abstract)</b></summary>

Surgical scene understanding is constrained by data scarcity and long-tail class imbalance, as annotating surgical videos demands scarce clinical expertise while rare procedural events remain underrepresented. We tackle this through data generation and propose SurgGenesis, a generative surgical world model that learns future surgical state transitions in a latent space from visual observations and textual procedural descriptions, serving as a scalable surgical data engine. To adapt a large-scale video diffusion model to the data-scarce surgical domain without catastrophic forgetting, we introduce a three-stage progressive LoRA strategy that incrementally injects endoscopic visual priors, procedural semantics, and spatial motion cues. Rather than judging generation by visual fidelity alone, we validate SurgGenesis along two axes that separate a world model from a generic generator: (i) future-awareness, by comparing predicted futures against the real future through a GT-latent prediction gap and temporal-horizon analysis, and (ii) action-faithful usefulness, by checking that generated clips contain the commanded surgical triplet and that using them to rebalance long-tail events and drive downstream understanding (segmentation, triplet recognition, 3D reconstruction) yields action-specific gains. Conditioned futures therefore complement conventional long-tail sampling. Code: https://zh-qr.github.io/SurgGenesis

</details>

<details>
<summary><b>中文摘要</b></summary>

Surgical scene understanding is constrained by data scarcity and long-tail class imbalance, as annotating surgical videos demands scarce clinical expertise while rare procedural events remain underrepresented. We tackle this through data generation and propose SurgGenesis, a generative surgical world model that learns future surgical state transitions in a latent space from visual observations and textual procedural descriptions, serving as a scalable surgical data engine. To adapt a large-scale video diffusion model to the data-scarce surgical domain without catastrophic forgetting, we introduce a three-stage progressive LoRA strategy that incrementally injects endoscopic visual priors, procedural semantics, and spatial motion cues. Rather than judging generation by visual fidelity alone, we validate SurgGenesis along two axes that separate a world model from a generic generator: (i) future-awareness, by comparing predicted futures against the real future through a GT-latent prediction gap and temporal-horizon analysis, and (ii) action-faithful usefulness, by checking that generated clips contain the commanded surgical triplet and that using them to rebalance long-tail events and drive downstream understanding (segmentation, triplet recognition, 3D reconstruction) yields action-specific gains. Conditioned futures therefore complement conventional long-tail sampling. Code: https://zh-qr.github.io/SurgGenesis

</details>

## 46. DeCo-Attention: Decompose to Compensate for Low-Precision Attention

**中文标题**: DeCo-Attention: Decompose to Compensate for Low-Precision Attention

**作者**: Yuhang He, Harrison Zhang, Fanrong Li, Chang Liu, RuQing G. Xu et al.

**发布日期**: 2026-11-24

**arXiv ID**: [2611.32035v1](http://arxiv.org/abs/2611.32035v1)

**PDF**: [下载链接](http://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.32035v1)

**类别**: cs.CV

<details>
<summary><b>摘要 (Abstract)</b></summary>

Self-attention over tens of thousands of tokens dominates the generation time of video diffusion transformers, because its cost grows quadratically with sequence length. The FP4 matrix products of modern GPUs can speed up attention, but the 4-bit rounding of the queries Q and the keys K introduces large errors. We show that the exponential in softmax amplifies the error of their product QK, which therefore dominates the attention error. We propose DeCo-Attention, a co-design of algorithm and system for low-precision attention. DeCo-Attention is training-free and compensates the rounding error of Q and K along a few principal directions found by a per-head eigendecomposition. The lightweight compensation terms are appended to Q and K as extra channels, so the FP4 matrix product corrects its own rounding error while it computes the attention scores. Once the products run in FP4, the softmax bounds the kernel, and we optimize the softmax so that the gain of FP4 computation translates into realized speedup. With QK in FP4, we implement DeCo-Attention with the rest of attention in FP8 on GB300 and VR200 and in FP4 on RTX PRO 6000. On Wan2.2, these kernels run 2.13x, 2.49x, and 2.82x faster than BF16 attention, and the whole generation runs 1.55x, 1.68x, and 1.80x faster. On four video generation models, the FP8 configuration achieves LPIPS comparable to prior 8-bit QK methods, and the fully FP4 configuration lower LPIPS than prior fully 4-bit kernels.

</details>

<details>
<summary><b>中文摘要</b></summary>

Self-attention over tens of thousands of tokens dominates the generation time of video diffusion transformers, because its cost grows quadratically with sequence length. The FP4 matrix products of modern GPUs can speed up attention, but the 4-bit rounding of the queries Q and the keys K introduces large errors. We show that the exponential in softmax amplifies the error of their product QK, which therefore dominates the attention error. We propose DeCo-Attention, a co-design of algorithm and system for low-precision attention. DeCo-Attention is training-free and compensates the rounding error of Q and K along a few principal directions found by a per-head eigendecomposition. The lightweight compensation terms are appended to Q and K as extra channels, so the FP4 matrix product corrects its own rounding error while it computes the attention scores. Once the products run in FP4, the softmax bounds the kernel, and we optimize the softmax so that the gain of FP4 computation translates into realized speedup. With QK in FP4, we implement DeCo-Attention with the rest of attention in FP8 on GB300 and VR200 and in FP4 on RTX PRO 6000. On Wan2.2, these kernels run 2.13x, 2.49x, and 2.82x faster than BF16 attention, and the whole generation runs 1.55x, 1.68x, and 1.80x faster. On four video generation models, the FP8 configuration achieves LPIPS comparable to prior 8-bit QK methods, and the fully FP4 configuration lower LPIPS than prior fully 4-bit kernels.

</details>

## 47. Beyond Before-and-After Images: Evaluating Temporal Grounding in a Lightweight VLM for Disaster Assessment

**中文标题**: Beyond Before-and-After Images: Evaluating Temporal Grounding in a Lightweight VLM for Disaster Assessment

**作者**: Shubh Garg, Pratham Arora, Debabrata Ghosh

**发布日期**: 2026-11-24

**arXiv ID**: [2611.32022v1](http://arxiv.org/abs/2611.32022v1)

**PDF**: [下载链接](http://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.32022v1)

**类别**: cs.CV, cs.AI

<details>
<summary><b>摘要 (Abstract)</b></summary>

Bi-temporal satellite imagery is central to disaster assessment: deciding what was damaged requires comparing a pre-event scene with its post-event counterpart. A vision-language model (VLM) can nonetheless appear to answer change questions while relying mainly on the post-event image or on the prompt's temporal framing. We present a controlled diagnostic of temporal grounding and apply it to one lightweight VLM, InternVL3-2B, on DisasterM3: 1,659 optical and SAR image pairs from 41 events and nine disaster types, in event-disjoint partitions, with 9,031 reasoning questions. We compare post-only input, the true pre/post pair, the post image repeated twice, reversed image order, and an unrelated pre-event image. The true pair scores $+0.5$ accuracy points above the repeated-post control (95\% paired bootstrap CI $[-0.2, +1.2]$), and reversing the order changes accuracy by $-0.2$ points ($[-0.8, +0.5]$). Under this protocol we therefore find no measurable benefit from the content of the true pre-event image and little sensitivity to temporal order. The model is not blind to the first image, however: the true pair scores $2.5$ points above a pair whose pre-event image comes from another event ($[+1.6, +3.4]$). State-transition and multi-hop questions remain hard, and optical and SAR inputs show different profiles. We outline temporal scene graphs as a testable next step, not a validated solution, and argue that temporal benchmarks for Earth observation should report whether answers depend on the intended evidence, not only how often they are right.

</details>

<details>
<summary><b>中文摘要</b></summary>

Bi-temporal satellite imagery is central to disaster assessment: deciding what was damaged requires comparing a pre-event scene with its post-event counterpart. A vision-language model (VLM) can nonetheless appear to answer change questions while relying mainly on the post-event image or on the prompt's temporal framing. We compare post-only input, the true pre/post pair, the post image repeated twice, reversed image order, and an unrelated pre-event image. The true pair scores $+0.5$ accuracy points above the repeated-post control (95\% paired bootstrap CI $[-0.2, +1.2]$), and reversing the order changes accuracy by $-0.2$ points ($[-0.8, +0.5]$). Under this protocol we therefore find no measurable benefit from the content of the true pre-event image and little sensitivity to temporal order. The model is not blind to the first image, however: the true pair scores $2.5$ points above a pair whose pre-event image comes from another event ($[+1.6, +3.4]$). State-transition and multi-hop questions remain hard, and optical and SAR inputs show different profiles. We outline temporal scene graphs as a testable next step, not a validated solution, and argue that temporal benchmarks for Earth observation should report whether answers depend on the intended evidence, not only how often they are right.

</details>

## 48. Learning Agent States for Latent Action Modeling

**中文标题**: Learning Agent States for Latent Action Modeling

**作者**: Shaoan Xie, Fan Feng, Peiyuan Zhu, Peter Spirtes, Kun Zhang

**发布日期**: 2026-11-24

**arXiv ID**: [2611.32017v1](http://arxiv.org/abs/2611.32017v1)

**PDF**: [下载链接](http://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.32017v1)

**代码仓库**: https://github.com/Mid-Push/Agent-State-LAM.

**类别**: cs.CV

<details>
<summary><b>摘要 (Abstract)</b></summary>

Latent action models (LAMs) learn action-like latents from unlabeled video by coupling frame-pair inference with latent-conditioned prediction, enabling policy and world-model pretraining. However, what the latent must explain depends on the representation space used for action inference and transition prediction. When this space entangles the agent's evolving physical state with visual context, such as background and persistent agent appearance, inferred latents can encode information unrelated to the agent's actions. To address this challenge, we propose Agent-State LAM, which jointly learns a compact agent-state representation and latent actions modeling its transitions. The compact state aggregates dense visual features into a shared space for both action inference and forward prediction. To guide what this state retains, we introduce spatially matched temporal discrimination (SMTD), which trains the state to distinguish its source frame from nearby frames through spatially flexible matching with frozen local visual features. Rather than comparing global frame representations, SMTD scores each frame by emphasizing its strongest local feature matches across spatial locations. This scoring allows background and persistent agent appearance to remain matched across frames despite displacement, making these shared cues less informative for identifying the source frame and encouraging the representation to capture the agent's evolving physical state. Extensive experiments on distracting control tasks and six real robot video datasets demonstrate improved action recovery, downstream control, and world-model prediction. Code and checkpoints are available at https://github.com/Mid-Push/Agent-State-LAM.

</details>

<details>
<summary><b>中文摘要</b></summary>

Latent action models (LAMs) learn action-like latents from unlabeled video by coupling frame-pair inference with latent-conditioned prediction, enabling policy and world-model pretraining. However, what the latent must explain depends on the representation space used for action inference and transition prediction. When this space entangles the agent's evolving physical state with visual context, such as background and persistent agent appearance, inferred latents can encode information unrelated to the agent's actions. To address this challenge, we propose Agent-State LAM, which jointly learns a compact agent-state representation and latent actions modeling its transitions. The compact state aggregates dense visual features into a shared space for both action inference and forward prediction. To guide what this state retains, we introduce spatially matched temporal discrimination (SMTD), which trains the state to distinguish its source frame from nearby frames through spatially flexible matching with frozen local visual features. Rather than comparing global frame representations, SMTD scores each frame by emphasizing its strongest local feature matches across spatial locations. This scoring allows background and persistent agent appearance to remain matched across frames despite displacement, making these shared cues less informative for identifying the source frame and encouraging the representation to capture the agent's evolving physical state. Extensive experiments on distracting control tasks and six real robot video datasets demonstrate improved action recovery, downstream control, and world-model prediction. Code and checkpoints are available at https://github.com/Mid-Push/Agent-State-LAM.

</details>

---

## 49. DynaCue: Comparing Motion and Timing Between Groups of Videos

**中文标题**: DynaCue: Comparing Motion and Timing Between Groups of Videos

**作者**: Hao Chen

**发布日期**: 2026-11-24

**arXiv ID**: [2611.32014v1](http://arxiv.org/abs/2611.32014v1)

**PDF**: [下载链接](http://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.32014v1)

**类别**: cs.CV

<details>
<summary><b>摘要 (Abstract)</b></summary>

A score that distinguishes videos with different temporal-consistency ratings can miss differences associated with motion preference. VideoScore's temporal consistency score distinguished higher- from lower-rated Pika videos in 95% of comparisons with 15 videos per group, yet could not detect the difference between preferred videos and their same-prompt alternatives in any of the 200 comparisons of 41 VideoGen-RewardBench pairs. We introduce DynaCue, a two-sample permutation test that combines complementary measurements of motion, frame continuity, and appearance to compare groups of videos without choosing a single cue in advance. DynaCue measures local changes in optical flow both before and after accounting for each clip's usual activity, captures interruptions through frame differences and freeze counts, and incorporates scores for motion and appearance. In controlled experiments, DynaCue detected group differences across five interventions affecting playback continuity, displacement, or rhythm. In real-world evaluations of generated videos where viewers rated temporal consistency or chose which of two videos they preferred for its motion, DynaCue detected group differences in at least 80% of resampled comparisons on each of the three datasets at the smaller sample sizes studied. Every other method evaluated on all three had a detection rate of 60% or less on at least one dataset.

</details>

<details>
<summary><b>中文摘要</b></summary>

A score that distinguishes videos with different temporal-consistency ratings can miss differences associated with motion preference. VideoScore's temporal consistency score distinguished higher- from lower-rated Pika videos in 95% of comparisons with 15 videos per group, yet could not detect the difference between preferred videos and their same-prompt alternatives in any of the 200 comparisons of 41 VideoGen-RewardBench pairs. We introduce DynaCue, a two-sample permutation test that combines complementary measurements of motion, frame continuity, and appearance to compare groups of videos without choosing a single cue in advance. DynaCue measures local changes in optical flow both before and after accounting for each clip's usual activity, captures interruptions through frame differences and freeze counts, and incorporates scores for motion and appearance. In controlled experiments, DynaCue detected group differences across five interventions affecting playback continuity, displacement, or rhythm. In real-world evaluations of generated videos where viewers rated temporal consistency or chose which of two videos they preferred for its motion, DynaCue detected group differences in at least 80% of resampled comparisons on each of the three datasets at the smaller sample sizes studied. Every other method evaluated on all three had a detection rate of 60% or less on at least one dataset.

</details>
