## 50. Distractors are the Real Enemy: Visual Distraction Adversarial Perturbations on SAM2-Based Video Tracking and Segmentation

**中文标题**: Distractors are the Real Enemy: Visual Distraction Adversarial Perturbations on SAM2-Based Video Tracking and Segmentation

**作者**: Adonay Demewez Gebremedhin, Mohamad Alansari, Wessam Shehieb, Muzammal Naseer

**发布日期**: 2026-11-24

**arXiv ID**: [2611.32011v1](http://arxiv.org/abs/2611.32011v1)

**PDF**: [下载链接](http://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.32011v1)

**项目主页**: https://adonaydem.github.io/sam2-da-page/

**类别**: cs.CV

<details>
<summary><b>摘要 (Abstract)</b></summary>

Promptable video trackers such as SAM2 achieve accurate and robust performance through strong temporal consistency, making them important foundations for downstream video understanding and tracking applications. As their use continues to expand, understanding their robustness to adversarial perturbations becomes increasingly important. Existing attacks on SAM2 largely focus on local mask degradation and short-term temporal feature disruption, leaving its longer-term dynamics and competing object interferences (Distractors) insufficiently explored. We observe that SAM2 itself exposes useful distractor cues when tracking becomes uncertain. Based on these observations, we propose DistractAttack, a white-box attack that uses background distractors to disrupt target tracking persistently. Its core procedure, Counterfactual Distractor Search (CDS), propagates clean and attacked trajectories in parallel to discover plausible competing regions and identify those that become increasingly effective under perturbation. The discovered evidence is accumulated over time to weaken target support and promote recurrent alternatives or disappearance predictions. Extensive experiments under video-level and one-shot perturbations across Visual Object Tracking and Video Object Segmentation demonstrate strong attack effectiveness, raising DRE to $4.4\times$ and NRE to $7.3\times$ their nearest baselines under video perturbation, while transferring effectively across model scales and tracker variants in black-box settings. Project page: https://adonaydem.github.io/sam2-da-page/

</details>

<details>
<summary><b>中文摘要</b></summary>

As their use continues to expand, understanding their robustness to adversarial perturbations becomes increasingly important. We observe that SAM2 itself exposes useful distractor cues when tracking becomes uncertain. Based on these observations, we propose DistractAttack, a white-box attack that uses background distractors to disrupt target tracking persistently. Its core procedure, Counterfactual Distractor Search (CDS), propagates clean and attacked trajectories in parallel to discover plausible competing regions and identify those that become increasingly effective under perturbation. The discovered evidence is accumulated over time to weaken target support and promote recurrent alternatives or disappearance predictions. Extensive experiments under video-level and one-shot perturbations across Visual Object Tracking and Video Object Segmentation demonstrate strong attack effectiveness, raising DRE to $4.4\times$ and NRE to $7.3\times$ their nearest baselines under video perturbation, while transferring effectively across model scales and tracker variants in black-box settings. Project page: https://adonaydem.github.io/sam2-da-page/

</details>

## 51. SPAR: Self-Referenced World-Model Rewards for Physical Alignment in Video Generation

**中文标题**: SPAR: Self-Referenced World-Model Rewards for Physical Alignment in Video Generation

**作者**: Junyi Zhang, Qichao Wang, Wenzhe Qian, Yuanchuan Wang, Min Zhang

**发布日期**: 2026-11-24

**arXiv ID**: [2611.31958v1](http://arxiv.org/abs/2611.31958v1)

**PDF**: [下载链接](http://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.31958v1)

**项目主页**: https://gavin-jy.github.io/spar-page/

**类别**: cs.CV

<details>
<summary><b>摘要 (Abstract)</b></summary>

Generating videos with physically plausible dynamics remains challenging despite advances in visual quality. Latent world models offer predictive signals for evaluating physical plausibility, but higher agreement between predicted and observed features may reflect easier-to-predict scene content rather than more plausible dynamics. We propose \textbf{S}elf-Referenced \textbf{P}hysical \textbf{A}lignment \textbf{R}ewards (\textbf{SPAR}), a framework that uses world-model predictions for inference-time physical alignment. SPAR compares each video's prediction error with that of static clips constructed by repeating its own frames, providing a content-matched reference for evaluating generated dynamics. To account for the uneven spatial and temporal distribution of physical interactions, we introduce a unified \textbf{spatiotemporal reward refinement} strategy. \textbf{Spatial refinement} emphasizes localized prediction discrepancies, while \textbf{temporal refinement} moderates brief error peaks and supplements prediction errors with agreement between predicted and observed feature changes. Evaluations across multiple benchmarks demonstrate that SPAR improves the physical plausibility of both \textbf{text-to-video generation} and \textbf{image-to-video continuation}, bringing generated dynamics into closer alignment with the physical regularities governing real-world events. Additional results and resources are available at https://gavin-jy.github.io/spar-page/

</details>

<details>
<summary><b>中文摘要</b></summary>

Generating videos with physically plausible dynamics remains challenging despite advances in visual quality. Latent world models offer predictive signals for evaluating physical plausibility, but higher agreement between predicted and observed features may reflect easier-to-predict scene content rather than more plausible dynamics. We propose \textbf{S}elf-Referenced \textbf{P}hysical \textbf{A}lignment \textbf{R}ewards (\textbf{SPAR}), a framework that uses world-model predictions for inference-time physical alignment. SPAR compares each video's prediction error with that of static clips constructed by repeating its own frames, providing a content-matched reference for evaluating generated dynamics. To account for the uneven spatial and temporal distribution of physical interactions, we introduce a unified \textbf{spatiotemporal reward refinement} strategy. \textbf{Spatial refinement} emphasizes localized prediction discrepancies, while \textbf{temporal refinement} moderates brief error peaks and supplements prediction errors with agreement between predicted and observed feature changes. Evaluations across multiple benchmarks demonstrate that SPAR improves the physical plausibility of both \textbf{text-to-video generation} and \textbf{image-to-video continuation}, bringing generated dynamics into closer alignment with the physical regularities governing real-world events. Additional results and resources are available at https://gavin-jy.github.io/spar-page/

</details>

## 52. TACTUS: Unifying Surgical Action Understanding in Time and Space

**中文标题**: TACTUS: Unifying Surgical Action Understanding in Time and Space

**作者**: Lurui Wang, Hao Ding, Chenhao Yu, Chenyan Jing, Jiru Xu et al.

**发布日期**: 2026-11-24

**arXiv ID**: [2611.31956v1](http://arxiv.org/abs/2611.31956v1)

**PDF**: [下载链接](http://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.31956v1)

**类别**: cs.CV, cs.RO

<details>
<summary><b>摘要 (Abstract)</b></summary>

Advancing surgical automation requires a comprehensive spatio-temporal understanding of how instruments act on tissue throughout an action. Existing surgical benchmarks, however, largely define supervision through task-specific targets, leaving the relationships between action semantics, instrument motion, and tissue interaction incompletely specified. To address this limitation, we introduce TACTUS (Tool, Action, Contact and Trajectory Understanding in Surgery), a new dataset and benchmark that establishes a unified annotation framework for complete surgical actions. The framework jointly specifies tool and action identities, nested action and interaction windows, instrument-specific landmark trajectories, and tissue-region tracks. To construct these annotations at scale, we develop a two-tier pipeline combining human annotation, point tracking, and model-generated spatial prompts. Using this pipeline, we re-annotate 141 videos from five public sources, producing 22,995 action clips spanning 2.9 million frames. The dataset comprises 15,668 Gold clips with human spatial prompts followed by review and re-checking, and 7,327 Silver clips combining human semantic and temporal annotations with model-generated spatial prompts. We further introduce TactusNet, the companion reference network, to benchmark semantic, temporal, and spatial prediction from shared video features. Across TactusNet and 13 published baselines, tool-action accuracy reaches 95.8% with per-head encoder fine-tuning, while the best temporal recall and contact Dice reach 46.6% at temporal IoU 0.7 and 0.351, respectively. Moreover, adding Silver improves the default TactusNet's pair accuracy from 93.8% to 95.5%. Together, these contributions establish a foundation for learning and evaluating the instrument-tissue interactions underlying future surgical automation.

</details>

<details>
<summary><b>中文摘要</b></summary>

Advancing surgical automation requires a comprehensive spatio-temporal understanding of how instruments act on tissue throughout an action. Existing surgical benchmarks, however, largely define supervision through task-specific targets, leaving the relationships between action semantics, instrument motion, and tissue interaction incompletely specified. To address this limitation, we introduce TACTUS (Tool, Action, Contact and Trajectory Understanding in Surgery), a new dataset and benchmark that establishes a unified annotation framework for complete surgical actions. The framework jointly specifies tool and action identities, nested action and interaction windows, instrument-specific landmark trajectories, and tissue-region tracks. To construct these annotations at scale, we develop a two-tier pipeline combining human annotation, point tracking, and model-generated spatial prompts. Using this pipeline, we re-annotate 141 videos from five public sources, producing 22,995 action clips spanning 2.9 million frames. The dataset comprises 15,668 Gold clips with human spatial prompts followed by review and re-checking, and 7,327 Silver clips combining human semantic and temporal annotations with model-generated spatial prompts. We further introduce TactusNet, the companion reference network, to benchmark semantic, temporal, and spatial prediction from shared video features. Across TactusNet and 13 published baselines, tool-action accuracy reaches 95.8% with per-head encoder fine-tuning, while the best temporal recall and contact Dice reach 46.6% at temporal IoU 0.7 and 0.351, respectively. Moreover, adding Silver improves the default TactusNet's pair accuracy from 93.8% to 95.5%. Together, these contributions establish a foundation for learning and evaluating the instrument-tissue interactions underlying future surgical automation.

</details>

## 53. DraftKV: Protecting Persistent Worlds through Hierarchical Streaming Retrieval

**中文标题**: DraftKV: Protecting Persistent Worlds through Hierarchical Streaming Retrieval

**作者**: Xiaoling Zhou, Haopeng Li, Yufa Zhou, Rui Ding, Yu Li et al.

**发布日期**: 2026-11-24

**arXiv ID**: [2611.31944v1](http://arxiv.org/abs/2611.31944v1)

**PDF**: [下载链接](http://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.31944v1)

**类别**: cs.CV

<details>
<summary><b>摘要 (Abstract)</b></summary>

Interactive video world models enable action- and camera-controlled exploration, yet struggle to maintain consistent spatial geometry and appearance when revisiting previously explored locations. Historical KV caches help auto-regressive video diffusion models maintain long-term consistency, but their retrieval and attention computation introduce substantial latency as history grows. We propose DraftKV, a training-free framework that retrieves historical KV caches based on geometric grounding and query-conditioned relevance, while combining CPU offloading with tile-wise streaming attention to reduce GPU memory usage and inference latency. Theoretically, we show that draft relevance reproduces the mean branch of full-resolution block relevance exactly, so its deviation is confined to the $β$-weighted max branch, and we bound retrieval utility loss in terms of the measured Top-K selection overlap. We further show that draft relevance cannot distinguish blocks with similar pooled descriptors regardless of camera pose, motivating geometric grounding before refinement. Then, we introduce streaming CG-Attention, which overlaps CPU-to-GPU KV transfers with attention computation through tile-wise streaming, hiding transfer latency while maintaining bounded GPU memory usage. Experiments across diverse model families show that DraftKV achieves superior generation quality and consistency while achieving $3.27\times$ end-to-end speedup and 42.2% peak GPU memory reduction over other CPU-offloaded KV cache retrieval methods. Notably, DraftKV enables high-quality interactive world generation with historical KV retrieval on a single H100 GPU using SolarWM-33B.

</details>

<details>
<summary><b>中文摘要</b></summary>

Interactive video world models enable action- and camera-controlled exploration, yet struggle to maintain consistent spatial geometry and appearance when revisiting previously explored locations. Historical KV caches help auto-regressive video diffusion models maintain long-term consistency, but their retrieval and attention computation introduce substantial latency as history grows. We propose DraftKV, a training-free framework that retrieves historical KV caches based on geometric grounding and query-conditioned relevance, while combining CPU offloading with tile-wise streaming attention to reduce GPU memory usage and inference latency. Theoretically, we show that draft relevance reproduces the mean branch of full-resolution block relevance exactly, so its deviation is confined to the $β$-weighted max branch, and we bound retrieval utility loss in terms of the measured Top-K selection overlap. We further show that draft relevance cannot distinguish blocks with similar pooled descriptors regardless of camera pose, motivating geometric grounding before refinement. Then, we introduce streaming CG-Attention, which overlaps CPU-to-GPU KV transfers with attention computation through tile-wise streaming, hiding transfer latency while maintaining bounded GPU memory usage. Experiments across diverse model families show that DraftKV achieves superior generation quality and consistency while achieving $3.27\times$ end-to-end speedup and 42.2% peak GPU memory reduction over other CPU-offloaded KV cache retrieval methods. Notably, DraftKV enables high-quality interactive world generation with historical KV retrieval on a single H100 GPU using SolarWM-33B.

</details>

## 54. TANDEM: Temporal Anchor Densification With Reliability-Aware Memory for Lightweight Sparse Depth Completion in Endoscopic Robotic Navigation

**中文标题**: TANDEM: Temporal Anchor Densification With Reliability-Aware Memory for Lightweight Sparse Depth Completion in Endoscopic Robotic Navigation

**作者**: Weikuan Qiu, Jiale Hou, Chongzhao Xu, Jinlian Ma

**发布日期**: 2026-11-24

**arXiv ID**: [2611.31922v1](http://arxiv.org/abs/2611.31922v1)

**PDF**: [下载链接](http://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.31922v1)

**代码仓库**: https://github.com/Wk-cpu-art/TANDEM

**类别**: cs.CV

<details>
<summary><b>摘要 (Abstract)</b></summary>

For endoscopic robotic surgery, accurate dense depth perception serves as a foundational capability, which enables navigation, instrument localization, and tissue interaction. However, endoscopic video lacks reliable metric depth, while metric measurements obtained via tracking, time-of-flight sensing, or instrument contact only cover a small fraction of pixels in each frame, rendering sparse depth completion an urgent and unsolved challenge. In this work, we propose Temporal Anchor Densification with Reliability-Aware Memory (TANDEM), a causal sparse depth completion framework for endoscopic video. It adopts a lightweight design and leverages the video stream: past measurements re-projected into the current view recover the metric evidence unavailable within a single sparse frame. To keep this evidence interpretable, accurate, and efficient, an explicit memory scores each re-projected measurement with analytic re-observation, age, and motion-parallax factors without overriding current measurements. A compact network then reconstructs depth via reliability-weighted gradient integration and a frequency-gated reference prior. We evaluate TANDEM on the C3VD colonoscopy and StereoMIS laparoscopy datasets. With only 5.58 M parameters, TANDEM reduces the root mean square error by 11.7% and 32.0%, respectively, compared with the strongest baseline, and achieves state-of-the-art accuracy. It also remains accurate under pose errors of the magnitude reported for endoscopic pose estimation. Code will be released at https://github.com/Wk-cpu-art/TANDEM upon acceptance.

</details>

<details>
<summary><b>中文摘要</b></summary>

For endoscopic robotic surgery, accurate dense depth perception serves as a foundational capability, which enables navigation, instrument localization, and tissue interaction. However, endoscopic video lacks reliable metric depth, while metric measurements obtained via tracking, time-of-flight sensing, or instrument contact only cover a small fraction of pixels in each frame, rendering sparse depth completion an urgent and unsolved challenge. In this work, we propose Temporal Anchor Densification with Reliability-Aware Memory (TANDEM), a causal sparse depth completion framework for endoscopic video. It adopts a lightweight design and leverages the video stream: past measurements re-projected into the current view recover the metric evidence unavailable within a single sparse frame. To keep this evidence interpretable, accurate, and efficient, an explicit memory scores each re-projected measurement with analytic re-observation, age, and motion-parallax factors without overriding current measurements. A compact network then reconstructs depth via reliability-weighted gradient integration and a frequency-gated reference prior. We evaluate TANDEM on the C3VD colonoscopy and StereoMIS laparoscopy datasets. With only 5.58 M parameters, TANDEM reduces the root mean square error by 11.7% and 32.0%, respectively, compared with the strongest baseline, and achieves state-of-the-art accuracy. It also remains accurate under pose errors of the magnitude reported for endoscopic pose estimation. Code will be released at https://github.com/Wk-cpu-art/TANDEM upon acceptance.

</details>

## 55. Loupe: Reinforcing Knowledge-Intensive Video Generation Agents

**中文标题**: Loupe: Reinforcing Knowledge-Intensive Video Generation Agents

**作者**: Kai Zeng, Ruichuan An, Zhen Fang, Xi Fang, Han Lyu et al.

**发布日期**: 2026-11-24

**arXiv ID**: [2611.31895v1](http://arxiv.org/abs/2611.31895v1)

**PDF**: [下载链接](http://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.31895v1)

**项目主页**: https://zengkaiya.github.io/Loupe/.

**类别**: cs.CV, cs.AI

<details>
<summary><b>摘要 (Abstract)</b></summary>

Knowledge-intensive video generation remains challenging because prompts often omit the facts, appearances, and temporal relationships required for accurate generation. While search-based workflows can retrieve missing evidence, manually designed strategies limit adaptive planning, and conventional training suffers from costly video-level feedback and coarse trajectory-level credit assignment. In this paper, we present Loupe, a video generation agent that retrieves and verifies textual and visual evidence to construct evidence-grounded temporal video prompts with reference images. We further introduce Rubric-Based Credit (RBC), which leverages prompt-specific factual, visual, and temporal requirements to evaluate intermediate search and planning decisions before video rendering. Building on RBC, RBC-SFT reweights assistant-token learning by step quality, while RBC-GRPO incorporates step-level credits alongside a final planning reward, without rendering videos during RL. To address the lack of dedicated search-oriented evaluation, we construct Search-VBench, which contains 150 held-out prompts across seven categories. Experiments show that Loupe improves all four evaluation dimensions across three video generators, raising the overall score of MiniMax-H3 from 32.80 to 70.67 and outperforming an untrained search workflow. Ablation experiments show that full RBC training outperforms standard SFT followed by trajectory-level GRPO by 5.27 points. Human evaluations and results on Sci-VBench and KIVI-Bench further demonstrate improved generation quality, scientific grounding, factual precision, and helpfulness. Our project page is available at https://zengkaiya.github.io/Loupe/.

</details>

<details>
<summary><b>中文摘要</b></summary>

While search-based workflows can retrieve missing evidence, manually designed strategies limit adaptive planning, and conventional training suffers from costly video-level feedback and coarse trajectory-level credit assignment. Experiments show that Loupe improves all four evaluation dimensions across three video generators, raising the overall score of MiniMax-H3 from 32.80 to 70.67 and outperforming an untrained search workflow. Ablation experiments show that full RBC training outperforms standard SFT followed by trajectory-level GRPO by 5.27 points. Human evaluations and results on Sci-VBench and KIVI-Bench further demonstrate improved generation quality, scientific grounding, factual precision, and helpfulness. Our project page is available at https://zengkaiya.github.io/Loupe/.

</details>

## 56. Open-OmniCoach: Evidence-Enhanced Trajectory Learning for Omni Video Agents

**中文标题**: Open-OmniCoach: Evidence-Enhanced Trajectory Learning for Omni Video Agents

**作者**: Weisong Liu, Xiangtai Li, Haochen Wang, Haiquan Wen, Zhaoxiang Zhang

**发布日期**: 2026-11-24

**arXiv ID**: [2611.31891v1](http://arxiv.org/abs/2611.31891v1)

**PDF**: [下载链接](http://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.31891v1)

**类别**: cs.CV

<details>
<summary><b>摘要 (Abstract)</b></summary>

Training capable omni video agents requires more than diverse question-answer pairs: agents must learn to reliably find, connect, and correct task-relevant evidence in omni-setting. However, we argue that existing data pipelines provide limited control over task-relevant evidence and weak supervision for these capabilities. In this work, we present Open OmniCoach, a new framework for evidence-seeking omni video agents. It contains a high-quality dataset, OmniCoach-240K, and a new agentic reinforcement learning (RL) method, Step-Level Evidence-Guided Policy Optimization (SEPO). For the former, OmniCoach-240K comprises 248.2K interaction trajectories, including 230.7K multi-span trajectories and 17.4K reflection trajectories. We design a three-stage data pipeline by leveraging both fine-grained omni-evidence and the agent's own errors. Meanwhile, compared with other RL baselines, SEPO provides turn-level credit based on improvements in evidence coverage and temporal localization. Applying both trajectory data and the SEPO method, we obtain Open OmniAgent models. The resulting models outperform the baselines across multiple video and audio-visual understanding tasks by significant margins, demonstrating the effectiveness of the Open OmniCoach framework. In particular, Open OmniAgent-30B improves average accuracy over Qwen3-Omni by 6.3 percentage points across nine benchmarks, with gains of 12.2 and 13.3 points on LVOmniBench and LVBench, respectively.

</details>

<details>
<summary><b>中文摘要</b></summary>

Training capable omni video agents requires more than diverse question-answer pairs: agents must learn to reliably find, connect, and correct task-relevant evidence in omni-setting. However, we argue that existing data pipelines provide limited control over task-relevant evidence and weak supervision for these capabilities. In this work, we present Open OmniCoach, a new framework for evidence-seeking omni video agents. It contains a high-quality dataset, OmniCoach-240K, and a new agentic reinforcement learning (RL) method, Step-Level Evidence-Guided Policy Optimization (SEPO). For the former, OmniCoach-240K comprises 248.2K interaction trajectories, including 230.7K multi-span trajectories and 17.4K reflection trajectories. We design a three-stage data pipeline by leveraging both fine-grained omni-evidence and the agent's own errors. Meanwhile, compared with other RL baselines, SEPO provides turn-level credit based on improvements in evidence coverage and temporal localization. Applying both trajectory data and the SEPO method, we obtain Open OmniAgent models. The resulting models outperform the baselines across multiple video and audio-visual understanding tasks by significant margins, demonstrating the effectiveness of the Open OmniCoach framework. In particular, Open OmniAgent-30B improves average accuracy over Qwen3-Omni by 6.3 percentage points across nine benchmarks, with gains of 12.2 and 13.3 points on LVOmniBench and LVBench, respectively.

</details>

## 57. Rethinking Video Agents: When and Why Video Harnesses Work

**中文标题**: Rethinking Video Agents: When and Why Video Harnesses Work

**作者**: Luoyuan Zhang, Xuanyu Zheng, Yancheng Long, Haoyang Xu, Bin Wen et al.

**发布日期**: 2026-11-24

**arXiv ID**: [2611.31883v1](http://arxiv.org/abs/2611.31883v1)

**PDF**: [下载链接](http://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.31883v1)

**类别**: cs.CV, cs.AI, cs.MM

<details>
<summary><b>摘要 (Abstract)</b></summary>

Video understanding depends not only on how models reason about visual evidence, but also on which evidence they observe. Fixed observation policies determine this evidence in advance; agentic harnesses allow models to seek further observations as their understanding develops. This shift creates an opportunity to realize capabilities that fixed inputs leave untapped, yet the ability to request observations does not ensure their effective use. We investigate when and why video harnesses improve understanding, examining how task demands and models' capacity for interaction jointly shape their benefits. Using Video-Relook as an experimental framework, we let models adapt their temporal and spatial observations from a common default configuration, and analyze performance and interaction trajectories across models and tasks. Adaptive observation yields substantial gains on fine-grained action understanding, with benefits also extending to Video-MME-v2. Even under the same observation interface, gains differ substantially across models; interaction traces reveal corresponding differences in evidence seeking and the reliability of answer revision. The benefits also vary across tasks for the same model, ranging from substantial improvements to limited gains and regressions. Together, these findings support an account in which harness effectiveness depends on the alignment between opportunities for informative observation and the model's ability to turn interaction into reliable revisions. They motivate video harnesses that coordinate perception and reasoning, matching observational autonomy to task demands and model capabilities.

</details>

<details>
<summary><b>中文摘要</b></summary>

Video understanding depends not only on how models reason about visual evidence, but also on which evidence they observe. Fixed observation policies determine this evidence in advance; agentic harnesses allow models to seek further observations as their understanding develops. This shift creates an opportunity to realize capabilities that fixed inputs leave untapped, yet the ability to request observations does not ensure their effective use. We investigate when and why video harnesses improve understanding, examining how task demands and models' capacity for interaction jointly shape their benefits. Using Video-Relook as an experimental framework, we let models adapt their temporal and spatial observations from a common default configuration, and analyze performance and interaction trajectories across models and tasks. Adaptive observation yields substantial gains on fine-grained action understanding, with benefits also extending to Video-MME-v2. Even under the same observation interface, gains differ substantially across models; interaction traces reveal corresponding differences in evidence seeking and the reliability of answer revision. The benefits also vary across tasks for the same model, ranging from substantial improvements to limited gains and regressions. Together, these findings support an account in which harness effectiveness depends on the alignment between opportunities for informative observation and the model's ability to turn interaction into reliable revisions. They motivate video harnesses that coordinate perception and reasoning, matching observational autonomy to task demands and model capabilities.

</details>

## 58. G0.7: Scaling Up a Fast World Action Model with Long Context

**中文标题**: G0.7: Scaling Up a Fast World Action Model with Long Context

**作者**: Tianyuan Yuan, Baijun Ye, Shicheng Cao, Xing Nie, Xiaopeng Yu et al.

**发布日期**: 2026-11-24

**arXiv ID**: [2611.31822v1](http://arxiv.org/abs/2611.31822v1)

**PDF**: [下载链接](http://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.31822v1)

**类别**: cs.CV, cs.RO

<details>
<summary><b>摘要 (Abstract)</b></summary>

World action models (WAMs) adapt pretrained video generators for robot control by jointly predicting future camera frames and actions, transferring physical priors learned from video into manipulation policies. However, generating future frames before every action makes test-time inference too slow for closed-loop control, while most existing policies remain restricted to short observation histories. Building on Fast-WAM, which showed that video prediction shapes policy representations during training so that future frame synthesis can be skipped at deployment, we present G0.7, a generalist world action model scaled along two axes: extending the context window from a single observation to up to five minutes of history, and scaling pretraining to a large-scale corpus of robot teleoperation and human egocentric (ego) video. Across public simulation suites and real-robot deployments, direct action decoding (Fast mode) matches imagine-then-act (IDM) inference from the same checkpoint while running in 60 ms per 16-step action chunk on a single RTX 5090 (7.6x faster than IDM) and supporting pause-free real-time chunking. Performance scales consistently with pretraining data, with human ego video driving the largest gains in out-of-distribution generalization: G0.7 achieves a 70.8 mean score across five simulation benchmark suites, 48% and 54% zero-shot pick-and-place success on Galaxea R1 Pro and R1 Lite, 96.7% in-distribution and 93.3% out-of-distribution success on video-conditioned in-context learning tasks, and 86.9% mean task progress across seven real-world post-training tasks including multi-minute box folding, drink preparation, and product bagging.

</details>

<details>
<summary><b>中文摘要</b></summary>

Across public simulation suites and real-robot deployments, direct action decoding (Fast mode) matches imagine-then-act (IDM) inference from the same checkpoint while running in 60 ms per 16-step action chunk on a single RTX 5090 (7.6x faster than IDM) and supporting pause-free real-time chunking. Performance scales consistently with pretraining data, with human ego video driving the largest gains in out-of-distribution generalization: G0.7 achieves a 70.8 mean score across five simulation benchmark suites, 48% and 54% zero-shot pick-and-place success on Galaxea R1 Pro and R1 Lite, 96.7% in-distribution and 93.3% out-of-distribution success on video-conditioned in-context learning tasks, and 86.9% mean task progress across seven real-world post-training tasks including multi-minute box folding, drink preparation, and product bagging.

</details>

## 59. Beyond the Benchmark: Localizing Released Autonomous Driving Models to Real Vehicles

**中文标题**: Beyond the Benchmark: Localizing Released Autonomous Driving Models to Real Vehicles

**作者**: Morui Zhu, Deyuan Qu, Qi Chen, Qing Yang

**发布日期**: 2026-11-24

**arXiv ID**: [2611.31814v1](http://arxiv.org/abs/2611.31814v1)

**PDF**: [下载链接](http://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.31814v1)

**项目主页**: https://released-to-real.github.io.

**类别**: cs.RO, cs.CV

<details>
<summary><b>摘要 (Abstract)</b></summary>

Released autonomous-driving models achieve strong benchmark performance, yet deploying them on a new real vehicle remains challenging. We study this deployment gap and find that released models can remain coupled to assumptions from their source sensing, data, and execution processes even after conventional adaptation. We introduce Released-to-Real (R2R), a deployment-localization methodology that identifies such source-system coupling and corrects only the affected components while preserving the released model. For data and behavior adaptation, we find that the mismatch between trajectory-level optimization and receding-horizon execution enters through the ego state rather than the observation, and introduce Human-Anchored Reinforcement Learning (HA-RL), which corrects it on recorded target driving without the costly rollouts of generative closed-loop reinforcement learning (RL). For physical sensor adaptation, R2R localizes and corrects source-dependent spatial and temporal representations with frozen perception weights, requiring neither target annotations nor retraining. HA-RL improves the closed-loop success rate of a released diffusion-based planner from 82.2% to 95.2%, while sensor adaptation across multiple released detectors and three real sensor rigs improves the vehicle mean average precision (mAP) of a released multi-camera detector from 0.041 to 0.420 on a five-camera target rig. These results provide a practical path for preparing released models before deployment on new real vehicles. Our project page is publicly available at https://released-to-real.github.io.

</details>

<details>
<summary><b>中文摘要</b></summary>

Released autonomous-driving models achieve strong benchmark performance, yet deploying them on a new real vehicle remains challenging. We study this deployment gap and find that released models can remain coupled to assumptions from their source sensing, data, and execution processes even after conventional adaptation. We introduce Released-to-Real (R2R), a deployment-localization methodology that identifies such source-system coupling and corrects only the affected components while preserving the released model. For data and behavior adaptation, we find that the mismatch between trajectory-level optimization and receding-horizon execution enters through the ego state rather than the observation, and introduce Human-Anchored Reinforcement Learning (HA-RL), which corrects it on recorded target driving without the costly rollouts of generative closed-loop reinforcement learning (RL). For physical sensor adaptation, R2R localizes and corrects source-dependent spatial and temporal representations with frozen perception weights, requiring neither target annotations nor retraining. HA-RL improves the closed-loop success rate of a released diffusion-based planner from 82.2% to 95.2%, while sensor adaptation across multiple released detectors and three real sensor rigs improves the vehicle mean average precision (mAP) of a released multi-camera detector from 0.041 to 0.420 on a five-camera target rig. These results provide a practical path for preparing released models before deployment on new real vehicles. Our project page is publicly available at https://released-to-real.github.io.

</details>

## 60. RoMo Hands: A Large Scale Richly Organized Text to Hand Motion Dataset

**中文标题**: RoMo Hands: A Large Scale Richly Organized Text to Hand Motion Dataset

**作者**: Yizhak Ben-Shabat, Jiahao Zhang, Joseph Liu, Seonghyeon Moon, Young-Yoon Lee et al.

**发布日期**: 2026-11-24

**arXiv ID**: [2611.31766v1](http://arxiv.org/abs/2611.31766v1)

**PDF**: [下载链接](http://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.31766v1)

**项目主页**: https://romodataset.github.io/romo-hands/.

**类别**: cs.CV, cs.AI, cs.GR, cs.LG

<details>
<summary><b>摘要 (Abstract)</b></summary>

While text-driven body motion generation has matured, pure-hand synthesis remains bottlenecked by data. Existing datasets often treat hands as rigid end-effectors, lack fine-grained captions, or are too limited in scale to support robust generalization. We introduce RoMo Hands, an in-the-wild, text-paired dataset of ~542K sequences (884 hours, 95.5M frames at 30 fps) which is an order of magnitude larger than prior work. To ensure high-quality supervision, a stringent dexterity filter discards low-articulation sequences, focusing the distribution on complex bimanual movement. Each clip is paired with five captions at increasing granularities from global semantic tags to atomic finger states, organized under a hierarchical taxonomy. To bridge the gap between body and hand architectures, we propose a novel 393-D bimanual representation that adapts redundant body-motion paradigms to the high-DoF kinematics of hands. Systematic benchmarking of diffusion, masked-token, and autoregressive generators reveals distinct trade-offs under these hand-specific adaptations, including a sharp divergence between semantic alignment and kinematic plausibility. Our dataset, representation, and benchmark establish a rigorous foundation for the next generation of dexterous motion synthesis. Project page: https://romodataset.github.io/romo-hands/.

</details>

<details>
<summary><b>中文摘要</b></summary>

While text-driven body motion generation has matured, pure-hand synthesis remains bottlenecked by data. Existing datasets often treat hands as rigid end-effectors, lack fine-grained captions, or are too limited in scale to support robust generalization. We introduce RoMo Hands, an in-the-wild, text-paired dataset of ~542K sequences (884 hours, 95.5M frames at 30 fps) which is an order of magnitude larger than prior work. Systematic benchmarking of diffusion, masked-token, and autoregressive generators reveals distinct trade-offs under these hand-specific adaptations, including a sharp divergence between semantic alignment and kinematic plausibility. Our dataset, representation, and benchmark establish a rigorous foundation for the next generation of dexterous motion synthesis. Project page: https://romodataset.github.io/romo-hands/.

</details>

## 61. Dreamcatcher: Remembering What Surprises a World Model in Video Streams

**中文标题**: Dreamcatcher: Remembering What Surprises a World Model in Video Streams

**作者**: Jinxi He, Ce Zhang, Wenhe Liu, Jianshu Zhang, Katia P. Sycara et al.

**发布日期**: 2026-11-24

**arXiv ID**: [2611.31765v1](http://arxiv.org/abs/2611.31765v1)

**PDF**: [下载链接](http://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.31765v1)

**类别**: cs.CV

<details>
<summary><b>摘要 (Abstract)</b></summary>

As live video streams grow longer, repeatedly encoding and processing their full histories becomes increasingly costly. Answering questions as they arrive therefore calls for visual memory built in one pass before future questions are known. Existing methods mainly keep tokens that differ from earlier ones or match surrogate queries fixed in advance. Without modeling world dynamics, these criteria struggle to tell subtle surprising details from expected change. We introduce Dreamcatcher, a training-free framework and, to our knowledge, the first to use predictive signals from an independently pretrained, frozen video world model for online memory formation in streaming video question answering. Conditioned on recent history, the world model predicts incoming features using learned world priors. Its normalized prediction errors yield temporal and spatial surprisal signals that guide online segmentation and the retention of a frozen Video-LLM's native visual tokens, balancing surprising details with broad visual coverage under budgets that decay with segment age. At query time, an evidence gate admits a segment from a similarity-based shortlist only when the Video-LLM judges that it adds evidence for the question beyond the current view. We evaluate Dreamcatcher on three streaming and three offline long-video benchmarks. On OVO-Bench, Dreamcatcher achieves the highest overall accuracy among the compared training-free methods for each of four frozen Video-LLMs, reaching 78.9% with Qwen3.8-27B. With the three Qwen Video-LLMs, it also reduces average input tokens for answer generation by more than 80% relative to their base models at 1 fps.

</details>

<details>
<summary><b>中文摘要</b></summary>

Answering questions as they arrive therefore calls for visual memory built in one pass before future questions are known. Existing methods mainly keep tokens that differ from earlier ones or match surrogate queries fixed in advance. Without modeling world dynamics, these criteria struggle to tell subtle surprising details from expected change. We introduce Dreamcatcher, a training-free framework and, to our knowledge, the first to use predictive signals from an independently pretrained, frozen video world model for online memory formation in streaming video question answering. Conditioned on recent history, the world model predicts incoming features using learned world priors. Its normalized prediction errors yield temporal and spatial surprisal signals that guide online segmentation and the retention of a frozen Video-LLM's native visual tokens, balancing surprising details with broad visual coverage under budgets that decay with segment age. At query time, an evidence gate admits a segment from a similarity-based shortlist only when the Video-LLM judges that it adds evidence for the question beyond the current view. We evaluate Dreamcatcher on three streaming and three offline long-video benchmarks. On OVO-Bench, Dreamcatcher achieves the highest overall accuracy among the compared training-free methods for each of four frozen Video-LLMs, reaching 78.9% with Qwen3.8-27B. With the three Qwen Video-LLMs, it also reduces average input tokens for answer generation by more than 80% relative to their base models at 1 fps.

</details>

## 62. LeanWAM: Learning Latent World Action Models from Frozen Video Models

**中文标题**: LeanWAM: Learning Latent World Action Models from Frozen Video Models

**作者**: Shaolong Li, Hongze Fu, Joyce Chai, Yinpei Dai

**发布日期**: 2026-11-24

**arXiv ID**: [2611.31740v1](http://arxiv.org/abs/2611.31740v1)

**PDF**: [下载链接](http://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.31740v1)

**类别**: cs.CV, cs.RO

<details>
<summary><b>摘要 (Abstract)</b></summary>

World action models (WAMs) typically adapt large pretrained video generators to jointly predict future observations and robot actions, incurring substantial training and deployment costs. However, it remains unclear which future representations a policy actually needs and how compactly they can be represented. Our probing study shows that complementary future representations can be compressed into a few tokens while retaining most of their action-decoding performance. Motivated by this finding, we introduce LeanWAM, a latent WAM that transfers video priors from a frozen video generator to a lightweight policy via two-stage training. In the first stage, a Future Extractor compresses observed transitions into 32 predictive tokens. These tokens condition the frozen generator and are jointly shaped by action, RGB, semantic, and geometric supervision, while a compact readout of the generator's hidden states supplies complementary world tokens. The second stage distills both representations into the policy, which learns to predict them from current observations while generating actions. With 1.4B parameters and no large-scale embodied pretraining, LeanWAM outperforms strong vision-language-action (VLA) and WAM baselines on LIBERO, LIBERO-Plus, and RoboTwin~2.0. It predicts an action chunk in 25.1\,ms on an A100 GPU, 12.3--41.5$\times$ faster than representative WAMs. These results demonstrate that compact predictive supervision can transfer video priors to robust and efficient control.

</details>

<details>
<summary><b>中文摘要</b></summary>

World action models (WAMs) typically adapt large pretrained video generators to jointly predict future observations and robot actions, incurring substantial training and deployment costs. However, it remains unclear which future representations a policy actually needs and how compactly they can be represented. Our probing study shows that complementary future representations can be compressed into a few tokens while retaining most of their action-decoding performance. Motivated by this finding, we introduce LeanWAM, a latent WAM that transfers video priors from a frozen video generator to a lightweight policy via two-stage training. In the first stage, a Future Extractor compresses observed transitions into 32 predictive tokens. These tokens condition the frozen generator and are jointly shaped by action, RGB, semantic, and geometric supervision, while a compact readout of the generator's hidden states supplies complementary world tokens. The second stage distills both representations into the policy, which learns to predict them from current observations while generating actions. With 1.4B parameters and no large-scale embodied pretraining, LeanWAM outperforms strong vision-language-action (VLA) and WAM baselines on LIBERO, LIBERO-Plus, and RoboTwin~2.0. It predicts an action chunk in 25.1\,ms on an A100 GPU, 12.3--41.5$\times$ faster than representative WAMs. These results demonstrate that compact predictive supervision can transfer video priors to robust and efficient control.

</details>

## 63. EventSSM: Causal Spatial-State Modelling for Asynchronous Events Event-Depth Estimation

**中文标题**: EventSSM: Causal Spatial-State Modelling for Asynchronous Events Event-Depth Estimation

**作者**: Sadiq O. Macaulay, Dipon Ghosh, Hadfield Simon

**发布日期**: 2026-11-24

**arXiv ID**: [2611.31720v1](http://arxiv.org/abs/2611.31720v1)

**PDF**: [下载链接](http://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.31720v1)

**类别**: cs.CV, cs.AI, cs.LG

<details>
<summary><b>摘要 (Abstract)</b></summary>

Event cameras report asynchronous brightness changes, yet metric-depth supervision is typically available only at sparsely sampled reference timestamps. Aggregating events into frame-like representations simplifies learning but can obscure the fine-grained temporal structure of the original event stream. We introduce EventSSM, a causal spatial-state framework for event-associated metric-depth estimation using a recurrent memory updated by chronological event packets. To bridge asynchronous predictions and frame-time supervision, predicted event depths are geometrically transported using calibrated camera poses and differentiably rendered at reference timestamps. Benchmark evaluations demonstrate improvements over established event-depth methods across multiple depth-error measures. Controlled ablations further examine cross-modal initialization, temporal recurrence, individual state, readout, and supervision components, and packet subdivision. Together, these findings establish EventSSM as an effective framework for causal event-associated metric-depth estimation, combining persistent spatial context with physically timed state retention.

</details>

<details>
<summary><b>中文摘要</b></summary>

Event cameras report asynchronous brightness changes, yet metric-depth supervision is typically available only at sparsely sampled reference timestamps. Aggregating events into frame-like representations simplifies learning but can obscure the fine-grained temporal structure of the original event stream. We introduce EventSSM, a causal spatial-state framework for event-associated metric-depth estimation using a recurrent memory updated by chronological event packets. To bridge asynchronous predictions and frame-time supervision, predicted event depths are geometrically transported using calibrated camera poses and differentiably rendered at reference timestamps. Benchmark evaluations demonstrate improvements over established event-depth methods across multiple depth-error measures. Controlled ablations further examine cross-modal initialization, temporal recurrence, individual state, readout, and supervision components, and packet subdivision. Together, these findings establish EventSSM as an effective framework for causal event-associated metric-depth estimation, combining persistent spatial context with physically timed state retention.

</details>

## 64. Temporal Conditioning for Human Behavioral Cloning in Chess

**中文标题**: Temporal Conditioning for Human Behavioral Cloning in Chess

**作者**: Victor Dziwniel, Tianxiang Lu

**发布日期**: 2026-11-24

**arXiv ID**: [2611.33106v1](http://arxiv.org/abs/2611.33106v1)

**PDF**: [下载链接](http://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.33106v1)

**类别**: cs.AI, cs.HC

<details>
<summary><b>摘要 (Abstract)</b></summary>

Behavioral cloning models in chess typically predict human moves from board states while ignoring temporal inputs such as the remaining clock. However, time pressure is a major determinant of human decision-making quality, and temporal state is a missing dimension of human behavioral modeling. This paper introduces a time-aware transformer that extends a Chessformer-style architecture with learned temporal conditioning tokens encoding player clock state, time control, and recent tempo, together with behavioral heads for thinking time and resignation. The model is trained on large-scale online chess data and evaluated against time-agnostic baselines and the state of the art. Results show that temporal conditioning yields modest overall accuracy improvements but substantially stronger gains under time pressure. Beyond move choice, it also produces more accurate thinking-time and resignation predictions. More importantly, the model better matches human move-quality degradation in critical time regimes, reducing the systematic over-strength observed in time-agnostic behavioral cloning models. These results suggest that temporal state is a previously underutilized but important signal for human behavioral cloning in chess.

</details>

<details>
<summary><b>中文摘要</b></summary>

Behavioral cloning models in chess typically predict human moves from board states while ignoring temporal inputs such as the remaining clock. However, time pressure is a major determinant of human decision-making quality, and temporal state is a missing dimension of human behavioral modeling. This paper introduces a time-aware transformer that extends a Chessformer-style architecture with learned temporal conditioning tokens encoding player clock state, time control, and recent tempo, together with behavioral heads for thinking time and resignation. The model is trained on large-scale online chess data and evaluated against time-agnostic baselines and the state of the art. Results show that temporal conditioning yields modest overall accuracy improvements but substantially stronger gains under time pressure. Beyond move choice, it also produces more accurate thinking-time and resignation predictions. More importantly, the model better matches human move-quality degradation in critical time regimes, reducing the systematic over-strength observed in time-agnostic behavioral cloning models. These results suggest that temporal state is a previously underutilized but important signal for human behavioral cloning in chess.

</details>

## 65. BARE: Body-Aware Reflexive Evasion for Humanoid Robots

**中文标题**: BARE: Body-Aware Reflexive Evasion for Humanoid Robots

**作者**: Zihao Li, Tong Chen, Hua Song, Zitong Ding, Zihao Xu et al.

**发布日期**: 2026-11-24

**arXiv ID**: [2611.32642v1](http://arxiv.org/abs/2611.32642v1)

**PDF**: [下载链接](http://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.32642v1)

**类别**: cs.RO, cs.AI

<details>
<summary><b>摘要 (Abstract)</b></summary>

Dynamic obstacle avoidance is essential for safely deploying humanoid robots in real-world environments, where rapidly approaching objects require immediate evasive responses. Recent advances in reflexive evasion have focused primarily on quadrupeds, which typically rely on large-scale whole-body maneuvers. In contrast, humanoids can exploit their rich body articulation to evade through small, localized body adjustments. By moving threatened body regions without repositioning the entire body, these responses may require less execution time and less surrounding free space. However, local adjustments can alter mass distribution or support conditions, making it challenging to achieve rapid evasion while maintaining whole-body stability. To this end, we propose BARE (Body-Aware Reflexive Evasion), a humanoid reflexive evasion framework guided by human motion priors that enables rapid local evasion while maintaining whole-body stability. A risk-guided motion adapter is designed to select reference motions from a retargeted human evasive motion library based on the threatened body region and to adjust their temporal phases based on collision urgency, allowing the robot to reach evasive postures before potential contact. Conditioned on the adapted reference and link-level collision risk, a reflexive evasion policy learns through reinforcement learning to coordinate local body adjustments with whole-body stabilization. We validate BARE in simulation and real-world experiments, demonstrating its ability to perform targeted local evasion while maintaining whole-body stability.

</details>

<details>
<summary><b>中文摘要</b></summary>

Recent advances in reflexive evasion have focused primarily on quadrupeds, which typically rely on large-scale whole-body maneuvers. In contrast, humanoids can exploit their rich body articulation to evade through small, localized body adjustments. By moving threatened body regions without repositioning the entire body, these responses may require less execution time and less surrounding free space. A risk-guided motion adapter is designed to select reference motions from a retargeted human evasive motion library based on the threatened body region and to adjust their temporal phases based on collision urgency, allowing the robot to reach evasive postures before potential contact. Conditioned on the adapted reference and link-level collision risk, a reflexive evasion policy learns through reinforcement learning to coordinate local body adjustments with whole-body stabilization. We validate BARE in simulation and real-world experiments, demonstrating its ability to perform targeted local evasion while maintaining whole-body stability.

</details>

## 66. ContinualDrive: A Deployment-Anchored, Multi-Axis Benchmark for Continual Updates of Learned Driving Planners

**中文标题**: ContinualDrive: A Deployment-Anchored, Multi-Axis Benchmark for Continual Updates of Learned Driving Planners

**作者**: Zirui Li, Zhiqi Mao, Yangang Ren, Heye Huang, Haohan Yang et al.

**发布日期**: 2026-11-24

**arXiv ID**: [2611.32529v1](http://arxiv.org/abs/2611.32529v1)

**PDF**: [下载链接](http://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.32529v1)

**类别**: cs.RO, cs.AI

<details>
<summary><b>摘要 (Abstract)</b></summary>

Learned driving planners keep being updated after deployment as new logs arrive, yet continual learning evaluations rarely ask whether an update improves on the deployed model, and their verdicts rest on unstated evaluation choices. We present ContinualDrive, a deployment-anchored benchmark for post-deployment continual learning of driving planners. It updates a deployed planner on real-world nuPlan logs one scenario-type group at a time, evaluates the same checkpoints in open and closed loop, makes four measurement axes explicit (evaluation loop, reference checkpoint, temporal aggregation and stream protocol), and anchors a three-valued release verdict to the deployed checkpoint. We study two planners (PlanTF and Diffusion Planner) under full fine-tuning and adapter or LoRA updates, with and without replay or gradient projection, in three task orders. Verdicts can reverse or vanish when one axis changes: open-loop ADE ranks the final PlanTF model above the deployed one in half of its core-suite runs, whereas every closed-loop score ranks it below; backward transfer misses this loss, which on average is already present when each group has just been learned; and regime rankings vary far less across training seeds than across task orders. In the core suite of full and adapter updates, no regime measurably improved on the deployed planner in closed loop; on a panel more than ten times larger every evaluated final checkpoint trails it, and joint training on all groups recovers much of the loss without exceeding its nuPlan closed-loop score. We distill these findings into a reporting contract and will release the code and all checkpoints.

</details>

<details>
<summary><b>中文摘要</b></summary>

Learned driving planners keep being updated after deployment as new logs arrive, yet continual learning evaluations rarely ask whether an update improves on the deployed model, and their verdicts rest on unstated evaluation choices. We present ContinualDrive, a deployment-anchored benchmark for post-deployment continual learning of driving planners. It updates a deployed planner on real-world nuPlan logs one scenario-type group at a time, evaluates the same checkpoints in open and closed loop, makes four measurement axes explicit (evaluation loop, reference checkpoint, temporal aggregation and stream protocol), and anchors a three-valued release verdict to the deployed checkpoint. We distill these findings into a reporting contract and will release the code and all checkpoints.

</details>

## 67. Roboclock: A Unified Benchmark for Time-Aware Robotic Intelligence

**中文标题**: Roboclock: A Unified Benchmark for Time-Aware Robotic Intelligence

**作者**: Yujie Zhao, Anthony L. Hu, Zimeng Huang, Zhijing Wu, Luobin Wang et al.

**发布日期**: 2026-11-24

**arXiv ID**: [2611.32233v1](http://arxiv.org/abs/2611.32233v1)

**PDF**: [下载链接](http://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.32233v1)

**类别**: cs.RO, cs.AI

<details>
<summary><b>摘要 (Abstract)</b></summary>

Time is a fundamental dimension of robotic intelligence: a robot may fail despite physically correct actions if it acts outside a valid time window, responds too slowly to an event, or fails to meet required throughput. Yet existing robotic benchmarks primarily emphasize spatial correctness and logical task completion, largely overlooking the temporal constraints of real-world applications. We present RoboClock, a unified benchmark for time-aware robotic manipulation featuring 60 tasks across five application domains. Mirroring these failure modes, RoboClock structures tasks around three types of temporal constraints: Temporal Window, Event-Reactive, and Throughput. Furthermore, RoboClock incorporates a Latency-Aligned evaluation protocol that continuously advances environment time during model inference and control-interface delays, paired with time-sensitive metrics. Evaluating over 30 methods, we find that the best-performing approach is the trained VLA, yet it achieves only 8.3% temporal success even on Easy-level tasks, with persistent challenges in generalization and transfer. These findings reveal fundamental limitations in current robotic systems and highlight the need to jointly optimize physical competence, temporal reasoning, and execution latency.

</details>

<details>
<summary><b>中文摘要</b></summary>

Time is a fundamental dimension of robotic intelligence: a robot may fail despite physically correct actions if it acts outside a valid time window, responds too slowly to an event, or fails to meet required throughput. Yet existing robotic benchmarks primarily emphasize spatial correctness and logical task completion, largely overlooking the temporal constraints of real-world applications. We present RoboClock, a unified benchmark for time-aware robotic manipulation featuring 60 tasks across five application domains. Mirroring these failure modes, RoboClock structures tasks around three types of temporal constraints: Temporal Window, Event-Reactive, and Throughput. Furthermore, RoboClock incorporates a Latency-Aligned evaluation protocol that continuously advances environment time during model inference and control-interface delays, paired with time-sensitive metrics. Evaluating over 30 methods, we find that the best-performing approach is the trained VLA, yet it achieves only 8.3% temporal success even on Easy-level tasks, with persistent challenges in generalization and transfer. These findings reveal fundamental limitations in current robotic systems and highlight the need to jointly optimize physical competence, temporal reasoning, and execution latency.

</details>

---

## 68. Duo: Towards Spatiotemporal Action Coordination in Bimanual Policies

**中文标题**: Duo: Towards Spatiotemporal Action Coordination in Bimanual Policies

**作者**: Zhimu Zhou, Yuqi Yang, Yixin Lin, Junsen Zhang, Jian Zhao et al.

**发布日期**: 2026-11-24

**arXiv ID**: [2611.32108v1](http://arxiv.org/abs/2611.32108v1)

**PDF**: [下载链接](http://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.32108v1)

**类别**: cs.RO, cs.AI

<details>
<summary><b>摘要 (Abstract)</b></summary>

Bimanual manipulation requires each arm to perform distinct motions while coordinating with the other. Common flow-based robotic policies combine both arms' actions into one token per future timestep, giving attention distinct positions across time but not across arms. We introduce Duo, a simple but effective design that models spatial and temporal dependencies within one shared action head. Specifically, Duo separates both arms into two action tokens per timestep, allowing each arm's motion to interact via cross-arm attention during flow matching. On RoboTwin 2.0, Duo improves the overall success of all four base policies, from 0.45B to 3.62B parameters, with gains of 12.55 and 9.02 percentage points for FLOWER (0.89B) and $π_{0.5}$ (3.62B), respectively. Despite its compact size, FLOWER-Duo achieves the best randomized-generalization, precision, and memory scores on RoboDojo. In real-world tasks, it outperforms $π_{0.5}$ in both success rate and execution speed, while scaling effectively with increasing training data. To provide deeper insight into the design of Duo, we conduct systematic ablations on inter-arm coordination, action grouping, and token granularity, confirming that cross-arm attention drives the improvement while arm-wise interaction offers the best accuracy-compute trade-off.

</details>

<details>
<summary><b>中文摘要</b></summary>

Bimanual manipulation requires each arm to perform distinct motions while coordinating with the other. Common flow-based robotic policies combine both arms' actions into one token per future timestep, giving attention distinct positions across time but not across arms. We introduce Duo, a simple but effective design that models spatial and temporal dependencies within one shared action head. Specifically, Duo separates both arms into two action tokens per timestep, allowing each arm's motion to interact via cross-arm attention during flow matching. On RoboTwin 2.0, Duo improves the overall success of all four base policies, from 0.45B to 3.62B parameters, with gains of 12.55 and 9.02 percentage points for FLOWER (0.89B) and $π_{0.5}$ (3.62B), respectively. Despite its compact size, FLOWER-Duo achieves the best randomized-generalization, precision, and memory scores on RoboDojo. In real-world tasks, it outperforms $π_{0.5}$ in both success rate and execution speed, while scaling effectively with increasing training data. To provide deeper insight into the design of Duo, we conduct systematic ablations on inter-arm coordination, action grouping, and token granularity, confirming that cross-arm attention drives the improvement while arm-wise interaction offers the best accuracy-compute trade-off.

</details>

## 69. From Memory Access to Evidence Sufficiency: Diagnosing and Addressing Demand--Evidence Misalignment

**中文标题**: From Memory Access to Evidence Sufficiency: Diagnosing and Addressing Demand--Evidence Misalignment

**作者**: Shiyao Liu, Yixing Fan, Ruqing Zhang, Xiaohui Yan, Jiafeng Guo et al.

**发布日期**: 2026-11-24

**arXiv ID**: [2611.32088v1](http://arxiv.org/abs/2611.32088v1)

**PDF**: [下载链接](http://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.32088v1)

**类别**: cs.AI

<details>
<summary><b>摘要 (Abstract)</b></summary>

Long-term memory retrieval often returns query-relevant records, but relevance does not guarantee sufficient evidence: answering requires a set of records that jointly satisfies a question's factual, relational, temporal, and state constraints. We study this demand--evidence misalignment through MemPath, an interventional framework that diagnoses failures in memory access, demand coverage, and evidence utilization. Guided by this diagnosis, we propose BiDE-Mem, which represents questions as typed demands and uses them to guide retrieval and evidence organization. We formalize demand--evidence support; in the evaluated compact path, it is conveyed through provenance-aware evidence organization and Reader guidance rather than a separate generative four-state verification step. On LoCoMo, BiDE-Mem improves accuracy by 6.23 percentage points over Mnemis; on LongMemEval, it achieves 91.80%, with strong multi-hop and temporal performance. Supplementary Qwen-Reader evaluations place it first among seven systems on LoCoMo and LongMemEval-S under two judging protocols. Ablations and evidence-level analyses show that demand representation, retrieval, and evidence presentation each contribute to answer quality.

</details>

<details>
<summary><b>中文摘要</b></summary>

Long-term memory retrieval often returns query-relevant records, but relevance does not guarantee sufficient evidence: answering requires a set of records that jointly satisfies a question's factual, relational, temporal, and state constraints. We study this demand--evidence misalignment through MemPath, an interventional framework that diagnoses failures in memory access, demand coverage, and evidence utilization. Guided by this diagnosis, we propose BiDE-Mem, which represents questions as typed demands and uses them to guide retrieval and evidence organization. We formalize demand--evidence support; in the evaluated compact path, it is conveyed through provenance-aware evidence organization and Reader guidance rather than a separate generative four-state verification step. On LoCoMo, BiDE-Mem improves accuracy by 6.23 percentage points over Mnemis; on LongMemEval, it achieves 91.80%, with strong multi-hop and temporal performance. Supplementary Qwen-Reader evaluations place it first among seven systems on LoCoMo and LongMemEval-S under two judging protocols. Ablations and evidence-level analyses show that demand representation, retrieval, and evidence presentation each contribute to answer quality.

</details>

## 70. EffectWAM: Learning Action-Grounded Effects for Robot Manipulation

**中文标题**: EffectWAM: Learning Action-Grounded Effects for Robot Manipulation

**作者**: Yuan Zhang, Hao Cui, Jingfei Ni, Meiyi Wang, Xin Nie et al.

**发布日期**: 2026-11-24

**arXiv ID**: [2611.32053v1](http://arxiv.org/abs/2611.32053v1)

**PDF**: [下载链接](http://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.32053v1)

**类别**: cs.AI

<details>
<summary><b>摘要 (Abstract)</b></summary>

World-action models connect visual prediction with executable robot control. We present EffectWAM, which develops this connection through three complementary components: Effect, camera positional encoding (CaPE), and Action Grounding. Effect represents visual change as the difference between independently encoded future observations and a shared current-observation anchor. CaPE conditions endpoint pooling on relative camera pose when reliable extrinsics are available, while sharing the same learned parameters with pose-free pooling for uncalibrated trajectories. Action Grounding trains the visual representation to retain measured changes in end-effector pose and gripper state. An exponential moving average of the observation encoder supplies Effect targets. A shared generation expert learns to jointly predict Effect and an executable action chunk through flow matching, and reuses the sampled pair to generate future video on demand. This allows the controller to execute actions without waiting for video synthesis. We train on more than 20,000 hours of heterogeneous embodied data, excluding unused chain-of-thought data, and accommodate dataset-specific joint, end-effector, and hand-pose representations. Our evaluation uses the Small (8B) configuration on all five benchmarks and additionally evaluates the Large (26B) configuration on RoboDojo. Experiments examine closed-loop manipulation across complementary benchmarks, and comparisons across multiple baseline policies show consistent improvements from Action Grounding.

</details>

<details>
<summary><b>中文摘要</b></summary>

World-action models connect visual prediction with executable robot control. We present EffectWAM, which develops this connection through three complementary components: Effect, camera positional encoding (CaPE), and Action Grounding. Effect represents visual change as the difference between independently encoded future observations and a shared current-observation anchor. CaPE conditions endpoint pooling on relative camera pose when reliable extrinsics are available, while sharing the same learned parameters with pose-free pooling for uncalibrated trajectories. Action Grounding trains the visual representation to retain measured changes in end-effector pose and gripper state. An exponential moving average of the observation encoder supplies Effect targets. A shared generation expert learns to jointly predict Effect and an executable action chunk through flow matching, and reuses the sampled pair to generate future video on demand. This allows the controller to execute actions without waiting for video synthesis. We train on more than 20,000 hours of heterogeneous embodied data, excluding unused chain-of-thought data, and accommodate dataset-specific joint, end-effector, and hand-pose representations. Our evaluation uses the Small (8B) configuration on all five benchmarks and additionally evaluates the Large (26B) configuration on RoboDojo. Experiments examine closed-loop manipulation across complementary benchmarks, and comparisons across multiple baseline policies show consistent improvements from Action Grounding.

</details>

---

## 71. Provenance-Isolated Epistemic Quorums: Execution-Bound Evidence Certification and Plausible-World Safety Gating in Agentic Control Planes

**中文标题**: Provenance-Isolated Epistemic Quorums: Execution-Bound Evidence Certification and Plausible-World Safety Gating in Agentic Control Planes

**作者**: Jun He, Deying Yu

**发布日期**: 2026-11-24

**arXiv ID**: [2611.32032v1](http://arxiv.org/abs/2611.32032v1)

**PDF**: [下载链接](http://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.32032v1)

**类别**: cs.DC, cs.AI, cs.MA

<details>
<summary><b>摘要 (Abstract)</b></summary>

Agreement among large language models is not physical evidence for mutating production infrastructure. Because generative models share pretraining priors and conform to peer rationales during deliberation, voting ensembles correlate errors instead of providing independent fault tolerance; yet attaching rigid deterministic checks to unconstrained agent outputs paralyzes incident remediation. We present Provenance-Isolated Epistemic Quorums (PIEQ), an execution-bound certification layer that keeps every language model strictly outside the trusted computing base. PIEQ unifies assumption-based truth maintenance, physical provenance isolation, control-theoretic plausible-world safety shielding, and linearizable concurrency governance around three distinct obligations: certifying diagnostic preconditions from disjoint physical failure domains, gating candidate mutations across all unrefuted fault worlds while falling back to read-only diagnostic probes under ambiguity, and binding admission certificates to target execution through snapshot barriers, revision checks, authorization freshness, trusted temporal-precondition policies, and capability-tiered enforcement.   Across formal guarantees, automated etcd fault suites, frozen software benchmarks, and live language-model runs, PIEQ achieves zero within-assumption unsafe admissions and zero false-precondition commits, converting occasional agent proof omissions into recoverable false blocks rather than unsafe actions. On a physical PostgreSQL/Patroni cluster, PIEQ gates supervised failovers alongside Patroni and etcd's native split-brain protection and enforces a full Tier E2 contract inside a single serializable PostgreSQL transaction for bounded capacity reservations. All benchmarks are author-labeled, live model snapshots are unpinned, and Tier E2 physical failover promotion as well as multi-host hardware fault isolation remain open work.

</details>

<details>
<summary><b>中文摘要</b></summary>

Agreement among large language models is not physical evidence for mutating production infrastructure. Because generative models share pretraining priors and conform to peer rationales during deliberation, voting ensembles correlate errors instead of providing independent fault tolerance; yet attaching rigid deterministic checks to unconstrained agent outputs paralyzes incident remediation. Across formal guarantees, automated etcd fault suites, frozen software benchmarks, and live language-model runs, PIEQ achieves zero within-assumption unsafe admissions and zero false-precondition commits, converting occasional agent proof omissions into recoverable false blocks rather than unsafe actions. On a physical PostgreSQL/Patroni cluster, PIEQ gates supervised failovers alongside Patroni and etcd's native split-brain protection and enforces a full Tier E2 contract inside a single serializable PostgreSQL transaction for bounded capacity reservations. All benchmarks are author-labeled, live model snapshots are unpinned, and Tier E2 physical failover promotion as well as multi-host hardware fault isolation remain open work.

</details>

## 72. Cheap Memory Costs Future Compute: Co-Designing Persistent KV and GPU Execution for Efficient Streaming Video Diffusion

**中文标题**: Cheap Memory Costs Future Compute: Co-Designing Persistent KV and GPU Execution for Efficient Streaming Video Diffusion

**作者**: Jian Yang, Zhizhuo Kou, Qiyuan Zhu, Han Chen, Sirui Han et al.

**发布日期**: 2026-11-24

**arXiv ID**: [2611.31892v1](http://arxiv.org/abs/2611.31892v1)

**PDF**: [下载链接](http://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.31892v1)

**类别**: cs.AI

<details>
<summary><b>摘要 (Abstract)</b></summary>

Block-causal video diffusion generates long videos block by block, conditioned on a persistent key-value (KV) cache. After each block is denoised, an extra noise-free ($t=0$) forward pass writes its clean KV into the cache; once distillation and speculative early exit make denoising cheap, this clean KV update dominates the runtime. Reusing stale KV shortens the update, but stale memory corrupts the conditioning of later blocks and inflates their denoising steps, so the saved compute reappears downstream. We present SAMR (Step-Aware Memory Reconstruction), a training-free method for frozen causal video DiTs that refreshes only part of the cache and reconstructs the rest without DiT forwards. Our key observation is that, once rotary position phases are removed, motion-aligned historical KV and the in-flight KV a token holds at its exit step are complementary estimates of its clean KV. SAMR debiases in-flight KV with offline-calibrated per-head affine maps in Pre-RoPE space, fuses the two estimates, and reserves true $t=0$ refreshes for tokens flagged by an attention-readout error proxy, all executed by tile-aligned, synchronization-free Triton kernels. On 48-second LongLive-1.3B videos, SAMR reduces DiT latency by 20.9% and principal FLOPs by 30.7% relative to dense KV updates, lowers downstream denoising steps, and improves final-quarter PSNR over token reuse by 1.61 dB (1.14 dB on held-out VidProM-16). The reconstruction also transfers to frozen Self-Forcing-DMD-1.3B: zero-shot, it reduces reuse-induced step inflation ($2.47 \to 2.30$ steps/token) and improves late PSNR by $+0.33$ dB, and a one-time training-free calibration recovers the diagnostic score that zero-shot transfer lowers ($89.62 \to 91.10$).

</details>

<details>
<summary><b>中文摘要</b></summary>

Block-causal video diffusion generates long videos block by block, conditioned on a persistent key-value (KV) cache. After each block is denoised, an extra noise-free ($t=0$) forward pass writes its clean KV into the cache; once distillation and speculative early exit make denoising cheap, this clean KV update dominates the runtime. Reusing stale KV shortens the update, but stale memory corrupts the conditioning of later blocks and inflates their denoising steps, so the saved compute reappears downstream. Our key observation is that, once rotary position phases are removed, motion-aligned historical KV and the in-flight KV a token holds at its exit step are complementary estimates of its clean KV. SAMR debiases in-flight KV with offline-calibrated per-head affine maps in Pre-RoPE space, fuses the two estimates, and reserves true $t=0$ refreshes for tokens flagged by an attention-readout error proxy, all executed by tile-aligned, synchronization-free Triton kernels. On 48-second LongLive-1.3B videos, SAMR reduces DiT latency by 20.9% and principal FLOPs by 30.7% relative to dense KV updates, lowers downstream denoising steps, and improves final-quarter PSNR over token reuse by 1.61 dB (1.14 dB on held-out VidProM-16). </details>
