## 73. HEAR, SEE, SPIKE ONCE: Highly Efficient NeuroAI Temporal Neural Networks for Low-Precision Multimodal Audio-Visual Classification and Online Learning

**中文标题**: HEAR, SEE, SPIKE ONCE: Highly Efficient NeuroAI Temporal Neural Networks for Low-Precision Multimodal Audio-Visual Classification and Online Learning

**作者**: Shanmuga Venkatachalam, Quinn Jacobson, John Paul Shen

**发布日期**: 2026-11-24

**arXiv ID**: [2611.31884v1](http://arxiv.org/abs/2611.31884v1)

**PDF**: [下载链接](http://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.31884v1)

**类别**: cs.AI

<details>
<summary><b>摘要 (Abstract)</b></summary>

Spiking neural networks for multimodal audio-visual classification reach high accuracy by adopting deep-learning machinery: multi-stage convolutional or transformer encoders trained end-to-end with surrogate-gradient backpropagation through time. These prior works typically rely on full-precision (32-bit) parameters and do not treat hardware efficiency as a primary design objective. In this paper, we propose NeuTNN-AV, a much simpler hardware-amenable multimodal substrate built on the recently proposed NeuroAI Temporal Neural Network (NeuTNN) and demonstrate that it can match or outperform state-of-the-art SNN baselines with a fraction of their synapse counts. NeuTNN neurons carry multiple active dendritic segments. Each input line spikes at most once (temporal coding) and every weight is learned via a local reward-modulated spike-timing dependent plasticity (STDP) rule. All weights are 4-bit integers and inference uses only comparisons and additions. On CREMA-D, it reaches 78.6% with 2.34M synapses establishing a new state-of-the-art SNN accuracy, against 77.14% for MISNet-XL (31M parameters). On UrbanSound8K-AV, NeuTNN reaches 98.82% with 1.38M synapses, against 96.85% for SMMT (9.99M parameters) and 98.96% for MISNet-XL (31M parameters). Weight memory is 23-106x smaller than the spiking baselines, and the measured synaptic energy per inference is two to three orders of magnitude lower. Furthermore, the proposed NeuTNN learns CREMA-D in a single pass with 73.8% accuracy and demonstrates rapid online continual learning by adapting to unseen actors from a few dozen clips.

</details>

<details>
<summary><b>中文摘要</b></summary>

Spiking neural networks for multimodal audio-visual classification reach high accuracy by adopting deep-learning machinery: multi-stage convolutional or transformer encoders trained end-to-end with surrogate-gradient backpropagation through time. These prior works typically rely on full-precision (32-bit) parameters and do not treat hardware efficiency as a primary design objective. In this paper, we propose NeuTNN-AV, a much simpler hardware-amenable multimodal substrate built on the recently proposed NeuroAI Temporal Neural Network (NeuTNN) and demonstrate that it can match or outperform state-of-the-art SNN baselines with a fraction of their synapse counts. NeuTNN neurons carry multiple active dendritic segments. Each input line spikes at most once (temporal coding) and every weight is learned via a local reward-modulated spike-timing dependent plasticity (STDP) rule. All weights are 4-bit integers and inference uses only comparisons and additions. On CREMA-D, it reaches 78.6% with 2.34M synapses establishing a new state-of-the-art SNN accuracy, against 77.14% for MISNet-XL (31M parameters). On UrbanSound8K-AV, NeuTNN reaches 98.82% with 1.38M synapses, against 96.85% for SMMT (9.99M parameters) and 98.96% for MISNet-XL (31M parameters). Weight memory is 23-106x smaller than the spiking baselines, and the measured synaptic energy per inference is two to three orders of magnitude lower. Furthermore, the proposed NeuTNN learns CREMA-D in a single pass with 73.8% accuracy and demonstrates rapid online continual learning by adapting to unseen actors from a few dozen clips.

</details>

## 74. POISE: Curvature-Aware Critic Regularization for Smooth Actor-Critic Control

**中文标题**: POISE: Curvature-Aware Critic Regularization for Smooth Actor-Critic Control

**作者**: Jeong Woon Lee, Namho Kim, Hyoseok Hwang

**发布日期**: 2026-11-24

**arXiv ID**: [2611.31835v1](http://arxiv.org/abs/2611.31835v1)

**PDF**: [下载链接](http://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.31835v1)

**类别**: cs.RO, cs.AI

<details>
<summary><b>摘要 (Abstract)</b></summary>

Actor-critic methods achieve high returns in robotic control, yet trained actors exhibit high-frequency action oscillations. Most smoothness methods constrain the actor, whereas PAVE regularizes the critic by separately penalizing action-gradient variation and encouraging negative action curvature. The same gradient change, however, moves the critic's preferred action farther where the critic is flat, and separate penalties do not measure this motion. We characterize this motion through the greedy shift, which approximates greedy-action displacement across state transitions to first order under local concavity. Since displacement alone does not reflect its critic-value consequences, the analysis further shows that the shift's curvature-weighted energy approximates the value lost by retaining the previous greedy action to second order. Based on this relationship, POISE regularizes the curvature-weighted energy along temporal and spatial state displacements. It employs a saddle-free curvature metric and reward-scale-consistent scaling while leaving the actor unchanged. On six continuous control tasks with TD3, POISE reduces the smoothness score on every task by $5.9\times$ on average ($4.5\times$ geometric mean), compared with $2.2\times$ ($1.9\times$) for the strongest baseline, and achieves the lowest score on five tasks. Paired return intervals include zero on four tasks, with a 14% increase on Ant and an 11% decrease on Hopper.

</details>

<details>
<summary><b>中文摘要</b></summary>

Most smoothness methods constrain the actor, whereas PAVE regularizes the critic by separately penalizing action-gradient variation and encouraging negative action curvature. The same gradient change, however, moves the critic's preferred action farther where the critic is flat, and separate penalties do not measure this motion. We characterize this motion through the greedy shift, which approximates greedy-action displacement across state transitions to first order under local concavity. Since displacement alone does not reflect its critic-value consequences, the analysis further shows that the shift's curvature-weighted energy approximates the value lost by retaining the previous greedy action to second order. Based on this relationship, POISE regularizes the curvature-weighted energy along temporal and spatial state displacements. It employs a saddle-free curvature metric and reward-scale-consistent scaling while leaving the actor unchanged. On six continuous control tasks with TD3, POISE reduces the smoothness score on every task by $5.9\times$ on average ($4.5\times$ geometric mean), compared with $2.2\times$ ($1.9\times$) for the strongest baseline, and achieves the lowest score on five tasks. Paired return intervals include zero on four tasks, with a 14% increase on Ant and an 11% decrease on Hopper.

</details>

## 75. A User-Item Aware Encoding Framework for Short Video

**中文标题**: A User-Item Aware Encoding Framework for Short Video

**作者**: Weihui Deng, He Liu, Bin Wang, Xiaocheng Li, Deliang Fu et al.

**发布日期**: 2026-11-24

**arXiv ID**: [2611.32187v1](http://arxiv.org/abs/2611.32187v1)

**PDF**: [下载链接](http://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.32187v1)

**类别**: cs.MM

<details>
<summary><b>摘要 (Abstract)</b></summary>

Traditional encoding methods from the Professional Generated Content (PGC) era face significant challenges due to two unique characteristics of user-generated short videos: massive daily uploads (reaching hundreds of millions) and heterogeneous content-consumer relationships (varying video quality and diverse consumer contexts). To address these challenges, we propose UIAE (User-Item Aware Encoding), a novel multiple bitrate ladder group encoding method comprising three key components: 1) Establishing user-item relationships via rule-based or DNN-based models; 2) Developing local optimization models maximizing Quality of Experience (QoE) for sub-populations based on contextual consumption patterns; 3) Deriving globally optimal encoding strategies through hierarchical model integration. Experimental results demonstrate that the UIAE framework adaptively adjusts encoding outcomes for distinct user groups with varying context profiles, ensuring optimal QoE for end viewers. We have deployed the UIAE method into the online pipeline, handling tens of millions of video transcoding tasks daily.

</details>

<details>
<summary><b>中文摘要</b></summary>

Traditional encoding methods from the Professional Generated Content (PGC) era face significant challenges due to two unique characteristics of user-generated short videos: massive daily uploads (reaching hundreds of millions) and heterogeneous content-consumer relationships (varying video quality and diverse consumer contexts). To address these challenges, we propose UIAE (User-Item Aware Encoding), a novel multiple bitrate ladder group encoding method comprising three key components: 1) Establishing user-item relationships via rule-based or DNN-based models; 2) Developing local optimization models maximizing Quality of Experience (QoE) for sub-populations based on contextual consumption patterns; 3) Deriving globally optimal encoding strategies through hierarchical model integration. Experimental results demonstrate that the UIAE framework adaptively adjusts encoding outcomes for distinct user groups with varying context profiles, ensuring optimal QoE for end viewers. We have deployed the UIAE method into the online pipeline, handling tens of millions of video transcoding tasks daily.

</details>

## 76. DiffGR00T: Execution-Aware Differentiable Training for Humanoid Whole-Body Vision-Language-Action Models

**中文标题**: DiffGR00T: Execution-Aware Differentiable Training for Humanoid Whole-Body Vision-Language-Action Models

**作者**: Sichen Wu, Qian Sun, Mengfan Liu, Weikai Qin, Bike Zhu et al.

**发布日期**: 2026-11-24

**arXiv ID**: [2611.32665v1](http://arxiv.org/abs/2611.32665v1)

**PDF**: [下载链接](http://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.32665v1)

**类别**: cs.RO

<details>
<summary><b>摘要 (Abstract)</b></summary>

Vision-language-action (VLA) models have achieved strong results in arm-centric manipulation, yet humanoid whole-body control (WBC) requires dynamic balance and tightly coupled full-body coordination. Existing hierarchical humanoid VLAs typically decouple motion generation from physical execution, leaving tracking and balance errors unable to directly optimize the VLA generator. We propose DiffGR00T, an end-to-end differentiable training framework that couples GR00T N1.7 with SONIC, a pretrained whole-body behavior foundation model. To enable execution-aware differentiable training, we replace the non-differentiable simulator with a learned, action-conditioned world model, allowing multi-step body-execution losses to backpropagate through the frozen whole-body controller and the flow-matching sampler directly into the VLA action head. On four HumanoidArena simulation tasks, DiffGR00T improves average task success by 12.5 percentage points over the supervised base VLA (59.2% to 71.7%), demonstrating that execution-aware gradients effectively align high-level motion generation with closed-loop whole-body dynamics.

</details>

<details>
<summary><b>中文摘要</b></summary>

Vision-language-action (VLA) models have achieved strong results in arm-centric manipulation, yet humanoid whole-body control (WBC) requires dynamic balance and tightly coupled full-body coordination. Existing hierarchical humanoid VLAs typically decouple motion generation from physical execution, leaving tracking and balance errors unable to directly optimize the VLA generator. We propose DiffGR00T, an end-to-end differentiable training framework that couples GR00T N1.7 with SONIC, a pretrained whole-body behavior foundation model. To enable execution-aware differentiable training, we replace the non-differentiable simulator with a learned, action-conditioned world model, allowing multi-step body-execution losses to backpropagate through the frozen whole-body controller and the flow-matching sampler directly into the VLA action head. On four HumanoidArena simulation tasks, DiffGR00T improves average task success by 12.5 percentage points over the supervised base VLA (59.2% to 71.7%), demonstrating that execution-aware gradients effectively align high-level motion generation with closed-loop whole-body dynamics.

</details>

## 77. PreWAM: Exploring the Design of Latent Future Prediction for World-Action Modeling

**中文标题**: PreWAM: Exploring the Design of Latent Future Prediction for World-Action Modeling

**作者**: Wan Chi, Sen Li, Zihang Cui, Chuqin Huang, Yunlong Wang et al.

**发布日期**: 2026-11-24

**arXiv ID**: [2611.31901v1](http://arxiv.org/abs/2611.31901v1)

**PDF**: [下载链接](http://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.31901v1)

**类别**: cs.RO

<details>
<summary><b>摘要 (Abstract)</b></summary>

World-action models combine action generation with future prediction, allowing visual transitions to serve as supervision for policy learning. Our controlled study of prediction spaces shows that spatially structured semantic features support better overall generalization under visual perturbations and make motion information more readily recoverable from learned predictive representations. Building on these observations, we introduce PreWAM, a world-action model that organizes future supervision around state changes in a prediction space defined by pretrained DINO features. Specifically, Residual World Prediction jointly learns source-relative changes and their resulting future states to emphasize dynamic information while preserving future-state semantics. Temporal-Scale Prediction conditions world queries on temporal distance to capture both short-term local motion and longer-horizon state evolution. These predictive objectives jointly shape a shared vision-language backbone with action supervision during pretraining and downstream policy learning. Experiments across the LIBERO benchmark family, SimplerEnv, RoboTwin 2.0, and real-world robotic manipulation demonstrate strong manipulation performance and improved generalization and robustness under diverse distribution shifts.

</details>

<details>
<summary><b>中文摘要</b></summary>

World-action models combine action generation with future prediction, allowing visual transitions to serve as supervision for policy learning. Our controlled study of prediction spaces shows that spatially structured semantic features support better overall generalization under visual perturbations and make motion information more readily recoverable from learned predictive representations. Building on these observations, we introduce PreWAM, a world-action model that organizes future supervision around state changes in a prediction space defined by pretrained DINO features. Specifically, Residual World Prediction jointly learns source-relative changes and their resulting future states to emphasize dynamic information while preserving future-state semantics. Temporal-Scale Prediction conditions world queries on temporal distance to capture both short-term local motion and longer-horizon state evolution. These predictive objectives jointly shape a shared vision-language backbone with action supervision during pretraining and downstream policy learning. Experiments across the LIBERO benchmark family, SimplerEnv, RoboTwin 2.0, and real-world robotic manipulation demonstrate strong manipulation performance and improved generalization and robustness under diverse distribution shifts.

</details>

## 78. ST-GNN-AAAS: Spatio-Temporal Graph Neural Networks for Anytime Automatic Algorithm Selection in the Traveling Salesman Problem

**中文标题**: ST-GNN-AAAS: Spatio-Temporal Graph Neural Networks for Anytime Automatic Algorithm Selection in the Traveling Salesman Problem

**作者**: Zeyu Zhang, Mustafa Misir

**发布日期**: 2026-11-24

**arXiv ID**: [2611.32602v1](http://arxiv.org/abs/2611.32602v1)

**PDF**: [下载链接](http://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.32602v1)

**类别**: cs.LG

<details>
<summary><b>摘要 (Abstract)</b></summary>

Anytime Automatic Algorithm Selection (AAAS) aims to make solver choices under varying time budgets. The existing AAAS approaches largely rely on static instance characteristics and make limited use of temporal information revealed during solver execution. We propose ST-GNN-AAAS, a Spatio-Temporal Graph Neural Network framework for AAAS in the Traveling Salesman Problem (TSP). The framework represents evolving incumbent tours using time-varying node features, including distances from each node to its neighbors in the current tour, and combines these features with solver performance observations over a short history window. Given this short history, ST-GNN-AAAS predicts solver selections over a longer future horizon. We evaluate the method on a subset of diverse TSP benchmarks from NATIONAL, TNM, TSPLIB, and VLSI, releasing the corresponding solver execution data to support reproducibility. Our model achieves a selection accuracy of 76.59% and closes 46.05% of the average rank gap between the single best solver (SBS) and the virtual best solver (VBS).

</details>

<details>
<summary><b>中文摘要</b></summary>

Anytime Automatic Algorithm Selection (AAAS) aims to make solver choices under varying time budgets. The existing AAAS approaches largely rely on static instance characteristics and make limited use of temporal information revealed during solver execution. We propose ST-GNN-AAAS, a Spatio-Temporal Graph Neural Network framework for AAAS in the Traveling Salesman Problem (TSP). The framework represents evolving incumbent tours using time-varying node features, including distances from each node to its neighbors in the current tour, and combines these features with solver performance observations over a short history window. Given this short history, ST-GNN-AAAS predicts solver selections over a longer future horizon. We evaluate the method on a subset of diverse TSP benchmarks from NATIONAL, TNM, TSPLIB, and VLSI, releasing the corresponding solver execution data to support reproducibility. Our model achieves a selection accuracy of 76.59% and closes 46.05% of the average rank gap between the single best solver (SBS) and the virtual best solver (VBS).

</details>

## 79. Contact Geometry for Generative Models: An Unbalanced Optimal Transport Formulation

**中文标题**: Contact Geometry for Generative Models: An Unbalanced Optimal Transport Formulation

**作者**: Andrea Testa, Søren Hauberg, Andras Gabor Kupcsik, Tamim Asfour, Leonel Rozo

**发布日期**: 2026-11-24

**arXiv ID**: [2611.32547v1](http://arxiv.org/abs/2611.32547v1)

**PDF**: [下载链接](http://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.32547v1)

**类别**: cs.LG

<details>
<summary><b>摘要 (Abstract)</b></summary>

Real-world generative problems in biology, medical imaging, or robotics, rarely come with perfectly paired source and target distributions. While unbalanced Optimal Transport (uOT) provides a robust framework to bridge unpaired datasets via minimum-length paths in probability space, existing formulations based on the Wasserstein-Fisher-Rao (WFR) geometry introduce a structural bias toward high density regions, often triggering mode collapse. We introduce Contact unbalanced Optimal Transport (CuOT), a novel uOT formulation grounded in contact geometry that decouples density transport direction from mass growth to eliminate structural bias. This allows CuOT to adapt sampling steps to the local data structure, naturally reducing step sizes in high-density regions to prevent overshooting and ensuring stable convergence. The result is a stable, scalable path-length minimization solver that consistently outperforms WFR-based methods on biological dynamics reconstruction, unpaired image-to-image translation, and video generation.

</details>

<details>
<summary><b>中文摘要</b></summary>

Real-world generative problems in biology, medical imaging, or robotics, rarely come with perfectly paired source and target distributions. While unbalanced Optimal Transport (uOT) provides a robust framework to bridge unpaired datasets via minimum-length paths in probability space, existing formulations based on the Wasserstein-Fisher-Rao (WFR) geometry introduce a structural bias toward high density regions, often triggering mode collapse. We introduce Contact unbalanced Optimal Transport (CuOT), a novel uOT formulation grounded in contact geometry that decouples density transport direction from mass growth to eliminate structural bias. This allows CuOT to adapt sampling steps to the local data structure, naturally reducing step sizes in high-density regions to prevent overshooting and ensuring stable convergence. The result is a stable, scalable path-length minimization solver that consistently outperforms WFR-based methods on biological dynamics reconstruction, unpaired image-to-image translation, and video generation.

</details>

## 80. Beyond Hard Tokens: Predictive Memory for Multimodal Protein Generation

**中文标题**: Beyond Hard Tokens: Predictive Memory for Multimodal Protein Generation

**作者**: Xiaoliang Shi, Zichen Wang, Zhijian Wei, Zhanpeng Shi, Zhenghong Zhou et al.

**发布日期**: 2026-11-24

**arXiv ID**: [2611.32347v1](http://arxiv.org/abs/2611.32347v1)

**PDF**: [下载链接](http://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.32347v1)

**类别**: cs.LG, q-bio.BM

<details>
<summary><b>摘要 (Abstract)</b></summary>

Multimodal protein language models unify sequence and discrete structural tokens via iterative masked decoding, offering a promising paradigm for joint protein design. However, standard decoders rely on a memoryless mask reset at each iteration: only a sparse subset of newly committed hard tokens is carried forward, while all remaining unresolved sites are reset to an identical [MASK] embedding. In open-ended co-generation where neither modality provides a fixed external anchor, this stateless transition limits single-step refinement depth and deprives both tracks of stable cross-step soft anchors, leading to slow convergence or degenerative repetitive collapse. Observing that uncommitted predictions evolve with pronounced temporal coherence rather than erratic noise, we introduce Predictive Memory (PredMem), a training-free self-conditioning recurrence that carries these soft beliefs across steps. At each step, PredMem summarizes raw logits into a probability-weighted Top-$k$ expectation embedding at every residue and affinely interpolates it into eligible masked inputs on the next step at zero extra denoiser cost. Across DPLM-2, DPLM-2.1, and ESM3, PredMem raises unconditional sequence-structure co-generation success by 3.6, 17.7, and 48.7 percentage points, respectively, while improving the quality-diversity trade-off and suppressing repetitive mode collapse. Controlled diagnostics show that these gains stem primarily from early trajectory stabilization in unanchored cross-modal co-evolution rather than extra compute or simple entropy reduction, establishing PredMem as an efficient inference mechanism for multimodal discrete generation.

</details>

<details>
<summary><b>中文摘要</b></summary>

However, standard decoders rely on a memoryless mask reset at each iteration: only a sparse subset of newly committed hard tokens is carried forward, while all remaining unresolved sites are reset to an identical [MASK] embedding. Observing that uncommitted predictions evolve with pronounced temporal coherence rather than erratic noise, we introduce Predictive Memory (PredMem), a training-free self-conditioning recurrence that carries these soft beliefs across steps. At each step, PredMem summarizes raw logits into a probability-weighted Top-$k$ expectation embedding at every residue and affinely interpolates it into eligible masked inputs on the next step at zero extra denoiser cost. Across DPLM-2, DPLM-2.1, and ESM3, PredMem raises unconditional sequence-structure co-generation success by 3.6, 17.7, and 48.7 percentage points, respectively, while improving the quality-diversity trade-off and suppressing repetitive mode collapse. Controlled diagnostics show that these gains stem primarily from early trajectory stabilization in unanchored cross-modal co-evolution rather than extra compute or simple entropy reduction, establishing PredMem as an efficient inference mechanism for multimodal discrete generation.

</details>

## 81. Scale-Robust and Global-Aware Representation Learning for Multivariate Time Series Anomaly Detection

**中文标题**: Scale-Robust and Global-Aware Representation Learning for Multivariate Time Series Anomaly Detection

**作者**: Gyuwon Lee, Saejoon Park, Won-Seok Hwang, Bogoan Kim, Yunyong Ko

**发布日期**: 2026-11-24

**arXiv ID**: [2611.32033v1](http://arxiv.org/abs/2611.32033v1)

**PDF**: [下载链接](http://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.32033v1)

**类别**: cs.LG

<details>
<summary><b>摘要 (Abstract)</b></summary>

Multivariate time series anomaly detection (MTSAD) is an essential task for identifying abnormal system behaviors and supporting reliable operations across diverse real-world applications. Despite substantial advances in MTSAD, two key challenges in representation learning remain underexplored: (C1) how to learn scale-robust temporal representations that capture anomalies across different temporal scales and (C2) how to learn global-aware relational representations that capture inter-series dependencies by incorporating series-level global characteristics beyond local windows. To address these challenges, we propose STAGER, a novel method that jointly learns two complementary representations: scale-robust temporal and global-aware relational representations. Specifically, STAGER employs (1) a scale-robust temporal encoder that constructs a multi-scale temporal directed acyclic graph (DAG) to learn scale-robust temporal representations and (2) a global-aware relational encoder that incorporates learnable series-level global embeddings into the temporal representations and models inter-series relationships through graph neural networks. Two complementary auxiliary objectives further enhance the distinctiveness of the global embeddings. Extensive experiments on three real-world benchmark datasets demonstrate that STAGER achieves the best average rank among ten evaluated MTSAD methods across three evaluation metrics. Ablation and sensitivity analyses validate the effectiveness of the proposed components and auxiliary objectives, while efficiency evaluations demonstrate that STAGER achieves a favorable trade-off between detection performance and inference time compared with existing methods.

</details>

<details>
<summary><b>中文摘要</b></summary>

Multivariate time series anomaly detection (MTSAD) is an essential task for identifying abnormal system behaviors and supporting reliable operations across diverse real-world applications. Despite substantial advances in MTSAD, two key challenges in representation learning remain underexplored: (C1) how to learn scale-robust temporal representations that capture anomalies across different temporal scales and (C2) how to learn global-aware relational representations that capture inter-series dependencies by incorporating series-level global characteristics beyond local windows. To address these challenges, we propose STAGER, a novel method that jointly learns two complementary representations: scale-robust temporal and global-aware relational representations. Specifically, STAGER employs (1) a scale-robust temporal encoder that constructs a multi-scale temporal directed acyclic graph (DAG) to learn scale-robust temporal representations and (2) a global-aware relational encoder that incorporates learnable series-level global embeddings into the temporal representations and models inter-series relationships through graph neural networks. Two complementary auxiliary objectives further enhance the distinctiveness of the global embeddings. Extensive experiments on three real-world benchmark datasets demonstrate that STAGER achieves the best average rank among ten evaluated MTSAD methods across three evaluation metrics. Ablation and sensitivity analyses validate the effectiveness of the proposed components and auxiliary objectives, while efficiency evaluations demonstrate that STAGER achieves a favorable trade-off between detection performance and inference time compared with existing methods.

</details>

## 82. PFedHASNet: A Personalized Federated Hybrid Analog-Spike Network for Multi-Subject Motor Imagery EEG Decoding

**中文标题**: PFedHASNet: A Personalized Federated Hybrid Analog-Spike Network for Multi-Subject Motor Imagery EEG Decoding

**作者**: Peijia Li, Ruidong Ma, Xianghong Lin, Xiangwen Wang, Guoyong Shi et al.

**发布日期**: 2026-11-24

**arXiv ID**: [2611.32019v1](http://arxiv.org/abs/2611.32019v1)

**PDF**: [下载链接](http://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.32019v1)

**类别**: cs.LG

<details>
<summary><b>摘要 (Abstract)</b></summary>

Motor imagery electroencephalography (MI-EEG) data are often collected and stored separately across subjects or institutions, and EEG acquisition and annotation remain costly. Training models solely on individual subject data limits the use of information from other subjects, whereas centralizing raw EEG recordings raises privacy and data governance concerns. Federated learning (FL) enables collaborative model training across distributed clients without sharing raw EEG data, providing a practical approach to leveraging information from multiple subjects. Accordingly, we propose PFedHASNet, a personalized federated hybrid analog-spike network for MI-EEG decoding. The shared encoder integrates continuous-valued temporal features, leaky integrate-and-fire (LIF) spike responses, and channel-wise log-variance statistics through learnable fusion weights. Client-private residual adapters and classifiers perform subject-specific feature refinement and personalized prediction, respectively. Experiments on held-out trials from participating subjects across four public MI-EEG datasets show that PFedHASNet achieves the highest mean client macro-F1 among 18 model configurations in cross-model comparisons, while maintaining competitive decoding performance with a compact client-side model in comparisons using the same backbone. Ablation studies further examine the contributions of hybrid representations and personalization components to decoding performance.

</details>

<details>
<summary><b>中文摘要</b></summary>

Training models solely on individual subject data limits the use of information from other subjects, whereas centralizing raw EEG recordings raises privacy and data governance concerns. Accordingly, we propose PFedHASNet, a personalized federated hybrid analog-spike network for MI-EEG decoding. Ablation studies further examine the contributions of hybrid representations and personalization components to decoding performance.

</details>

## 83. Rethinking Universal Time Series Foundation Models from Unified Representations to Shared Dynamics (UniTFU)

**中文标题**: Rethinking Universal Time Series Foundation Models from Unified Representations to Shared Dynamics (UniTFU)

**作者**: Shuxin Li, Yingjie Zhou, Le Zhang, Jingyuan Xia, Shengxi Li et al.

**发布日期**: 2026-11-24

**arXiv ID**: [2611.31921v1](http://arxiv.org/abs/2611.31921v1)

**PDF**: [下载链接](http://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.31921v1)

**类别**: cs.LG

<details>
<summary><b>摘要 (Abstract)</b></summary>

Time series foundation models (TSFMs) have attracted increasing attention in cross-domain forecasting. Existing works mainly rely on large-scale pre-training and general modeling paradigms. However, the former heavily relies on the diversity and domain coverage of pre-training data. Meanwhile, the latter learns unified representations across heterogeneous domains without explicitly identifying the temporal regularities shared among them, limiting robust cross-domain generalization. This work explores cross-domain generalization by characterizing commonalities in recurring local dynamics across domains, rather than relying solely on implicitly learned temporal representations. To capture such cross-domain commonalities, we propose Time Series Foundation Units (TFUs) as reusable building blocks discovered from multi-scale temporal segments, each characterizing a recurring local dynamic pattern shared across domains. Building on TFUs, we develop a unified time series foundation model (UniTFU) that explicitly reuses and composes shared temporal regularities across domains for forecasting. Experiments on seven public datasets show that UniTFU substantially outperforms existing TSFMs in cross-domain forecasting and can efficiently adapt to target-domain dynamics by updating its reusable components with limited target data. UniTFU also achieves strong forecasting performance compared with state-of-the-art methods.

</details>

<details>
<summary><b>中文摘要</b></summary>

This work explores cross-domain generalization by characterizing commonalities in recurring local dynamics across domains, rather than relying solely on implicitly learned temporal representations. UniTFU also achieves strong forecasting performance compared with state-of-the-art methods.

</details>

## 84. ReJEPA: Bounded Predictive Regulation for Latent World Models

**中文标题**: ReJEPA: Bounded Predictive Regulation for Latent World Models

**作者**: Fangzhou Lin, Yuping Wang, Shuo Xing, Yang Chen, Yuwen Heng et al.

**发布日期**: 2026-11-24

**arXiv ID**: [2611.31910v1](http://arxiv.org/abs/2611.31910v1)

**PDF**: [下载链接](http://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.31910v1)

**类别**: cs.LG

<details>
<summary><b>摘要 (Abstract)</b></summary>

Latent world models learn representations by predicting future states in an abstract embedding space rather than reconstructing raw pixels. When training across multi-frame sequences, however, not all frames and spatial regions are equally informative or predictable. We argue that forcing a world model to minimize every prediction error may be unnecessary and harmful. Under standard $L_2$ loss, unbounded gradients force the encoder and predictor to overfit noisy or transient frames at the expense of core physical structure, while amplifying gradient conflict with representation regularizers. Conversely, $L_1$ loss bounds large gradients, but its constant gradient magnitude near zero disrupts the local smoothness required for planning. We resolve this tension with \textbf{ReJEPA}, a bounded predictive regulation principle that combines bounded gradients for large errors with smooth quadratic behavior near zero, instantiated simply via the element-wise $\log\cosh$ loss.   As a drop-in training replacement across Temporal Straightening, LeWorldModel, and V-JEPA~2.1, $\log\cosh$ consistently outperforms $L_2$, a curvature-matched quadratic control, $L_1$, and Smooth-$L_1$ baselines without altering architectures or inference procedures. Notably, the gains grow with environmental complexity, scaling from simple navigation to contact-rich manipulation and real-world autonomous driving on NAVSIM. Probing and geometric analyses confirm that relaxing the penalty on extreme errors yields representations that better capture physical states and align with the true planning cost landscape.

</details>

<details>
<summary><b>中文摘要</b></summary>

Latent world models learn representations by predicting future states in an abstract embedding space rather than reconstructing raw pixels. When training across multi-frame sequences, however, not all frames and spatial regions are equally informative or predictable. We argue that forcing a world model to minimize every prediction error may be unnecessary and harmful. We resolve this tension with \textbf{ReJEPA}, a bounded predictive regulation principle that combines bounded gradients for large errors with smooth quadratic behavior near zero, instantiated simply via the element-wise $\log\cosh$ loss. Notably, the gains grow with environmental complexity, scaling from simple navigation to contact-rich manipulation and real-world autonomous driving on NAVSIM. Probing and geometric analyses confirm that relaxing the penalty on extreme errors yields representations that better capture physical states and align with the true planning cost landscape.

</details>

## 85. Tokenization and Architecture Jointly Allocate Component Roles in Time Series Transformers

**中文标题**: Tokenization and Architecture Jointly Allocate Component Roles in Time Series Transformers

**作者**: Mingyu Kim, Doguk Kim

**发布日期**: 2026-11-24

**arXiv ID**: [2611.31730v1](http://arxiv.org/abs/2611.31730v1)

**PDF**: [下载链接](http://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.31730v1)

**类别**: cs.LG

<details>
<summary><b>摘要 (Abstract)</b></summary>

Time series transformers have achieved strong forecasting performance, yet it remains unclear which of their components, attention or the feed-forward network (FFN), constructs temporal features, and how tokenization such as patching changes this. We propose a temporal probing framework that uses 15 token-level diagnostic tasks to measure feature construction as the gain in linear decodability of temporal properties from one sub-layer to the next (a matched MLP probe already saturates at the embedding). It targets models whose probed representations are read by a linear forecasting head. Applied to 5 supervised models trained on 8 long-term forecasting benchmarks, it finds that the constructing component is not fixed but allocated by the interaction of tokenization and architecture. Under patch tokenization (PatchTST), the FFN's gain exceeds attention's in probing on all 8 datasets, and its 8-dataset mean is 3.2x attention's. Without attention, the FFN alone reaches nearly the same total gain, both when attention is removed from a trained model and when PatchTST is trained without it, and the retrained model forecasts within a few percent of the full model. In probing, attention dominates under variate tokenization (iTransformer); in CATS, cross-attention is the only input path. In a controlled manipulation of PatchTST, single-scalar tokens (P=1) nearly eliminate the FFN's construction, while one more point per token (P=2) restores it. Our forecasting-side interventions (activation patching, knockout, and training without attention) establish component-level reliance and sufficiency. These results offer a representational reading of prior findings on the gains from patching, the competitiveness of self-attention-free models (TSMixer, PatchMLP), and the distinct role of cross-attention in CATS.

</details>

<details>
<summary><b>中文摘要</b></summary>

</details>

## 86. GeoMVGen: Joint Appearance-Geometry Modeling for Multi-View Video Generation in Robotic Manipulation

**中文标题**: GeoMVGen: Joint Appearance-Geometry Modeling for Multi-View Video Generation in Robotic Manipulation

**作者**: Qiaoqiao Jia, Guile Wu, Dongfeng Bai

**发布日期**: 2026-11-23

**arXiv ID**: [2611.31689v1](http://arxiv.org/abs/2611.31689v1)

**PDF**: [下载链接](http://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.31689v1)

**类别**: cs.CV

<details>
<summary><b>摘要 (Abstract)</b></summary>

Robotic manipulation requires predicting how robot-object interactions evolve over time, encompassing both visual appearance and scene geometry. In multi-camera settings, such predictions need to capture the evolving 3D scene consistently across viewpoints, while supporting controllable generation under different robot actions and camera configurations. Existing video generation models provide strong priors for visual appearance and motion but typically lack explicit geometric modeling. Meanwhile, recent advances in geometry-aware and multi-view generation have explored joint appearance-geometry modeling and cross-view consistency, yet generating temporally coherent appearance and geometry across multiple viewpoints under robotic manipulation conditions remains challenging. In this work, we propose GeoMVGen, a framework for joint multi-view RGB and pointmap video generation in robotic manipulation. Built on a video diffusion transformer, our framework employs modality-specific adaptation modules to capture the distinct characteristics of appearance and geometry, while using cross-modal residual connections to facilitate information exchange between the two modalities. To establish geometric correspondences across viewpoints, we further introduce a cross-view projection mechanism with complementary geometric supervisions, combining pointmap consistency with geometry foundation model-guided feature alignment. These designs enable controllable generation of temporally evolving multi-view observations with coherent appearance and geometrically consistent scene structures. Extensive experiments on 4D video generation dataset comprising simulated and real-world robotic manipulation scenarios demonstrate that GeoMVGen outperforms state-of-the-art methods in video quality, geometric accuracy, and cross-view consistency.

</details>

<details>
<summary><b>中文摘要</b></summary>

Robotic manipulation requires predicting how robot-object interactions evolve over time, encompassing both visual appearance and scene geometry. In multi-camera settings, such predictions need to capture the evolving 3D scene consistently across viewpoints, while supporting controllable generation under different robot actions and camera configurations. Existing video generation models provide strong priors for visual appearance and motion but typically lack explicit geometric modeling. Meanwhile, recent advances in geometry-aware and multi-view generation have explored joint appearance-geometry modeling and cross-view consistency, yet generating temporally coherent appearance and geometry across multiple viewpoints under robotic manipulation conditions remains challenging. In this work, we propose GeoMVGen, a framework for joint multi-view RGB and pointmap video generation in robotic manipulation. To establish geometric correspondences across viewpoints, we further introduce a cross-view projection mechanism with complementary geometric supervisions, combining pointmap consistency with geometry foundation model-guided feature alignment. These designs enable controllable generation of temporally evolving multi-view observations with coherent appearance and geometrically consistent scene structures. Extensive experiments on 4D video generation dataset comprising simulated and real-world robotic manipulation scenarios demonstrate that GeoMVGen outperforms state-of-the-art methods in video quality, geometric accuracy, and cross-view consistency.

</details>

---

## 87. Anchoring VAE Fine-Tuning to Its Latent Diffusion Prior

**中文标题**: Anchoring VAE Fine-Tuning to Its Latent Diffusion Prior

**作者**: Rafael Bischof, Dominik Narnhofer, Julius Erbach, Michael Kraus, Konrad Schindler et al.

**发布日期**: 2026-11-23

**arXiv ID**: [2611.31651v1](http://arxiv.org/abs/2611.31651v1)

**PDF**: [下载链接](http://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.31651v1)

**类别**: cs.CV, cs.LG

<details>
<summary><b>摘要 (Abstract)</b></summary>

Pretrained latent diffusion models are effective priors for transfer learning, from geometry estimation to physics simulation. Existing approaches fine-tune the diffusion backbone but freeze the encoder of the VAE to preserve the latent distribution. However, a frozen encoder may miss salient details in non-RGB inputs and is limited to three channels. We introduce Diffusion Prior Anchoring (DPA), a regularizer applied during VAE fine-tuning that uses a score-distillation update to steer latents toward the high-density manifold on which the pretrained diffusion model denoises accurately. We validate DPA across three image and video models (FLUX 3, LTX-Video, Wan2.2) and four tasks: monocular depth estimation, MRI reconstruction, wind-flow simulation, and surface normal estimation, where DPA lowers the prediction error by 7.5% relative to the frozen VAE, 4.9% to decoder-only fine-tuning, and 4.0% to unanchored VAE fine-tuning. Its gain grows the further the target data lies from the RGB training distribution, from 3.3% on monocular depth estimation to 7.2% on complex-valued MRI. DPA also applies to modified VAEs, including pruned and multi-channel variants. With a four-channel VAE that encodes depth and surface normals, it lowers depth estimation error by 9.3%.

</details>

<details>
<summary><b>中文摘要</b></summary>

Pretrained latent diffusion models are effective priors for transfer learning, from geometry estimation to physics simulation. Existing approaches fine-tune the diffusion backbone but freeze the encoder of the VAE to preserve the latent distribution. However, a frozen encoder may miss salient details in non-RGB inputs and is limited to three channels. We introduce Diffusion Prior Anchoring (DPA), a regularizer applied during VAE fine-tuning that uses a score-distillation update to steer latents toward the high-density manifold on which the pretrained diffusion model denoises accurately. We validate DPA across three image and video models (FLUX 3, LTX-Video, Wan2.2) and four tasks: monocular depth estimation, MRI reconstruction, wind-flow simulation, and surface normal estimation, where DPA lowers the prediction error by 7.5% relative to the frozen VAE, 4.9% to decoder-only fine-tuning, and 4.0% to unanchored VAE fine-tuning. Its gain grows the further the target data lies from the RGB training distribution, from 3.3% on monocular depth estimation to 7.2% on complex-valued MRI. DPA also applies to modified VAEs, including pruned and multi-channel variants. With a four-channel VAE that encodes depth and surface normals, it lowers depth estimation error by 9.3%.

</details>

## 88. AdProducer: Towards Agentic Advertising Video Generation

**中文标题**: AdProducer: Towards Agentic Advertising Video Generation

**作者**: Kunyang Li, Chaoyang Wang, Mubarak Shah, Yuzhang Shang

**发布日期**: 2026-11-23

**arXiv ID**: [2611.31466v1](http://arxiv.org/abs/2611.31466v1)

**PDF**: [下载链接](http://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.31466v1)

**类别**: cs.CV, cs.AI

<details>
<summary><b>摘要 (Abstract)</b></summary>

Recent advances in video generation have made automated advertising production increasingly feasible. However, an effective advertisement must faithfully represent the product and communicate its selling points through compelling visual storytelling, which requires more than visual synthesis: it calls for deeper product understanding and strategic decisions about how to present product value. Crucially, product research and creative design are closely intertwined, as the information gathered about a product determines which features can be faithfully demonstrated and how they should be presented. In this paper, we introduce AdProducer, the first end-to-end agentic framework for advertising video creation, transforming concise creative briefs into finished advertisements. Our multimodal agent autonomously researches products through web and image search and translates its findings into selling-point-driven storyboards that guide a frozen video generator. To achieve this, we develop a dedicated data construction pipeline that yields two training datasets, AdProducer-SFT and AdProducer-RL, and train the agent through supervised fine-tuning followed by reinforcement learning with joint optimization of storyboard- and video-level rewards. We further introduce AdCreation-Bench, comprising 305 advertising briefs across eight domains, with two complementary metrics: PG-Score for product correctness and creative execution, and Ad-Score for overall advertising effectiveness assessed by an independent judge. Experiments demonstrate that AdProducer improves the PG-Score of MiniMax H3 from 17.3 to 51.3 and generalizes to Veo 3.1 without additional training, improving its score from 28.0 to 35.9, with consistent gains in Ad-Score, VideoScore2, and human evaluations. Code, models, and data are released.

</details>

<details>
<summary><b>中文摘要</b></summary>

Recent advances in video generation have made automated advertising production increasingly feasible. However, an effective advertisement must faithfully represent the product and communicate its selling points through compelling visual storytelling, which requires more than visual synthesis: it calls for deeper product understanding and strategic decisions about how to present product value. Crucially, product research and creative design are closely intertwined, as the information gathered about a product determines which features can be faithfully demonstrated and how they should be presented. In this paper, we introduce AdProducer, the first end-to-end agentic framework for advertising video creation, transforming concise creative briefs into finished advertisements. Our multimodal agent autonomously researches products through web and image search and translates its findings into selling-point-driven storyboards that guide a frozen video generator. To achieve this, we develop a dedicated data construction pipeline that yields two training datasets, AdProducer-SFT and AdProducer-RL, and train the agent through supervised fine-tuning followed by reinforcement learning with joint optimization of storyboard- and video-level rewards. We further introduce AdCreation-Bench, comprising 305 advertising briefs across eight domains, with two complementary metrics: PG-Score for product correctness and creative execution, and Ad-Score for overall advertising effectiveness assessed by an independent judge. Experiments demonstrate that AdProducer improves the PG-Score of MiniMax H3 from 17.3 to 51.3 and generalizes to Veo 3.1 without additional training, improving its score from 28.0 to 35.9, with consistent gains in Ad-Score, VideoScore2, and human evaluations. Code, models, and data are released.

</details>

## 89. Lyris: Layout-Guided Long Video Generation with Persistent 3D Memory

**中文标题**: Lyris: Layout-Guided Long Video Generation with Persistent 3D Memory

**作者**: Chen Wang, Jiahui Huang, Riccardo de Lutio, Alex Trevithick, Bryan Chu et al.

**发布日期**: 2026-11-23

**arXiv ID**: [2611.31239v2](http://arxiv.org/abs/2611.31239v2)

**PDF**: [下载链接](http://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.31239v2)

**类别**: cs.CV

<details>
<summary><b>摘要 (Abstract)</b></summary>

Exploring a scene from as little as a single photo requires generating a long, camera-controlled walkthrough that extends far beyond what was captured, yet remains consistent upon return. Memory-based video generation methods preserve what they have already seen, but have no anchor beyond it, where the camera drifts from its path and a text prompt can name objects but not place them. Layout-conditioned methods provide such an anchor and precise control over what appears where, but rely on layouts that are typically authored by hand. Both limitations arise because, beyond what was captured, nothing anchors the video model unless a human authors one. To overcome both, we propose Lyris, a long video generation framework that never leaves the video model without an anchor: a persistent point-cloud memory where the scene has been observed or generated, and a 3D layout that a vision-language model authors where it has not. Our key insight is that the interface between the two models can be surprisingly coarse. A layout of one labeled 3D box per object is simple enough for a vision-language model to author (and for a user to edit), yet expressive enough for a video model to follow once rendered along the camera path. We demonstrate walkthroughs of over three minutes and 50 meters from a single photo, passing through doorways into rooms it never shows. When evaluated on minute-long round trips across four datasets, Lyris outperforms state-of-the-art methods by a wide margin, reducing camera error by 3.8x and improving consistency upon return by 22% on average.

</details>

<details>
<summary><b>中文摘要</b></summary>

Exploring a scene from as little as a single photo requires generating a long, camera-controlled walkthrough that extends far beyond what was captured, yet remains consistent upon return. Memory-based video generation methods preserve what they have already seen, but have no anchor beyond it, where the camera drifts from its path and a text prompt can name objects but not place them. Layout-conditioned methods provide such an anchor and precise control over what appears where, but rely on layouts that are typically authored by hand. Both limitations arise because, beyond what was captured, nothing anchors the video model unless a human authors one. To overcome both, we propose Lyris, a long video generation framework that never leaves the video model without an anchor: a persistent point-cloud memory where the scene has been observed or generated, and a 3D layout that a vision-language model authors where it has not. Our key insight is that the interface between the two models can be surprisingly coarse. A layout of one labeled 3D box per object is simple enough for a vision-language model to author (and for a user to edit), yet expressive enough for a video model to follow once rendered along the camera path. We demonstrate walkthroughs of over three minutes and 50 meters from a single photo, passing through doorways into rooms it never shows. When evaluated on minute-long round trips across four datasets, Lyris outperforms state-of-the-art methods by a wide margin, reducing camera error by 3.8x and improving consistency upon return by 22% on average.

</details>

## 90. Reproducible Social Structure Emerges From Free-Form Interaction Among LLM Agents

**中文标题**: Reproducible Social Structure Emerges From Free-Form Interaction Among LLM Agents

**作者**: Tom Alisch, Sonia Murthy, Robert Hawkins, Felix Sosa, Core Francisco Park

**发布日期**: 2026-11-23

**arXiv ID**: [2611.31543v1](http://arxiv.org/abs/2611.31543v1)

**PDF**: [下载链接](http://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.31543v1)

**类别**: cs.MA, cs.AI

<details>
<summary><b>摘要 (Abstract)</b></summary>

Multi-agent LLM simulations are increasingly used as models of social systems, but most published findings are anecdotal, i.e., drawn from a single run. It is therefore unclear which observed phenomena are robust findings of the simulated system itself, and which were random events in one particular run. Here, we introduce a repeatable, goal-free, proactive multi-agent framework in which fifty persona-conditioned agents with persistent memory choose whether, with whom, and how to communicate across three social platforms. The framework supports non-perturbative measurements across temporal snapshots and repetitions. Running the same world ten times from identical initial conditions for a total of 700 hours, we find that most measured network- and agent-level parameters are stable across repetitions. Throughout a run, initially broad communication contracts into sparse sustained dyads in all ten worlds. Importantly, we find that many identical dyads re-form across worlds significantly more often than expected by chance or from agent-specific sociability alone. Static personality similarity largely fails to predict these dyads. Instead, alignment and extremity in the emotions and traits expressed during first contact predict whether a pair later forms a sustained dyad. Additionally, individual agents' emotion and trait expression in their chain-of-thought predicts their future behavior. Finally, swapping either persona prompts or accumulated memories between agents causes recipients to exhibit behavior more similar to donors. Both kinds of transplant move the recipients about 60% of the way toward its donor personality, and state-transplanted agents start behaving similar to their donor agent from their next turn on. Overall, we show that repeated worlds enable robust, statistically-grounded conclusions about emergent within-configuration behavior in agent societies.

</details>

<details>
<summary><b>中文摘要</b></summary>

Multi-agent LLM simulations are increasingly used as models of social systems, but most published findings are anecdotal, i.e., drawn from a single run. It is therefore unclear which observed phenomena are robust findings of the simulated system itself, and which were random events in one particular run. The framework supports non-perturbative measurements across temporal snapshots and repetitions. </details>

## 91. XRL4T1D: A Post-hoc Explainability Framework for Reinforcement Learning in Type 1 Diabetes Management

**中文标题**: XRL4T1D: A Post-hoc Explainability Framework for Reinforcement Learning in Type 1 Diabetes Management

**作者**: Malay Phadke, Chirath Hettiarachchi

**发布日期**: 2026-11-23

**arXiv ID**: [2611.31498v1](http://arxiv.org/abs/2611.31498v1)

**PDF**: [下载链接](http://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.31498v1)

**类别**: cs.AI

<details>
<summary><b>摘要 (Abstract)</b></summary>

Type 1 diabetes (T1D) requires lifelong insulin therapy, and reinforcement learning (RL) has shown promise for automated dosing in the UVA/Padova simulator. However, the decisions of RL controllers remain difficult to interpret. We present XRL4T1D, a post-hoc explainability framework for evaluating pretrained insulin-dosing policies without retraining. Each policy uses 60 minutes of continuous glucose monitor (CGM) and administered-insulin history, sampled every 5 minutes, to recommend insulin. We use SHapley Additive exPlanations (SHAP) to compare the importance of CGM and insulin-history inputs and summarize insulin-history attributions over three 20-minute windows motivated by insulin-on-board. We assess whether these attributions correspond to policy-output sensitivity through targeted input replacement and compare temporal rankings with TimeSHAP. In all 80 patient-policy-mask-size cases, replacing the highest-attribution inputs changes the normalized insulin recommendation more than replacing the lowest-attribution or random inputs. The highest-attribution insulin-history window is also the most output-sensitive in 115 of 120 patient-policy-scenario configurations, and KernelSHAP and TimeSHAP select the same top window in 111 of 120. Applying XRL4T1D to PPO and G2P2C across 20 virtual patients, we find that both controllers rely predominantly on CGM history, especially G2P2C. However, the distribution of insulin-history importance across recent, middle, and older windows varies by cohort, explainer, and analysis settings, without a consistent separation between controllers. Global feature-attribution rankings remain stable under the tested observation corruptions. These results show why attribution fidelity, temporal reproducibility, and controller-comparison robustness should be assessed separately.

</details>

<details>
<summary><b>中文摘要</b></summary>

Type 1 diabetes (T1D) requires lifelong insulin therapy, and reinforcement learning (RL) has shown promise for automated dosing in the UVA/Padova simulator. However, the decisions of RL controllers remain difficult to interpret. We present XRL4T1D, a post-hoc explainability framework for evaluating pretrained insulin-dosing policies without retraining. Each policy uses 60 minutes of continuous glucose monitor (CGM) and administered-insulin history, sampled every 5 minutes, to recommend insulin. We use SHapley Additive exPlanations (SHAP) to compare the importance of CGM and insulin-history inputs and summarize insulin-history attributions over three 20-minute windows motivated by insulin-on-board. We assess whether these attributions correspond to policy-output sensitivity through targeted input replacement and compare temporal rankings with TimeSHAP. In all 80 patient-policy-mask-size cases, replacing the highest-attribution inputs changes the normalized insulin recommendation more than replacing the lowest-attribution or random inputs. The highest-attribution insulin-history window is also the most output-sensitive in 115 of 120 patient-policy-scenario configurations, and KernelSHAP and TimeSHAP select the same top window in 111 of 120. Applying XRL4T1D to PPO and G2P2C across 20 virtual patients, we find that both controllers rely predominantly on CGM history, especially G2P2C. However, the distribution of insulin-history importance across recent, middle, and older windows varies by cohort, explainer, and analysis settings, without a consistent separation between controllers. Global feature-attribution rankings remain stable under the tested observation corruptions. These results show why attribution fidelity, temporal reproducibility, and controller-comparison robustness should be assessed separately.

</details>

## 92. Learning Neurovascular Codes for EEG-to-fMRI Synthesis

**中文标题**: Learning Neurovascular Codes for EEG-to-fMRI Synthesis

**作者**: Yiming Sun, Yifan Wang, Aosong Feng, Yijia Ma, Ben Cheng et al.

**发布日期**: 2026-11-23

**arXiv ID**: [2611.31194v1](http://arxiv.org/abs/2611.31194v1)

**PDF**: [下载链接](http://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.31194v1)

**类别**: cs.LG, cs.AI

<details>
<summary><b>摘要 (Abstract)</b></summary>

In this paper, we introduce Neurovascular Codes as shared EEG representations for fMRI generation. We first train a separate EEG encoder to predict each fMRI signal, learning target-specific temporal and spectral features from the same EEG window. After training, we freeze the encoders and project their concatenated hidden features into a shared neurovascular code. Conditioned on this code, stochastic interpolant transforms Gaussian noise into a vector of fMRI signals, allowing different responses to be sampled for the same EEG window. We evaluate our approach on eight public simultaneous EEG-fMRI datasets, including four resting-state and four task-based datasets with 61,646 volume-level pairs. Under within-subject and held-out-subject evaluation, our model improves average Pearson correlation over the strongest encoder baselines by 19.1% and 19.8%, respectively. The corresponding improvements over the strongest generator baselines are 18.8% and 47.9%. Beyond reconstruction accuracy, we also evaluate the generated signals using subject-identification classifiers trained on real fMRI. Our method achieves the highest mean accuracy among the evaluated generators. We hope that our work opens up new opportunities for cross-modal brain modeling.

</details>

<details>
<summary><b>中文摘要</b></summary>

In this paper, we introduce Neurovascular Codes as shared EEG representations for fMRI generation. We first train a separate EEG encoder to predict each fMRI signal, learning target-specific temporal and spectral features from the same EEG window. After training, we freeze the encoders and project their concatenated hidden features into a shared neurovascular code. Conditioned on this code, stochastic interpolant transforms Gaussian noise into a vector of fMRI signals, allowing different responses to be sampled for the same EEG window. We evaluate our approach on eight public simultaneous EEG-fMRI datasets, including four resting-state and four task-based datasets with 61,646 volume-level pairs. Under within-subject and held-out-subject evaluation, our model improves average Pearson correlation over the strongest encoder baselines by 19.1% and 19.8%, respectively. The corresponding improvements over the strongest generator baselines are 18.8% and 47.9%. Beyond reconstruction accuracy, we also evaluate the generated signals using subject-identification classifiers trained on real fMRI. Our method achieves the highest mean accuracy among the evaluated generators. We hope that our work opens up new opportunities for cross-modal brain modeling.

</details>

## 93. Faster, Smaller, and Better-Looking: Fast-GSVC for 2D Gaussian Video Coding

**中文标题**: Faster, Smaller, and Better-Looking: Fast-GSVC for 2D Gaussian Video Coding

**作者**: Longan Wang, Yuang Shi, Chia Qian Tan, Wei Tsang Ooi

**发布日期**: 2026-11-23

**arXiv ID**: [2611.30629v1](http://arxiv.org/abs/2611.30629v1)

**PDF**: [下载链接](http://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.30629v1)

**项目主页**: https://longanwang-cs.github.io/fastgsvc/.

**类别**: cs.MM

<details>
<summary><b>摘要 (Abstract)</b></summary>

2D Gaussians have emerged as a promising explicit representation for videos. However, fitting Gaussians to a video sequence requires substantial computation, resulting in long encoding times. In this work, we present Fast-GSVC, a 2D Gaussian video codec that accelerates encoding while improving rate-distortion performance. We identify the key bottlenecks in 2DGS training and accelerate optimization through improved initialization, smoother yet more aggressive parameter updates, coarse-to-fine progressive Gaussian optimization, and optical-flow guidance for both Gaussian initialization and temporal propagation. We also discovered that the quantization fine-tuning process of the existing codec does not fully exploit temporal coherence between frames, leading to suboptimal compression. To address this, we preserve spatiotemporal correlations to maintain concentrated residuals and introduce temporal-aware quantization for further improved rate-distortion performance. In our experiments on the UVG dataset, Fast-GSVC reduces encoding time by up to 92.5%, corresponding to a 13.4X speedup over GSVC. At comparable PSNR, it reduces file size by up to 58%; at comparable bit-per-pixel, it improves PSNR by up to 2.5 dB. Project page: https://longanwang-cs.github.io/fastgsvc/.

</details>

<details>
<summary><b>中文摘要</b></summary>

2D Gaussians have emerged as a promising explicit representation for videos. However, fitting Gaussians to a video sequence requires substantial computation, resulting in long encoding times. In this work, we present Fast-GSVC, a 2D Gaussian video codec that accelerates encoding while improving rate-distortion performance. We identify the key bottlenecks in 2DGS training and accelerate optimization through improved initialization, smoother yet more aggressive parameter updates, coarse-to-fine progressive Gaussian optimization, and optical-flow guidance for both Gaussian initialization and temporal propagation. We also discovered that the quantization fine-tuning process of the existing codec does not fully exploit temporal coherence between frames, leading to suboptimal compression. To address this, we preserve spatiotemporal correlations to maintain concentrated residuals and introduce temporal-aware quantization for further improved rate-distortion performance. In our experiments on the UVG dataset, Fast-GSVC reduces encoding time by up to 92.5%, corresponding to a 13.4X speedup over GSVC. At comparable PSNR, it reduces file size by up to 58%; at comparable bit-per-pixel, it improves PSNR by up to 2.5 dB. Project page: https://longanwang-cs.github.io/fastgsvc/.

</details>

## 94. RAPID: Codec-Aware Video Decoding for Efficient Long-Video Understanding

**中文标题**: RAPID: Codec-Aware Video Decoding for Efficient Long-Video Understanding

**作者**: Yuna Lee, Yulhwa Kim

**发布日期**: 2026-11-23

**arXiv ID**: [2611.30557v1](http://arxiv.org/abs/2611.30557v1)

**PDF**: [下载链接](http://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.30557v1)

**类别**: cs.CV, cs.MM

<details>
<summary><b>摘要 (Abstract)</b></summary>

Recent vision-language models (VLMs) can process hour-long videos, but the large number of input frames incurs substantial inference overhead. Existing approaches to efficient long-video understanding largely overlook video decoding, which can take longer than LLM prefill. We propose RAPID (Reference- and Access-Point-Informed Decoding), a codec-aware framework that exploits information embedded in compressed bitstreams to reduce both frame decoding and selection costs. RAPID consists of two components. RAPID-Dec exploits reference-frame information and random access points to skip unnecessary frames and decode the retained frames in parallel while preserving exact reconstruction of the target frames. It achieves up to a 3.7x decoding speedup over the fastest baseline on H.264/AVC videos. RAPID-Sel uses keyframes as coarse representatives of video segments, avoiding the dense candidate decoding and scoring required by existing frame selection methods. It achieves comparable accuracy with up to a 3.0x preprocessing speedup. Together, RAPID achieves a 4.9x time to first token (TTFT) speedup on hour-long videos over a state-of-the-art frame selection method using the fastest baseline decoder.

</details>

<details>
<summary><b>中文摘要</b></summary>

Recent vision-language models (VLMs) can process hour-long videos, but the large number of input frames incurs substantial inference overhead. Existing approaches to efficient long-video understanding largely overlook video decoding, which can take longer than LLM prefill. We propose RAPID (Reference- and Access-Point-Informed Decoding), a codec-aware framework that exploits information embedded in compressed bitstreams to reduce both frame decoding and selection costs. RAPID consists of two components. RAPID-Dec exploits reference-frame information and random access points to skip unnecessary frames and decode the retained frames in parallel while preserving exact reconstruction of the target frames. It achieves up to a 3.7x decoding speedup over the fastest baseline on H.264/AVC videos. RAPID-Sel uses keyframes as coarse representatives of video segments, avoiding the dense candidate decoding and scoring required by existing frame selection methods. It achieves comparable accuracy with up to a 3.0x preprocessing speedup. Together, RAPID achieves a 4.9x time to first token (TTFT) speedup on hour-long videos over a state-of-the-art frame selection method using the fastest baseline decoder.

</details>

## 95. A Self-Organizing Swarm Control Model for Underwater Robots Based on Turing-Type Reaction-Diffusion Principles

**中文标题**: A Self-Organizing Swarm Control Model for Underwater Robots Based on Turing-Type Reaction-Diffusion Principles

**作者**: Takeshi Ishida

**发布日期**: 2026-11-23

**arXiv ID**: [2611.31637v1](http://arxiv.org/abs/2611.31637v1)

**PDF**: [下载链接](http://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.31637v1)

**类别**: cs.RO, cs.ET

<details>
<summary><b>摘要 (Abstract)</b></summary>

In underwater environments, Global Positioning System (GPS) signals and high-speed radio communication are unavailable, and severe constraints exist on communication bandwidth and visibility; consequently, applying conventional swarm formation methods, such as the Boids model, which require the relative positions and velocity vectors of neighboring vehicles, is difficult. This study proposes a decentralized swarm control model that operates without a leader or absolute coordinates, relying solely on the number of received signals from two communication channels with different transmission ranges (inner and outer neighborhoods). The proposed model extends a cellular automaton model based on Turing pattern formation principles to continuous space and is grounded in the fundamental concept that swarms form as robots move toward regions of higher potential calculated from the difference in received signal counts. Specifically, stable behavior is determined through temporal averaging of potential values, hysteresis-based state evaluation, stepwise turning based on temporal differences, and vector composition with an external directional command. Two-dimensional simulation results demonstrated that autonomous swarm formation and control of swarm spatial dispersion via a morphological parameter are achievable, confirming robustness in maintaining swarms and high polarization even under communication dropouts where the communication success rate decreases to approximately 0.7.

</details>

<details>
<summary><b>中文摘要</b></summary>

In underwater environments, Global Positioning System (GPS) signals and high-speed radio communication are unavailable, and severe constraints exist on communication bandwidth and visibility; consequently, applying conventional swarm formation methods, such as the Boids model, which require the relative positions and velocity vectors of neighboring vehicles, is difficult. ## 96. Learning from Human Corrections through Temporal Preferences

**中文标题**: Learning from Human Corrections through Temporal Preferences

**作者**: Anjiabei Wang, Tesca Fitzgerald

**发布日期**: 2026-11-23

**arXiv ID**: [2611.31446v1](http://arxiv.org/abs/2611.31446v1)

**PDF**: [下载链接](http://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.31446v1)

**类别**: cs.RO, cs.LG

<details>
<summary><b>摘要 (Abstract)</b></summary>

When people intervene to correct a robot, they provide information not only through how they correct its behavior, but also through when they choose to intervene. Existing approaches to Learning from Corrections focus on comparing the new trajectory to the robot's originally intended behavior, thus underutilizing the context of the robot's behavior before the intervention. In this work, we investigate how both pre- and post-intervention behavior can be translated into preference pairs for learning value functions that approximate a human's latent reward. We construct post-intervention preferences by comparing corrected behavior with the behavior it replaces, and pre-intervention preferences from temporal relationships among states leading up to an intervention. We evaluate the learned value representations through two downstream tasks: goal inference and correction forecasting. Our results show that post-intervention preferences are particularly effective for goal inference, while pre-intervention preferences provide strong correction-forecasting performance. Combining both sources of information performs strongly across both tasks, even when generalizing to participants unseen during training. These findings demonstrate that both when and how humans correct provide complementary information for learning value representations from corrective interactions.

</details>

<details>
<summary><b>中文摘要</b></summary>

Existing approaches to Learning from Corrections focus on comparing the new trajectory to the robot's originally intended behavior, thus underutilizing the context of the robot's behavior before the intervention. ## 97. Wrist-Camera Video Diffusion as Generalizable Robot Policies

**中文标题**: Wrist-Camera Video Diffusion as Generalizable Robot Policies

**作者**: Jingyuan Liu, Yongwei Chen, Yishan Yang, Zexin He, Xingang Pan et al.

**发布日期**: 2026-11-23

**arXiv ID**: [2611.31079v1](http://arxiv.org/abs/2611.31079v1)

**PDF**: [下载链接](http://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.31079v1)

**项目主页**: https://wrist-video-policy.github.io.

**类别**: cs.RO, cs.CV

<details>
<summary><b>摘要 (Abstract)</b></summary>

A generalist manipulation policy should perform new tasks, operate in unfamiliar scenes, and transfer skills across robot embodiments. We present Wrist Video Policy (WVP), a closed-loop manipulation framework that learns how manipulation unfolds through video prediction and recovers executable motion through geometric estimation. Our key idea is to represent robot motion through wrist-camera trajectories, which determine the corresponding end-effector poses through a fixed hand-eye transformation. We pair a video generator that predicts future wrist views with a pose estimator adapted from a pretrained 3D model to recover the corresponding camera trajectories. Model stitching maps the generator's predicted latents directly into the estimator's intermediate feature space, bypassing RGB decoding. This decomposition allows task knowledge to expand through broad human and robot video data while reusing geometric motion estimation across tasks and embodiments. The resulting pose estimator transfers across Franka Panda, PiPER-X, and Galaxea R1 Pro without further fine-tuning. Across tabletop and laundry-room settings, WVP generalizes to tasks ranging from precise grasping and inserting to handling deformable objects, achieving a success rate of over 50% in zero-shot out-of-distribution evaluations. Our project page is at https://wrist-video-policy.github.io.

</details>

We pair a video generator that predicts future wrist views with a pose estimator adapted from a pretrained 3D model to recover the corresponding camera trajectories. Model stitching maps the generator's predicted latents directly into the estimator's intermediate feature space, bypassing RGB decoding. This decomposition allows task knowledge to expand through broad human and robot video data while reusing geometric motion estimation across tasks and embodiments. The resulting pose estimator transfers across Franka Panda, PiPER-X, and Galaxea R1 Pro without further fine-tuning. Across tabletop and laundry-room settings, WVP generalizes to tasks ranging from precise grasping and inserting to handling deformable objects, achieving a success rate of over 50% in zero-shot out-of-distribution evaluations. Our project page is at https://wrist-video-policy.github.io.

</details>

## 98. SurgeCount: A Protocol-Guided Composition Harness for Bimanual Surgical Sponge Counting

**中文标题**: SurgeCount: A Protocol-Guided Composition Harness for Bimanual Surgical Sponge Counting

**作者**: Sung Eun Kim, Xiaoman Zhang, Xucheng Wang, Pranav Rajpurkar

**发布日期**: 2026-11-23

**arXiv ID**: [2611.31037v1](http://arxiv.org/abs/2611.31037v1)

**PDF**: [下载链接](http://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.31037v1)

**类别**: cs.RO

<details>
<summary><b>摘要 (Abstract)</b></summary>

We present SurgeCount, a system for auditable robotic surgical sponge counting that composes learned skills under a written procedural protocol. Retained sponges cause preventable harm, and surgical teams count every sponge before closure to reduce that risk. The robot repeatedly isolates one deformable sponge from a tangled pile and places it in the next numbered counting-tray slot, requiring handling of occluded grasp threads while preserving step order. We train one subtask-conditioned policy on eleven short skills and learn when each skill ends. At each learned boundary, a stateful vision-language model selects the next skill from an operator-authored specification of step order, visual verification, and recovery. This creates temporal commitment to each subtask and enables failures to be recovered by recomposing existing skills rather than using a dedicated recovery policy. We evaluate five methods across 100 real-robot trials using 20 matched layouts. SurgeCount fills 47 of 60 ordered slots and completes 12 of 20 procedures, compared with 27 of 60 and 3 of 20 for the strongest of four end-to-end policies trained on the same data. Replacing the detailed protocol prompt with a minimal prompt reduces performance to 29 of 60 slots and 5 of 20 completed procedures. These results show that long-horizon performance depends not only on visuomotor capacity, but also on how learned skills are committed, verified, and composed. This tabletop proof of concept motivates further evaluation of protocol-guided robotic sponge counting in clinically representative settings.

</details>

<details>
<summary><b>中文摘要</b></summary>

We train one subtask-conditioned policy on eleven short skills and learn when each skill ends. This creates temporal commitment to each subtask and enables failures to be recovered by recomposing existing skills rather than using a dedicated recovery policy. We evaluate five methods across 100 real-robot trials using 20 matched layouts. SurgeCount fills 47 of 60 ordered slots and completes 12 of 20 procedures, compared with 27 of 60 and 3 of 20 for the strongest of four end-to-end policies trained on the same data. Replacing the detailed protocol prompt with a minimal prompt reduces performance to 29 of 60 slots and 5 of 20 completed procedures. These results show that long-horizon performance depends not only on visuomotor capacity, but also on how learned skills are committed, verified, and composed. This tabletop proof of concept motivates further evaluation of protocol-guided robotic sponge counting in clinically representative settings.

</details>
