## 99. World Models as Native Latent Action Models

**中文标题**: World Models as Native Latent Action Models

**作者**: Qinyue Li, Weijie Qiao, Qingyi Tao, Zerui Gong, Zhe Yang et al.

**发布日期**: 2026-11-23

**arXiv ID**: [2611.30898v1](http://arxiv.org/abs/2611.30898v1)

**PDF**: [下载链接](http://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.30898v1)

**类别**: cs.CV, cs.LG, cs.RO

<details>
<summary><b>摘要 (Abstract)</b></summary>

Large and diverse video sources, such as egocentric human video and heterogeneous robot recordings, carry rich dynamics but come either without actions or without a shared action space. Latent action models (LAMs) can recover these dynamics, but they train their inverse- and forward-dynamics models (IDM, FDM) from scratch, trading off pixel reconstruction against latent capacity and accuracy. Pretrained video world models natively generate high-fidelity dynamics, yet represent them densely, entangled across all layers. Viewing the world model as a LAM, we show that these dynamics can be extracted with minimal changes to the model. Native LAM operates one pretrained video diffusion transformer as both IDM and FDM, with learnable dynamics registers as the only causal bridge between the two, and trains on counterfactual temporal pairs that differ in motion alone, so that the registers distill a video's dynamics into a compact native latent action. We further propose a pretraining paradigm for vision-language-action (VLA) policies, Pretraining Actions from World models (PAWlicy), in which Native LAM serves as a dynamics teacher on large-scale video; unlike world action models, it never generates video. Native latent actions outperform existing LAMs on the LARY benchmark in both action classification and control regression, and replay the same motion on new robots, in new scenes, and from human hands to robots, although no transfer objective is used in training. With a 3.8B model, PAWlicy reaches a 93.6% average success rate on RoboTwin 2.0 trained with domain-randomized demonstrations, the highest among VLAs. On RoboDojo, it is competitive on every dimension we evaluate, and its Precision, evaluated by us, is on par with the leading leaderboard entries. On real robots, it improves over the pi_0.5 baseline on both tasks. Both stages use only public data and a publicly released world model.

</details>

<details>
<summary><b>中文摘要</b></summary>

Large and diverse video sources, such as egocentric human video and heterogeneous robot recordings, carry rich dynamics but come either without actions or without a shared action space. Latent action models (LAMs) can recover these dynamics, but they train their inverse- and forward-dynamics models (IDM, FDM) from scratch, trading off pixel reconstruction against latent capacity and accuracy. Viewing the world model as a LAM, we show that these dynamics can be extracted with minimal changes to the model. Native LAM operates one pretrained video diffusion transformer as both IDM and FDM, with learnable dynamics registers as the only causal bridge between the two, and trains on counterfactual temporal pairs that differ in motion alone, so that the registers distill a video's dynamics into a compact native latent action. We further propose a pretraining paradigm for vision-language-action (VLA) policies, Pretraining Actions from World models (PAWlicy), in which Native LAM serves as a dynamics teacher on large-scale video; unlike world action models, it never generates video. Native latent actions outperform existing LAMs on the LARY benchmark in both action classification and control regression, and replay the same motion on new robots, in new scenes, and from human hands to robots, although no transfer objective is used in training. With a 3.8B model, PAWlicy reaches a 93.6% average success rate on RoboTwin 2.0 trained with domain-randomized demonstrations, the highest among VLAs. On RoboDojo, it is competitive on every dimension we evaluate, and its Precision, evaluated by us, is on par with the leading leaderboard entries. On real robots, it improves over the pi_0.5 baseline on both tasks. Both stages use only public data and a publicly released world model.

</details>

## 100. Place-Revisit Consistency Evaluation of Reference Poses

**中文标题**: Place-Revisit Consistency Evaluation of Reference Poses

**作者**: Jianzhu Huai, Yujia Zhang, Grzegorz Jozkow

**发布日期**: 2026-11-23

**arXiv ID**: [2611.30741v1](http://arxiv.org/abs/2611.30741v1)

**PDF**: [下载链接](http://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.30741v1)

**类别**: cs.RO

<details>
<summary><b>摘要 (Abstract)</b></summary>

Reference trajectories are widely used to evaluate odometry and simultaneous localization and mapping systems. However, local inconsistencies in benchmark reference poses can bias such evaluations. This paper presents a place-revisit consistency evaluation method for reference poses in mobile sensing datasets. When a platform revisits a previously observed place after a sufficient temporal gap, the relative pose implied by the reference trajectory should agree with relative poses estimated directly from onboard sensors. We instantiate this idea with lidar scan registration and stereo-image pose estimation, and apply it to the KITTI Odometry and SNAIL Radar. The KITTI analysis identifies several revisit regions where the public reference poses disagree with sensor-derived relative poses, with median translation discrepancies up to 6.60 m. Representative point-cloud overlays further localize these discrepancies and support the use of place revisits as a benchmark-validation tool.

</details>

<details>
<summary><b>中文摘要</b></summary>

Reference trajectories are widely used to evaluate odometry and simultaneous localization and mapping systems. However, local inconsistencies in benchmark reference poses can bias such evaluations. This paper presents a place-revisit consistency evaluation method for reference poses in mobile sensing datasets. When a platform revisits a previously observed place after a sufficient temporal gap, the relative pose implied by the reference trajectory should agree with relative poses estimated directly from onboard sensors. We instantiate this idea with lidar scan registration and stereo-image pose estimation, and apply it to the KITTI Odometry and SNAIL Radar. The KITTI analysis identifies several revisit regions where the public reference poses disagree with sensor-derived relative poses, with median translation discrepancies up to 6.60 m. Representative point-cloud overlays further localize these discrepancies and support the use of place revisits as a benchmark-validation tool.

</details>

## 101. Coordinating Perception and Planning for Test-Time Adaptation in End-to-End Driving under Distribution Shifts

**中文标题**: Coordinating Perception and Planning for Test-Time Adaptation in End-to-End Driving under Distribution Shifts

**作者**: Shiyu Fang, Yiming Cui, Qihang Huang, Zhan Chen, Collister Chua et al.

**发布日期**: 2026-11-23

**arXiv ID**: [2611.30739v1](http://arxiv.org/abs/2611.30739v1)

**PDF**: [下载链接](http://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.30739v1)

**类别**: cs.RO

<details>
<summary><b>摘要 (Abstract)</b></summary>

End-to-end autonomous driving has achieved significant milestones, yet its reliance on the i.i.d. assumption renders it vulnerable to real-world distribution shifts. While Test-Time Adaptation (TTA) offers a promising paradigm for on-the-fly enhancement, its application to driving is hindered by two barriers: (i) multi-task adaptation dependency, where updates to upstream perception also affect downstream planning, while perception confidence alone does not directly assess these downstream effects; and (ii) continuous adaptation instability, where recursive online updates over long horizons cause compounding parameter drift. To address these challenges, we propose P2-TTA, a plug-and-play TTA framework for the continual adaptation of end-to-end driving models. P2-TTA coordinates adaptation through a joint objective combining perception entropy minimization with a retrospective temporal planning consistency objective derived from observed ego motion. To stabilize online updates, we use an EMA teacher as a moving parameter reference and reject candidate updates that increase historical planning loss. Extensive experiments across multiple models demonstrate that, by updating an average of 0.11 per mille of parameters with an average adaptation time of 19.9 ms on different backbones, P2-TTA achieves average recovery rates of 36.4%, 30.0%, and 9.3% for L2, collision rate, and PDMS, respectively, under severe distribution shifts. Under a shared perception-planning objective, P2-TTA achieves the highest L2 and collision recovery among the compared TTA baselines on UniAD.

</details>

<details>
<summary><b>中文摘要</b></summary>

While Test-Time Adaptation (TTA) offers a promising paradigm for on-the-fly enhancement, its application to driving is hindered by two barriers: (i) multi-task adaptation dependency, where updates to upstream perception also affect downstream planning, while perception confidence alone does not directly assess these downstream effects; and (ii) continuous adaptation instability, where recursive online updates over long horizons cause compounding parameter drift. To address these challenges, we propose P2-TTA, a plug-and-play TTA framework for the continual adaptation of end-to-end driving models. P2-TTA coordinates adaptation through a joint objective combining perception entropy minimization with a retrospective temporal planning consistency objective derived from observed ego motion. To stabilize online updates, we use an EMA teacher as a moving parameter reference and reject candidate updates that increase historical planning loss. Extensive experiments across multiple models demonstrate that, by updating an average of 0.11 per mille of parameters with an average adaptation time of 19.9 ms on different backbones, P2-TTA achieves average recovery rates of 36.4%, 30.0%, and 9.3% for L2, collision rate, and PDMS, respectively, under severe distribution shifts. Under a shared perception-planning objective, P2-TTA achieves the highest L2 and collision recovery among the compared TTA baselines on UniAD.

</details>

## 102. VeriTouch: Verified Visuo-Tactile World Models that Improve Robot Policy Learning

**中文标题**: VeriTouch: Verified Visuo-Tactile World Models that Improve Robot Policy Learning

**作者**: Shuxun Wang, Xiaonan Jiang, Zhaoyu Wang, Ziyuan Chu, Xihang Yue et al.

**发布日期**: 2026-11-23

**arXiv ID**: [2611.30572v1](http://arxiv.org/abs/2611.30572v1)

**PDF**: [下载链接](http://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.30572v1)

**类别**: cs.RO, cs.AI

<details>
<summary><b>摘要 (Abstract)</b></summary>

Scaling visuo-tactile policy learning is constrained by the high cost of real-world data collection and the difficulty of building realistic simulations. Generative models offer a scalable source of training data, yet useful demonstrations require plausible tactile feedback, temporal alignment with visual contact, and accurate actions. To this end, we present VeriTouch, a framework for generating and verifying synthetic visuo-tactile trajectories for policy learning. Conditioned on initial images and task instructions, our world model, VeriGen, jointly generates high-fidelity dual-view videos and realistic tactile sequences over complete manipulation trajectories. By integrating a dedicated tactile expert with a pretrained video model, it captures realistic visual details and tactile deformation dynamics through joint visuo-tactile modeling. Our multimodal verifier VeriEval then checks visual quality while focusing on tactile reliability, assessing tactile realism, contact-force plausibility, and temporal alignment between visible contact and tactile signals, thereby directly filtering out unreliable sequences. Finally, our inverse dynamics model, VeriIDM, combines global scene information from both camera views with fine-grained gripper details and motion across neighboring frames to extract actions from verified videos. Experiments on five real-world tasks show that synthetic data improves all four evaluated policies, with further scaling synthetic trajectories substantially boosting policy performance without additional real-world data collection.

</details>

<details>
<summary><b>中文摘要</b></summary>

Scaling visuo-tactile policy learning is constrained by the high cost of real-world data collection and the difficulty of building realistic simulations. Generative models offer a scalable source of training data, yet useful demonstrations require plausible tactile feedback, temporal alignment with visual contact, and accurate actions. To this end, we present VeriTouch, a framework for generating and verifying synthetic visuo-tactile trajectories for policy learning. Conditioned on initial images and task instructions, our world model, VeriGen, jointly generates high-fidelity dual-view videos and realistic tactile sequences over complete manipulation trajectories. By integrating a dedicated tactile expert with a pretrained video model, it captures realistic visual details and tactile deformation dynamics through joint visuo-tactile modeling. Our multimodal verifier VeriEval then checks visual quality while focusing on tactile reliability, assessing tactile realism, contact-force plausibility, and temporal alignment between visible contact and tactile signals, thereby directly filtering out unreliable sequences. Finally, our inverse dynamics model, VeriIDM, combines global scene information from both camera views with fine-grained gripper details and motion across neighboring frames to extract actions from verified videos. Experiments on five real-world tasks show that synthetic data improves all four evaluated policies, with further scaling synthetic trajectories substantially boosting policy performance without additional real-world data collection.

</details>

## 103. SOAR: temporal Structure-guided One-step Action generation for Robotic manipulation

**中文标题**: SOAR: temporal Structure-guided One-step Action generation for Robotic manipulation

**作者**: Di Xin, Mingfei Han, Genming Cui, Yuanxiang Chen, Xiaolong Wu et al.

**发布日期**: 2026-11-23

**arXiv ID**: [2611.30363v1](http://arxiv.org/abs/2611.30363v1)

**PDF**: [下载链接](http://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.30363v1)

**类别**: cs.RO

<details>
<summary><b>摘要 (Abstract)</b></summary>

Efficient robotic manipulation requires temporally coordinated actions with low inference latency. Multi-step diffusion and flow policies progressively refine action trajectories but require costly iterative inference, whereas one-step policies eliminate iterative trajectory refinement to reduce inference cost. We introduce **SOAR**, a structure-guided one-step generative policy that improves action quality while preserving one-step efficiency. SOAR predicts an observation-conditioned discrete cosine transform (DCT) representation of future actions, capturing temporal variations across multiple frequency bands to provide explicit guidance from key temporal structural information. Unlike velocity-parameterized one-step flow policies that recover clean actions indirectly from a predicted velocity field, SOAR directly generates clean action trajectories in action space. This aligns the model output with the executable control target and provides a direct interface for structural calibration. The predicted structure further corrects the generated trajectory according to their spectral discrepancy, without requiring an additional evaluation of the generative backbone. During training, the corresponding MeanFlow velocity is derived from the calibrated clean action and used for consistency learning, while an endpoint objective aligns training with the pure-noise condition used for one-step inference. Across 53 Adroit and Meta-World tasks, SOAR achieves 83.9% overall success with only 8.7 ms inference latency. When applied to the VLA model, π0.5, SOAR further achieves 97.2% on LIBERO and 71.7% on RoboTwin 2.0. Real-world experiments and extensive ablations further validate its effectiveness and efficiency.

</details>

<details>
<summary><b>中文摘要</b></summary>

We introduce **SOAR**, a structure-guided one-step generative policy that improves action quality while preserving one-step efficiency. Unlike velocity-parameterized one-step flow policies that recover clean actions indirectly from a predicted velocity field, SOAR directly generates clean action trajectories in action space. Real-world experiments and extensive ablations further validate its effectiveness and efficiency.

</details>

## 104. STAR: Structure-Aware Temporal Action Recovery for Point Supervised Temporal Action Localization

**中文标题**: STAR: Structure-Aware Temporal Action Recovery for Point Supervised Temporal Action Localization

**作者**: Yawar Rehman, Min Young Kim

**发布日期**: 2026-11-23

**arXiv ID**: [2611.30354v1](http://arxiv.org/abs/2611.30354v1)

**PDF**: [下载链接](http://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.30354v1)

**类别**: cs.CV, cs.RO

<details>
<summary><b>摘要 (Abstract)</b></summary>

Point supervised temporal action localization is becoming the need of time in this era of ever growing and non-stop video data. Existing method mainly rely on snippet level action response to generate pseudo labels. However, these labels often suffer from boundary ambiguity, over extended proposals, and weak temporal structure. To address these issues, we propose STAR, a structure-aware Temporal Action Recovery framework for point supervised temporal action localization. STAR works by enhancing the base S model with temporal difference interaction with attention module to capture forward and backward snippet changes, and introduces the learning of start, core, and end roles to model the internal temporal structure of actions. In addition, STAR further improves the pseudo label generation through All point proposal ranking and Rolebank based structural re-ranking, which suppress neighbor crossing and structurally inconsistent proposals. Finally, temporal action detector to STAR feedback supervision transfers high quality detector prediction back to the STAR model through soft start, core, end, action, and background targets. This helps to further refine the pseudo labels. Extensive experiments on four standard benchmarks shows the effectiveness of STAR. By using I3D features, STAR achieves 67.2% at AVG@0.1:0.7 on THUMOS'14, 58.1% on GTEA, 74.6% on BEOID, and 31.7% at AVG@0.5:0.95 on ActivityNet-1.3, outperforming previous point supervised methods across multiple benchmarks.

</details>

<details>
<summary><b>中文摘要</b></summary>

Point supervised temporal action localization is becoming the need of time in this era of ever growing and non-stop video data. Existing method mainly rely on snippet level action response to generate pseudo labels. However, these labels often suffer from boundary ambiguity, over extended proposals, and weak temporal structure. To address these issues, we propose STAR, a structure-aware Temporal Action Recovery framework for point supervised temporal action localization. STAR works by enhancing the base S model with temporal difference interaction with attention module to capture forward and backward snippet changes, and introduces the learning of start, core, and end roles to model the internal temporal structure of actions. In addition, STAR further improves the pseudo label generation through All point proposal ranking and Rolebank based structural re-ranking, which suppress neighbor crossing and structurally inconsistent proposals. </details>

## 105. Seeing Together, Acting Apart: Shared Environmental Understanding for Multi-Robot Navigation

**中文标题**: Seeing Together, Acting Apart: Shared Environmental Understanding for Multi-Robot Navigation

**作者**: Haihong Hao, Lei Chen, Mingfei Han, Chenglong Yan, Dong An et al.

**发布日期**: 2026-11-23

**arXiv ID**: [2611.30310v1](http://arxiv.org/abs/2611.30310v1)

**PDF**: [下载链接](http://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.30310v1)

**类别**: cs.RO, cs.CV

<details>
<summary><b>摘要 (Abstract)</b></summary>

Multi-robot embodied systems are typically designed to collaborate by coordinating actions, sharing trajectories, or learning joint policies. We ask a complementary question: can collaboration emerge before decision-making, by first building a shared understanding of the environment? We introduce Shared Environmental Understanding, a policy-agnostic representation that aggregates partial egocentric observations from multiple robots into a shared latent world model. This shifts collaboration from the policy space to the representation space, enabling decentralized decision-making with shared environmental context. To this end, we propose Shared Environment Encoding and Retrieval (SEER), a framework that organizes multi-robot observations into a shared memory, retrieves relevant context via spatio-temporal similarity, and encodes it using a predictive latent world model. The resulting representations are injected into downstream navigation agents as tokens. Experimental results demonstrate that SEER captures action-relevant environmental structure and consistently improves navigation performance across both specialized VLN agents and general vision-language model agents, while preserving single-agent capabilities.

</details>

<details>
<summary><b>中文摘要</b></summary>

Multi-robot embodied systems are typically designed to collaborate by coordinating actions, sharing trajectories, or learning joint policies. We ask a complementary question: can collaboration emerge before decision-making, by first building a shared understanding of the environment? We introduce Shared Environmental Understanding, a policy-agnostic representation that aggregates partial egocentric observations from multiple robots into a shared latent world model. This shifts collaboration from the policy space to the representation space, enabling decentralized decision-making with shared environmental context. To this end, we propose Shared Environment Encoding and Retrieval (SEER), a framework that organizes multi-robot observations into a shared memory, retrieves relevant context via spatio-temporal similarity, and encodes it using a predictive latent world model. The resulting representations are injected into downstream navigation agents as tokens. Experimental results demonstrate that SEER captures action-relevant environmental structure and consistently improves navigation performance across both specialized VLN agents and general vision-language model agents, while preserving single-agent capabilities.

</details>

## 106. Skill-Adaptive Noise Scheduling for Diffusion Policies

**中文标题**: Skill-Adaptive Noise Scheduling for Diffusion Policies

**作者**: Woo Kyung Kim, Gwangpyo Yoo, Eunyoung Park, Honguk Woo

**发布日期**: 2026-11-23

**arXiv ID**: [2611.30264v1](http://arxiv.org/abs/2611.30264v1)

**PDF**: [下载链接](http://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.30264v1)

**类别**: cs.RO, cs.LG

<details>
<summary><b>摘要 (Abstract)</b></summary>

Recent advances in diffusion policies have demonstrated strong effectiveness when integrated into hierarchical skill-based learning frameworks, leveraging offline data to capture multimodal and temporally abstracted behaviors. However, prior approaches have rarely considered the diffusion process as a learnable component, adopting fixed noise schedules applied uniformly across skills, regardless of their distinct behavioral contexts. Such a design fundamentally limits the achievable performance, particularly for contact-rich and dexterous manipulation tasks where different skills exhibit distinct couplings between action dimensions. In this paper, we present a skill-adaptive noise diffusion framework (SaND) which introduces a learnable, skill-conditioned noise schedule that modulates the diffusion process per action dimension, adapting the noising dynamics to each skill. To prevent the learned noise schedule from collapsing into trivial solutions, we employ an auxiliary inverse objective that reconstructs the skill embedding from the Legendre coefficients of the derivative of the noise schedule, encouraging the schedules to remain skill-discriminative. This formulation naturally enables adaptive control over the denoising steps for each skill, where noise schedules are clustered in the Legendre coefficient space, and the appropriate number of denoising steps is determined at the point where reconstruction error abruptly increases. At inference, we identify each skill embedding's cluster from its noise schedule and apply the corresponding number of denoising steps, dedicating more compute to skills requiring fine-grained control and less to simpler ones. In simulation and on real robot hardware, SaND outperforms existing skill learning and planning baselines on contact-rich and dexterous manipulation tasks, achieving higher success rates while requiring fewer denoising steps.

</details>

<details>
<summary><b>中文摘要</b></summary>

However, prior approaches have rarely considered the diffusion process as a learnable component, adopting fixed noise schedules applied uniformly across skills, regardless of their distinct behavioral contexts. In this paper, we present a skill-adaptive noise diffusion framework (SaND) which introduces a learnable, skill-conditioned noise schedule that modulates the diffusion process per action dimension, adapting the noising dynamics to each skill. To prevent the learned noise schedule from collapsing into trivial solutions, we employ an auxiliary inverse objective that reconstructs the skill embedding from the Legendre coefficients of the derivative of the noise schedule, encouraging the schedules to remain skill-discriminative. This formulation naturally enables adaptive control over the denoising steps for each skill, where noise schedules are clustered in the Legendre coefficient space, and the appropriate number of denoising steps is determined at the point where reconstruction error abruptly increases. At inference, we identify each skill embedding's cluster from its noise schedule and apply the corresponding number of denoising steps, dedicating more compute to skills requiring fine-grained control and less to simpler ones. In simulation and on real robot hardware, SaND outperforms existing skill learning and planning baselines on contact-rich and dexterous manipulation tasks, achieving higher success rates while requiring fewer denoising steps.

</details>

---

## 107. Calibrating World Action Models: From Adaptive System 2 Takeover to Self-Evolution

**中文标题**: Calibrating World Action Models: From Adaptive System 2 Takeover to Self-Evolution

**作者**: Wenhao Li, Yu Han, Xiu Su, Dehao Wu, Keke Huang et al.

**发布日期**: 2026-11-23

**arXiv ID**: [2611.30188v1](http://arxiv.org/abs/2611.30188v1)

**PDF**: [下载链接](http://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.30188v1)

**类别**: cs.RO

<details>
<summary><b>摘要 (Abstract)</b></summary>

World action models (WAMs) deliver fast visuomotor control, yet degrade sharply in challenging scenes and act blindly when about to fail; conversely, foundation-model-based agents generalize and recover reliably, but incur prohibitive latency and cost when queried at every step. To achieve both WAM (System 1)-level speed and agent (System 2)-level reliability--- we propose \textbf{\oursfull (\ours)}, a framework that calibrates WAMs' confidence--- adaptively triggering System~2 only when necessary, and distilling System~2's knowledge to form an evolving System~1. First, \ours equips a frozen WAM with \textbf{calibrated self-assessment}: a single lightweight judgment token reading internal video--action features is trained via our proposed \textbf{cross-fitted temporal-difference (TD) learning} using only binary rollout success/failure signals. It can predict a \textbf{calibrated success probability} at negligible overhead without affecting action predictions. Second, this calibrated success probability schedules control chunk by chunk---invoking System~2 to resolve bottlenecks when confidence drops, and handing control back to the WAM once confidence recovers, preserving WAM-like speed while approaching System~2 reliability. Third, as System~2 intervenes only when the WAM lacks confidence, its takeover trajectories directly capture the WAM's capability gaps. JiL distills these takeovers into the WAM to repair those gaps so System~1 can operate standalone without System~2 at test time, while our mastered-skill anchoring and dynamic confidence gating prevent degrading existing capabilities.

</details>

<details>
<summary><b>中文摘要</b></summary>

It can predict a \textbf{calibrated success probability} at negligible overhead without affecting action predictions. Second, this calibrated success probability schedules control chunk by chunk---invoking System~2 to resolve bottlenecks when confidence drops, and handing control back to the WAM once confidence recovers, preserving WAM-like speed while approaching System~2 reliability. Third, as System~2 intervenes only when the WAM lacks confidence, its takeover trajectories directly capture the WAM's capability gaps. JiL distills these takeovers into the WAM to repair those gaps so System~1 can operate standalone without System~2 at test time, while our mastered-skill anchoring and dynamic confidence gating prevent degrading existing capabilities.

</details>

## 108. Agent on Guard: Self-Improving Runtime Intervention for Robust Robotic Manipulation

**中文标题**: Agent on Guard: Self-Improving Runtime Intervention for Robust Robotic Manipulation

**作者**: Gengming Cui, Mingfei Han, Jiawei Zhang, Mozheng Liao, Yunkai Guo et al.

**发布日期**: 2026-11-23

**arXiv ID**: [2611.30101v1](http://arxiv.org/abs/2611.30101v1)

**PDF**: [下载链接](http://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.30101v1)

**类别**: cs.RO, cs.CV

<details>
<summary><b>摘要 (Abstract)</b></summary>

Robot foundation policies provide learned execution capabilities, while programming agents can reason about failures and construct recovery behaviors. Combining them effectively requires learning when intervention helps and when the policy can reliably resume. This challenge is inherently temporal: the physical world continues to evolve during reasoning, and a useful recovery may become infeasible before a decision takes effect. We introduce Agent on Guard (AoG), a hierarchical framework that improves collaboration between a frozen base policy and a programming agent through physical experience. An evolving outer planner allocates control as the physical state evolves, aligning decisions with the physical clock with attention to observation freshness, remaining recovery windows, and the time required for reasoning, recovery, and subsequent execution. Coupled inner loops jointly refine intervention timing, recovery skills, and handoff conditions. Rather than judging recovery by local correction alone, AoG uses downstream task progress, failure recurrence, final outcomes, and resource consumption to improve both recovery behaviors and scheduling. This turns temporary agent solutions into reusable collaboration capabilities that complement the frozen policy. On RoboDojo-Sim, AoG achieves a success rate of 50.57% and a task score of 57.26, exceeding the strongest evaluated baseline by 11.22 percentage points and 10.68 points, respectively. It also reduces end-to-end episode latency by 80.4% and online token consumption by 85.1% relative to the GPT-6 Astra agent.

</details>

<details>
<summary><b>中文摘要</b></summary>

Coupled inner loops jointly refine intervention timing, recovery skills, and handoff conditions. Rather than judging recovery by local correction alone, AoG uses downstream task progress, failure recurrence, final outcomes, and resource consumption to improve both recovery behaviors and scheduling. This turns temporary agent solutions into reusable collaboration capabilities that complement the frozen policy. On RoboDojo-Sim, AoG achieves a success rate of 50.57% and a task score of 57.26, exceeding the strongest evaluated baseline by 11.22 percentage points and 10.68 points, respectively. It also reduces end-to-end episode latency by 80.4% and online token consumption by 85.1% relative to the GPT-6 Astra agent.

</details>

---

## 109. MOVER: Planning Beyond Motion

**中文标题**: MOVER: Planning Beyond Motion

**作者**: Xiangyu Li, Yu Jiang, Tianyi Wang, Liang He, Qixing Huang et al.

**发布日期**: 2026-11-23

**arXiv ID**: [2611.30022v1](http://arxiv.org/abs/2611.30022v1)

**PDF**: [下载链接](http://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.30022v1)

**类别**: cs.CV, cs.RO

<details>
<summary><b>摘要 (Abstract)</b></summary>

Residual world models forecast a driving scene through its temporal change, so feature components that stay constant over time vanish from the residual pathway. We ask whether evidence with weak temporal change, such as a waiting pedestrian or a stopped vehicle, can be used more effectively for planning, and introduce MOVER, a complementary event-routing mechanism. A training-time teacher measures how each spatially grounded event query responds when a past frame is removed from shared history and contrasts that sensitivity with the event's pooled temporal residual, prioritizing events that depend on history but receive weak residual evidence. A student predicts the resulting priorities from unperturbed history, so the teacher adds no inference cost. Under fixed total routing mass, one distribution scales a low-rank future bird's-eye-view update and biases trajectory-conditioned event reading. On nuScenes, MOVER reduces ResWorld's UniAD-style average L2 from 0.65 to 0.55 m and its collision rate from 0.23% to 0.12% without direct planner ego status (0.50 m and 0.09% with it), adding 4.1 ms per frame. Matched controls show that the complementary teacher outperforms either signal alone and that the allocation acts mainly during training. Adapted to BridgeDrive-LEAD, MOVER reaches a 96.52 +/- 0.65 driving score over three seeds on the 220-route Bench2Drive closed-loop benchmark, exceeding matched controls in each paired seed by a margin within seed variation.

</details>

<details>
<summary><b>中文摘要</b></summary>

Matched controls show that the complementary teacher outperforms either signal alone and that the allocation acts mainly during training. Adapted to BridgeDrive-LEAD, MOVER reaches a 96.52 +/- 0.65 driving score over three seeds on the 220-route Bench2Drive closed-loop benchmark, exceeding matched controls in each paired seed by a margin within seed variation.

</details>

## 110. Motion Mamba 2.0: Autoregressive Diffusion Mamba for Long-Horizon Humanoid Control

**中文标题**: Motion Mamba 2.0: Autoregressive Diffusion Mamba for Long-Horizon Humanoid Control

**作者**: Yue Huang, Tianshan Zhang, Zeyu Zhang, Jayeon Kang, Akide Liu et al.

**发布日期**: 2026-11-23

**arXiv ID**: [2611.29897v1](http://arxiv.org/abs/2611.29897v1)

**PDF**: [下载链接](http://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.29897v1)

**类别**: cs.RO, cs.CV

<details>
<summary><b>摘要 (Abstract)</b></summary>

Long-horizon, real-time controllable motion generation is essential for interactive animation and humanoid robotics, where an agent must respond to changing instructions and spatial goals while moving. Existing autoregressive diffusion methods generate motion incrementally, but bounded attention caches limit access to distant history, while repeated denoising introduces substantial inference overhead. We present Motion Mamba 2.0, a Mamba-based root-body diffusion architecture that integrates local attention, resumable recurrent states, and persistent content memory (PCM) for long-horizon controllable motion generation. PCM transforms compressed motion history from Mamba recurrent states, together with state changes and text-conditioned features, into dynamically updated content slots accessible to local attention. To enable efficient inference, we further develop an execution scheme tailored to this architecture, combining CUDA Graph replay, fused kernels, and execution-flow simplification while preserving the canonical history update. Evaluations on retargeted HiPHI demonstrate accurate terminal control over rollouts of up to 39.52 s, while short-horizon comparisons on BONES Seed and retargeted HumanML3D show that the model also retains strong spatial control and text-motion matching. On an RTX 4090, the optimized execution reduces median complete-window latency from 1685.35 to 276.14 ms, achieving a median paired speedup of 6.138$\times$ while preserving identical outputs and committed states in the tested rollouts. Moreover, we conduct extensive real-world evaluation on the Unitree G1, demonstrating sequential text-guided action composition, expressive motion, and interactive locomotion through keyboard and mouse control.

</details>

<details>
<summary><b>中文摘要</b></summary>

PCM transforms compressed motion history from Mamba recurrent states, together with state changes and text-conditioned features, into dynamically updated content slots accessible to local attention. To enable efficient inference, we further develop an execution scheme tailored to this architecture, combining CUDA Graph replay, fused kernels, and execution-flow simplification while preserving the canonical history update. Evaluations on retargeted HiPHI demonstrate accurate terminal control over rollouts of up to 39.52 s, while short-horizon comparisons on BONES Seed and retargeted HumanML3D show that the model also retains strong spatial control and text-motion matching. On an RTX 4090, the optimized execution reduces median complete-window latency from 1685.35 to 276.14 ms, achieving a median paired speedup of 6.138$\times$ while preserving identical outputs and committed states in the tested rollouts. Moreover, we conduct extensive real-world evaluation on the Unitree G1, demonstrating sequential text-guided action composition, expressive motion, and interactive locomotion through keyboard and mouse control.

</details>

## 111. SOTS: Signature-based Optimal Transport Selection

**中文标题**: SOTS: Signature-based Optimal Transport Selection

**作者**: Khalid Akhmouch, Eric Jauer

**发布日期**: 2026-11-23

**arXiv ID**: [2611.31525v1](http://arxiv.org/abs/2611.31525v1)

**PDF**: [下载链接](http://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.31525v1)

**类别**: cs.LG, math.PR, stat.ML

<details>
<summary><b>摘要 (Abstract)</b></summary>

Training data selection for machine learning models is a critical bottleneck in industrial applications where expert annotation is costly and time-consuming. Existing coreset selection methods for time series either ignore temporal structure entirely or cannot handle variable-length, irregularly sampled, multi-channel signals, the setting most common in industrial monitoring and testing. We introduce SOTS (Signature-based Optimal Transport Selection), an unsupervised coreset selection framework for irregular multivariate time series (IMTS) classification. Each sample is mapped to a truncated path signature, capturing the joint geometric structure of its channels, and the coreset is selected by greedily minimizing the Sliced Wasserstein distance between the empirical signature distributions of the candidate subset and the full dataset, a criterion for which we establish a convergence guarantee for random coresets. We validate SOTS on a real-world, highly imbalanced industrial I&C testing dataset, where SOTS-Greedy exceeds 95% of full-dataset accuracy using only 30% of the training data, outperforming standard coreset baselines at most budgets. This advantage generalizes to public UEA regular multivariate time series benchmarks specifically when class-discriminative information is purely geometric.

</details>

<details>
<summary><b>中文摘要</b></summary>

We introduce SOTS (Signature-based Optimal Transport Selection), an unsupervised coreset selection framework for irregular multivariate time series (IMTS) classification. Each sample is mapped to a truncated path signature, capturing the joint geometric structure of its channels, and the coreset is selected by greedily minimizing the Sliced Wasserstein distance between the empirical signature distributions of the candidate subset and the full dataset, a criterion for which we establish a convergence guarantee for random coresets. We validate SOTS on a real-world, highly imbalanced industrial I&C testing dataset, where SOTS-Greedy exceeds 95% of full-dataset accuracy using only 30% of the training data, outperforming standard coreset baselines at most budgets. This advantage generalizes to public UEA regular multivariate time series benchmarks specifically when class-discriminative information is purely geometric.

</details>

## 112. Transferable, Time-Parallel Graph Dynamics via Space-Time Factorization

**中文标题**: Transferable, Time-Parallel Graph Dynamics via Space-Time Factorization

**作者**: Wencong Yang, Chaopeng Shen, Abdolmehdi Behroozi

**发布日期**: 2026-11-23

**arXiv ID**: [2611.31479v1](http://arxiv.org/abs/2611.31479v1)

**PDF**: [下载链接](http://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.31479v1)

**类别**: cs.LG, physics.geo-ph

<details>
<summary><b>摘要 (Abstract)</b></summary>

Time-parallel neural operators (FNO and successors) have transformed PDE surrogates on regular grids. Mesh-based operators (MeshGraphNets, Transolver) extend operator learning to irregular geometries, but jointly learning across diverse topologies while retaining non-recurrent temporal evaluation remains challenging. We propose space-time factorization: partition the model into local temporal operators (learned, graph-independent) and a spatial mixer that propagates information across nodes without graph-dependent learned parameters. This structural condition lets the same parameters run on unseen graphs; non-recurrent instances also admit time-parallel evaluation. Accurate zero-shot transfer further depends on controlling three information leaks (graph-dependent weights, graph-specific features, and graph-correlated training distributions) and on suitable task physics and training coverage. The protocol applies to two distinct spatial-mixer families, fixed-spectral and learned-attention. We validate the principle in three domains. For floodwave propagation in river networks, a 27K-parameter model trained on the Yangtze transfers zero-shot to the Mississippi with median NSE 0.998 in boundary-conditioned operational evaluation, training 5.5x faster than a stabilized recurrent baseline. On cross-geometry Navier-Stokes, the three-seed SepFNO mean relative vorticity error on five unseen targets falls by about 28% from 5 to 12 training geometries, with a consistent single-run SepTransolver trend. On cross-city traffic forecasting, zero-shot transfer reaches within 12.9% of an oracle trained on the target city. Across settings, cross-geometry error decreases with both the number of training geometries and their relatedness to the test, at a rate depending on the spatial mixer. This opens the path to pooled-data, foundation-style models for graph dynamics on irregular topologies.

</details>

<details>
<summary><b>中文摘要</b></summary>

Time-parallel neural operators (FNO and successors) have transformed PDE surrogates on regular grids. Mesh-based operators (MeshGraphNets, Transolver) extend operator learning to irregular geometries, but jointly learning across diverse topologies while retaining non-recurrent temporal evaluation remains challenging. This structural condition lets the same parameters run on unseen graphs; non-recurrent instances also admit time-parallel evaluation. Accurate zero-shot transfer further depends on controlling three information leaks (graph-dependent weights, graph-specific features, and graph-correlated training distributions) and on suitable task physics and training coverage. The protocol applies to two distinct spatial-mixer families, fixed-spectral and learned-attention. We validate the principle in three domains. For floodwave propagation in river networks, a 27K-parameter model trained on the Yangtze transfers zero-shot to the Mississippi with median NSE 0.998 in boundary-conditioned operational evaluation, training 5.5x faster than a stabilized recurrent baseline. On cross-geometry Navier-Stokes, the three-seed SepFNO mean relative vorticity error on five unseen targets falls by about 28% from 5 to 12 training geometries, with a consistent single-run SepTransolver trend. On cross-city traffic forecasting, zero-shot transfer reaches within 12.9% of an oracle trained on the target city. Across settings, cross-geometry error decreases with both the number of training geometries and their relatedness to the test, at a rate depending on the spatial mixer. This opens the path to pooled-data, foundation-style models for graph dynamics on irregular topologies.

</details>

## 113. Is 4 Bits Enough for EEG Sleep Staging? A Subject-Level Benchmark of MXFP4, NVFP4 and FP8

**中文标题**: Is 4 Bits Enough for EEG Sleep Staging? A Subject-Level Benchmark of MXFP4, NVFP4 and FP8

**作者**: Emese Batizi, Hossein Valavi

**发布日期**: 2026-11-23

**arXiv ID**: [2611.31436v1](http://arxiv.org/abs/2611.31436v1)

**PDF**: [下载链接](http://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.31436v1)

**类别**: cs.LG

<details>
<summary><b>摘要 (Abstract)</b></summary>

Staging sleep on a wearable device keeps the raw electroencephalogram (EEG) private and saves the energy needed to transmit it. It also requires small, energy-efficient neural networks. We benchmark 13 post-training quantization (PTQ) configurations, from FP16 to the block-scaled 4-bit formats MXFP4 and NVFP4, for five-stage sleep staging from single-channel Fpz-Cz EEG. We use Sleep-EDF-78 (78 subjects), subject-wise cross-validation, three training seeds, value-exact emulation, and per-subject paired statistics. All 8- and 16-bit formats stay within 0.45 percentage points (pp) of FP32 Macro-F1. With 4-bit weights and activations, an epoch-wise CNN loses 1.98 to 3.04 pp, and a temporal-context network loses at most 1.19 pp. A same-encoder ablation shows that temporal context explains little of this gap. At equal block size, NVFP4 outperforms MXFP4. The first layer, which quantizes the raw EEG, is the most sensitive layer in both networks. Keeping the first and last layers in FP8 brings both tested 4-bit formats within 0.48 pp of FP32. This recipe adds at most 2.1% weight storage, needs no retraining, and is equivalent to quantization-aware training (QAT) within an exploratory 1-pp margin. The 4-bit losses fall on the same subjects across architectures (Spearman correlation coefficient = 0.69 to 0.79), and they reduce N3 recall the most. On the nested Sleep-EDF-20 subset, NVFP4 with FP8 first and last layers again stays within 0.25 pp of FP32. Under MXFP4, however, the ranking of the architectures reverses, and FP8 first and last layers do not fully recover two of the three tested models. In our emulation, NVFP4 with FP8 first and last layers thus loses little accuracy without QAT in both cohorts.

</details>

<details>
<summary><b>中文摘要</b></summary>

Staging sleep on a wearable device keeps the raw electroencephalogram (EEG) private and saves the energy needed to transmit it. It also requires small, energy-efficient neural networks. We benchmark 13 post-training quantization (PTQ) configurations, from FP16 to the block-scaled 4-bit formats MXFP4 and NVFP4, for five-stage sleep staging from single-channel Fpz-Cz EEG. We use Sleep-EDF-78 (78 subjects), subject-wise cross-validation, three training seeds, value-exact emulation, and per-subject paired statistics. All 8- and 16-bit formats stay within 0.45 percentage points (pp) of FP32 Macro-F1. With 4-bit weights and activations, an epoch-wise CNN loses 1.98 to 3.04 pp, and a temporal-context network loses at most 1.19 pp. A same-encoder ablation shows that temporal context explains little of this gap. At equal block size, NVFP4 outperforms MXFP4. The first layer, which quantizes the raw EEG, is the most sensitive layer in both networks. Keeping the first and last layers in FP8 brings both tested 4-bit formats within 0.48 pp of FP32. This recipe adds at most 2.1% weight storage, needs no retraining, and is equivalent to quantization-aware training (QAT) within an exploratory 1-pp margin. The 4-bit losses fall on the same subjects across architectures (Spearman correlation coefficient = 0.69 to 0.79), and they reduce N3 recall the most. On the nested Sleep-EDF-20 subset, NVFP4 with FP8 first and last layers again stays within 0.25 pp of FP32. Under MXFP4, however, the ranking of the architectures reverses, and FP8 first and last layers do not fully recover two of the three tested models. In our emulation, NVFP4 with FP8 first and last layers thus loses little accuracy without QAT in both cohorts.

</details>

## 114. EEG Decoding in Context: A Framework for Tuning-Free EEG Foundation Models

**中文标题**: EEG Decoding in Context: A Framework for Tuning-Free EEG Foundation Models

**作者**: Hao-Run Cai, Si-Yang Liu, Han-Jia Ye, Hairong Zheng

**发布日期**: 2026-11-23

**arXiv ID**: [2611.31171v1](http://arxiv.org/abs/2611.31171v1)

**PDF**: [下载链接](http://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.31171v1)

**类别**: cs.LG

<details>
<summary><b>摘要 (Abstract)</b></summary>

Electroencephalography (EEG) decoding is a central route toward brain-computer interfaces for communication, rehabilitation, cognitive assessment, and neurological monitoring. Recent EEG foundation models have substantially advanced reusable signal representations, but their downstream use still typically requires a task-specific decoder, gradient-based adaptation, and validation-driven model selection. We investigate a complementary foundation-model paradigm based on in-context learning: labeled EEG recordings define the decoding task as context, and new recordings are predicted without updating model parameters. We first pair raw EEG features with a pretrained general-purpose in-context predictor. This direct formulation provides a promising starting point, but its performance remains limited and unstable and does not generalize reliably across EEG paradigms. We find that suitable context construction can address these limitations by improving the effective signal-to-noise ratio and exposing temporal and spatial structure. Building on this insight, we introduce a general framework that combines EEG-specific context construction with a frozen in-context predictor and a metric-based context adaptation mechanism, which matches each downstream task to an appropriate context representation without relying on validation data. Across ten datasets spanning seven EEG decoding paradigms, our method achieves the highest mean BAcc among eleven specialized and fine-tuned EEG baselines, without task-specific parameter updates or validation-based model selection. This framework provides a practical path toward tuning-free EEG foundation models in which adaptation is expressed through context rather than parameter updates.

</details>

<details>
<summary><b>中文摘要</b></summary>

Recent EEG foundation models have substantially advanced reusable signal representations, but their downstream use still typically requires a task-specific decoder, gradient-based adaptation, and validation-driven model selection. We investigate a complementary foundation-model paradigm based on in-context learning: labeled EEG recordings define the decoding task as context, and new recordings are predicted without updating model parameters. We first pair raw EEG features with a pretrained general-purpose in-context predictor. This direct formulation provides a promising starting point, but its performance remains limited and unstable and does not generalize reliably across EEG paradigms. Building on this insight, we introduce a general framework that combines EEG-specific context construction with a frozen in-context predictor and a metric-based context adaptation mechanism, which matches each downstream task to an appropriate context representation without relying on validation data. Across ten datasets spanning seven EEG decoding paradigms, our method achieves the highest mean BAcc among eleven specialized and fine-tuned EEG baselines, without task-specific parameter updates or validation-based model selection. This framework provides a practical path toward tuning-free EEG foundation models in which adaptation is expressed through context rather than parameter updates.

</details>

---

## 115. Analyzing and Improving FlowMap Distillation of Autoregressive Video Models

**中文标题**: Analyzing and Improving FlowMap Distillation of Autoregressive Video Models

**作者**: Yong-Hyun Park, Guande He, Joon Hyeok Kim, Hansheng Chen, Kaifan Yu et al.

**发布日期**: 2026-11-23

**arXiv ID**: [2611.31164v1](http://arxiv.org/abs/2611.31164v1)

**PDF**: [下载链接](http://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.31164v1)

**类别**: cs.CV, cs.LG

<details>
<summary><b>摘要 (Abstract)</b></summary>

FlowMap distillation offers a natural route to few-step generative modeling by directly learning a teacher's denoising trajectories. Yet for autoregressive (AR) video generation, existing few-step models still rely on distribution matching distillation (DMD) to remain competitive, sacrificing sample diversity and requiring a large bidirectional teacher. In this work, we analyze what limits standalone AR FlowMap distillation and introduce two complementary guidance mechanisms to close this gap: (1) anti-drifting guidance, which helps the AR teacher correct drifted student rollouts by training an auxiliary model to mimic student drift and using it as a negative guidance branch; and (2) motion-aware guidance, which makes motion easier for a FlowMap student to learn by locating motion patches and selectively applying skip-layer guidance. Empirically, our approach applies to $π$-Flow, MeanFlow, and consistency distillation with 1.3B AR teachers, improving generation quality and reducing drift over 30 s rollouts. Notably, our 4-step $π$-Flow students match DMD students distilled from 14B teachers on VBench while preserving the sample diversity of their AR teachers, demonstrating that pure FlowMap distillation is effective for few-step autoregressive video generation.

</details>

<details>
<summary><b>中文摘要</b></summary>

FlowMap distillation offers a natural route to few-step generative modeling by directly learning a teacher's denoising trajectories. Yet for autoregressive (AR) video generation, existing few-step models still rely on distribution matching distillation (DMD) to remain competitive, sacrificing sample diversity and requiring a large bidirectional teacher. In this work, we analyze what limits standalone AR FlowMap distillation and introduce two complementary guidance mechanisms to close this gap: (1) anti-drifting guidance, which helps the AR teacher correct drifted student rollouts by training an auxiliary model to mimic student drift and using it as a negative guidance branch; and (2) motion-aware guidance, which makes motion easier for a FlowMap student to learn by locating motion patches and selectively applying skip-layer guidance. Empirically, our approach applies to $π$-Flow, MeanFlow, and consistency distillation with 1.3B AR teachers, improving generation quality and reducing drift over 30 s rollouts. Notably, our 4-step $π$-Flow students match DMD students distilled from 14B teachers on VBench while preserving the sample diversity of their AR teachers, demonstrating that pure FlowMap distillation is effective for few-step autoregressive video generation.

</details>

---

## 116. MUNDO: a multiresolution generative framework for temporal downscaling

**中文标题**: MUNDO: a multiresolution generative framework for temporal downscaling

**作者**: Aymeric Delefosse, Manon Tavernier, Julie Keisler, Anastase Charantonis, Dominique Béréziat

**发布日期**: 2026-11-23

**arXiv ID**: [2611.30868v1](http://arxiv.org/abs/2611.30868v1)

**PDF**: [下载链接](http://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.30868v1)

**类别**: cs.LG, physics.ao-ph

<details>
<summary><b>摘要 (Abstract)</b></summary>

Dynamical systems are often observed at coarse temporal resolution through sparse states or temporal aggregates, leaving the underlying fine-scale trajectories unresolved. Recovering these trajectories requires probabilistic models that capture the unresolved variability while remaining consistent with the observations. We present MUNDO (Multiresolution UNified DOwnscaling), a generative framework for temporal downscaling under general linear observation operators. MUNDO introduces a null-space parameterization that enforces exact observation consistency in a single network evaluation, unifying temporal interpolation and disaggregation. To capture complex multiscale dynamics, we leverage reverse Markov learning to factorize the observation operator into a hierarchy of successive coarsenings. Each level models only the degrees of freedom lost between adjacent resolutions. We evaluate MUNDO on two-scale Lorenz-96, four two-dimensional physical systems, and multivariate ERA5 reanalysis over Europe, for both temporal interpolation and disaggregation. Across datasets and tasks, MUNDO generally outperforms flow-based baselines. Single-step MUNDO achieves strong predictive skill, while recursive MUNDO generally improves temporal realism and ensemble calibration, with better spread-skill agreement and predictive coverage.

</details>

<details>
<summary><b>中文摘要</b></summary>

Recovering these trajectories requires probabilistic models that capture the unresolved variability while remaining consistent with the observations. We present MUNDO (Multiresolution UNified DOwnscaling), a generative framework for temporal downscaling under general linear observation operators. MUNDO introduces a null-space parameterization that enforces exact observation consistency in a single network evaluation, unifying temporal interpolation and disaggregation. To capture complex multiscale dynamics, we leverage reverse Markov learning to factorize the observation operator into a hierarchy of successive coarsenings. Each level models only the degrees of freedom lost between adjacent resolutions. We evaluate MUNDO on two-scale Lorenz-96, four two-dimensional physical systems, and multivariate ERA5 reanalysis over Europe, for both temporal interpolation and disaggregation. Across datasets and tasks, MUNDO generally outperforms flow-based baselines. Single-step MUNDO achieves strong predictive skill, while recursive MUNDO generally improves temporal realism and ensemble calibration, with better spread-skill agreement and predictive coverage.

</details>

## 117. MTL-MHOL: Predicting Online Conversions under Delayed Feedback and Data Sparsity

**中文标题**: MTL-MHOL: Predicting Online Conversions under Delayed Feedback and Data Sparsity

**作者**: Mario Tejera Vicente, Eva Hagen, Emma van Breukelen, Quinten van de Vijver, Kathrin Gruber

**发布日期**: 2026-11-23

**arXiv ID**: [2611.30648v1](http://arxiv.org/abs/2611.30648v1)

**PDF**: [下载链接](http://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.30648v1)

**类别**: cs.LG, stat.ML

<details>
<summary><b>摘要 (Abstract)</b></summary>

This paper proposes the model-agnostic Multi-Task Learning Multi-Head Online Learning (MTL-MHOL) framework for conversion rate (CVR) prediction. Existing approaches typically address key challenges in CVR prediction, such as delayed feedback and data sparsity, in isolation or lack flexibility and practical applicability. MTL-MHOL adopts a time bucketing approach to account for delayed feedback and combines it with multi-task learning of an auxiliary task to mitigate data sparsity. We evaluate the framework on proprietary datasets from a private company and on a public dataset from Criteo. MTL-MHOL matches or outperforms all benchmark models in terms of Negative Log-Likelihood (NLL) and Relative Cross Entropy (RCE), and it correctly captures temporal trends in the data using an MLP backbone, while maintaining strong performance with a DeepFM backbone. In particular, MTL-MHOL matches the performance of the advanced delayed-feedback method FSIW, outperforms the entire-space approach ESMM by 21.5% in RCE, and achieves up to 81% lift in RCE compared to the best-performing classical benchmark.

</details>

<details>
<summary><b>中文摘要</b></summary>

Existing approaches typically address key challenges in CVR prediction, such as delayed feedback and data sparsity, in isolation or lack flexibility and practical applicability. MTL-MHOL adopts a time bucketing approach to account for delayed feedback and combines it with multi-task learning of an auxiliary task to mitigate data sparsity. We evaluate the framework on proprietary datasets from a private company and on a public dataset from Criteo. MTL-MHOL matches or outperforms all benchmark models in terms of Negative Log-Likelihood (NLL) and Relative Cross Entropy (RCE), and it correctly captures temporal trends in the data using an MLP backbone, while maintaining strong performance with a DeepFM backbone. In particular, MTL-MHOL matches the performance of the advanced delayed-feedback method FSIW, outperforms the entire-space approach ESMM by 21.5% in RCE, and achieves up to 81% lift in RCE compared to the best-performing classical benchmark.

## 118. Dynamics Forcing: Mitigating Error Accumulation in Causal Video World Models

**中文标题**: Dynamics Forcing: Mitigating Error Accumulation in Causal Video World Models

**作者**: Xin Zhou, Cong Miao

**发布日期**: 2026-11-22

**arXiv ID**: [2611.29839v1](http://arxiv.org/abs/2611.29839v1)

**PDF**: [下载链接](http://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.29839v1)

**类别**: cs.CV, cs.AI, cs.RO

<details>
<summary><b>摘要 (Abstract)</b></summary>

Causal video world models generate block by block from their own previous outputs. Small errors in early blocks are fed back as history and grow until the scene and the robot fall apart. Teacher forcing trains on clean ground-truth history, so the model never sees the history it will actually condition on, and it also finds a shortcut: copying the previous frame instead of predicting how the scene changes. We present Dynamics Forcing, a training scheme that keeps real video as the only supervision and reshapes how history is presented. History is perturbed in three ways: random noising, self-resampling by the model itself, and joint denoising, in which the most recent blocks are still partly noisy when the next block is predicted. On top of this we move history guidance and first-frame anchor guidance from inference into training, so that the model is pushed to use its history and its reference frame rather than ignore them. Dynamics Forcing needs no teacher model, no distillation, and no additional model at inference. Applied to a 5B video model with LoRA on 77 hours of manipulation video on a single GPU, it better matches unseen real continuations and reduces error accumulation.

</details>

<details>
<summary><b>中文摘要</b></summary>

Causal video world models generate block by block from their own previous outputs. Small errors in early blocks are fed back as history and grow until the scene and the robot fall apart. Teacher forcing trains on clean ground-truth history, so the model never sees the history it will actually condition on, and it also finds a shortcut: copying the previous frame instead of predicting how the scene changes. We present Dynamics Forcing, a training scheme that keeps real video as the only supervision and reshapes how history is presented. History is perturbed in three ways: random noising, self-resampling by the model itself, and joint denoising, in which the most recent blocks are still partly noisy when the next block is predicted. On top of this we move history guidance and first-frame anchor guidance from inference into training, so that the model is pushed to use its history and its reference frame rather than ignore them. Dynamics Forcing needs no teacher model, no distillation, and no additional model at inference. Applied to a 5B video model with LoRA on 77 hours of manipulation video on a single GPU, it better matches unseen real continuations and reduces error accumulation.

</details>

## 119. Generating Safety-Critical Driving Scenarios from Role-Typed Signal Temporal Logic Specifications Drafted by Large Language Models

**中文标题**: Generating Safety-Critical Driving Scenarios from Role-Typed Signal Temporal Logic Specifications Drafted by Large Language Models

**作者**: Zifan Zeng, Qunli Zhang, Peng Wang, Shiming Liu, Feng Liu et al.

**发布日期**: 2026-11-22

**arXiv ID**: [2611.29800v1](http://arxiv.org/abs/2611.29800v1)

**PDF**: [下载链接](http://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.29800v1)

**类别**: cs.RO, cs.SE

<details>
<summary><b>摘要 (Abstract)</b></summary>

Safety-critical scenarios are rare in recorded driving and must be generated to test and train motion planners of automated vehicles. In guided diffusion models, each scenario type needs its own weighted objective, separate from the validity check. Optimized together with the attacker, the vehicle under test can cancel the attack or draw it toward itself. We state each scenario as a signal temporal logic specification with clauses typed by role. Every clause names the agent it may change and the agents it may read, and the vehicle under test reads the attacker only from the attack onset. A large language model drafts the specification from a description, and a type checker accepts it. A compiler derives the optimization: it edits a diffusion model's samples by the smallest change that satisfies the specification, each clause moves only its owner, the attacker is solved against the unreacted vehicle under test before its response, and the monitor of the same specification decides acceptance. On nuScenes and CommonRoad, judged independently of the specification, one parameter set produced valid scenarios at a rate 0.14 above a weighted objective tuned per scenario type, and descriptions yielded at least one valid scenario for 11 of the 16 pre-crash types expressible in the public data. Learned planners fail more often on the generated scenarios than on their seeds. Fine-tuning on them lowers a planner's failure rate on critical scenarios, and the time headway one clause of the label requires, not the generator, sets the size of the gain.

</details>

<details>
<summary><b>中文摘要</b></summary>

In guided diffusion models, each scenario type needs its own weighted objective, separate from the validity check. Fine-tuning on them lowers a planner's failure rate on critical scenarios, and the time headway one clause of the label requires, not the generator, sets the size of the gain.

</details>

## 120. SparQ4D: Learning to Act through Sparse 4D Queries

**中文标题**: SparQ4D: Learning to Act through Sparse 4D Queries

**作者**: Siyuan Xie, Jinxi Xiao, Ran Ji, Yikai Liu, Mingxi Li et al.

**发布日期**: 2026-11-22

**arXiv ID**: [2611.29682v2](http://arxiv.org/abs/2611.29682v2)

**PDF**: [下载链接](http://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.29682v2)

**类别**: cs.RO

<details>
<summary><b>摘要 (Abstract)</b></summary>

Successful manipulation requires a policy to understand where things are and how they will move, yet action demonstrations leave this spatiotemporal structure implicit. While explicit 4D supervision has proven effective, reducing the cost of dense 4D prediction remains challenging. This paper introduces SparQ4D, a lightweight yet expressive decoder that efficiently injects holistic 4D scene understanding into policies and improves success rates. At its core is a query-based decoder attached to a pretrained policy, which casts many spatialtemporal tasks into one unified interface (e.g. depth, 2D/3D point motion, multi-view correspondence, and hand-eye relations). This design enables efficient training and inference without sacrificing expressivity: the policy learns rich 4D structure from a small number of sampled queries during training, while the decoder can be removed at inference with zero additional overhead. Across multiple backbones, simulated environments, and real-world settings, SparQ4D achieves state-of-the-art manipulation performance, outperforming competing methods while training up to $5\times$ faster. Probing analyses further reveal that this 4D structure is recoverable from the policy.

</details>

<details>
<summary><b>中文摘要</b></summary>

While explicit 4D supervision has proven effective, reducing the cost of dense 4D prediction remains challenging. At its core is a query-based decoder attached to a pretrained policy, which casts many spatialtemporal tasks into one unified interface (e.g. depth, 2D/3D point motion, multi-view correspondence, and hand-eye relations). This design enables efficient training and inference without sacrificing expressivity: the policy learns rich 4D structure from a small number of sampled queries during training, while the decoder can be removed at inference with zero additional overhead. Probing analyses further reveal that this 4D structure is recoverable from the policy.

</details>

## 121. Action Windows Are Inherently Temporal: A Benchmark for Time-Sensitive Robotic Manipulation

**中文标题**: Action Windows Are Inherently Temporal: A Benchmark for Time-Sensitive Robotic Manipulation

**作者**: Haoxiang Zhan, Haoqin Hong, Congcong Zhu, Jingrun Chen

**发布日期**: 2026-11-22

**arXiv ID**: [2611.29558v1](http://arxiv.org/abs/2611.29558v1)

**PDF**: [下载链接](http://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.29558v1)

**项目主页**: https://eyja42.github.io/await-site/.

**类别**: cs.RO, cs.CV

<details>
<summary><b>摘要 (Abstract)</b></summary>

Vision-language model (VLM) agents have surpassed learned-policy baselines on existing robotic manipulation benchmarks. However, these agents still struggle with time-sensitive everyday tasks that existing benchmarks for VLM agents cover only partially. In these tasks, evolving physical processes create finite windows for successful intervention. These action windows are inherently temporal and impose a shared time budget on decision making and physical execution. Coordinating intervention within this budget requires agents to determine when the window opens. We introduce AWAIT, a simulation benchmark that evaluates task completion within action windows while accounting for decision and execution time. Our evaluation reveals difficulties in recognizing window onset and shows that successive decisions and movements can exhaust an action window even when each decision takes less than the window duration. Project page: https://eyja42.github.io/await-site/.

</details>

<details>
<summary><b>中文摘要</b></summary>

Vision-language model (VLM) agents have surpassed learned-policy baselines on existing robotic manipulation benchmarks. However, these agents still struggle with time-sensitive everyday tasks that existing benchmarks for VLM agents cover only partially. In these tasks, evolving physical processes create finite windows for successful intervention. These action windows are inherently temporal and impose a shared time budget on decision making and physical execution. Coordinating intervention within this budget requires agents to determine when the window opens. We introduce AWAIT, a simulation benchmark that evaluates task completion within action windows while accounting for decision and execution time. Our evaluation reveals difficulties in recognizing window onset and shows that successive decisions and movements can exhaust an action window even when each decision takes less than the window duration. Project page: https://eyja42.github.io/await-site/.

</details>

## 122. Detecting Is Not Choosing: What a World Model Needs to Keep a VLA Policy Safe

**中文标题**: Detecting Is Not Choosing: What a World Model Needs to Keep a VLA Policy Safe

**作者**: Thanish Bolla

**发布日期**: 2026-11-22

**arXiv ID**: [2611.29553v1](http://arxiv.org/abs/2611.29553v1)

**PDF**: [下载链接](http://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.29553v1)

**类别**: cs.RO, cs.AI, cs.LG

<details>
<summary><b>摘要 (Abstract)</b></summary>

Vision-language-action (VLA) policies complete most benchmark tasks but often violate safety constraints. Since they are stochastic, one remedy is to sample several candidate action chunks and execute one that a world model predicts is safe. We ask what such a model must do. In simulation, a lookahead safety filter with the simulator as an exact world model raises the safety rate of three VLA policies on LIBERO and of pi0.5 on LIBERO-Plus by 11.5 to 25.7 points. It also helps pi0.5 on three RoboCasa tasks. Learned critics trained on one policy's data detect dangerous states (pooled AUROC up to 0.864) but rank candidates at one state close to chance (within-decision AUROC 0.551-0.560), and the learned dynamics and video models we test do no better. When the simulator's state is perturbed, selection degrades long before detection. In the loop, a median position error of 3.18 mm removes 70% of the gain on LIBERO-Spatial, partly because the error also corrupts the displacement check. On LIBERO-10 the loss is smaller, and the intervals overlap. The median does not predict the cost: a pixel reader with a 0.56 mm median error keeps 6.5 points less of the gain than Gaussian error of a similar median. A world model for safety should thus be judged by within-state ranking, and its state must be accurate in the tail and on handled objects, not only in the median.

</details>

<details>
<summary><b>中文摘要</b></summary>

Vision-language-action (VLA) policies complete most benchmark tasks but often violate safety constraints. Since they are stochastic, one remedy is to sample several candidate action chunks and execute one that a world model predicts is safe. We ask what such a model must do. In simulation, a lookahead safety filter with the simulator as an exact world model raises the safety rate of three VLA policies on LIBERO and of pi0.5 on LIBERO-Plus by 11.5 to 25.7 points. It also helps pi0.5 on three RoboCasa tasks. Learned critics trained on one policy's data detect dangerous states (pooled AUROC up to 0.864) but rank candidates at one state close to chance (within-decision AUROC 0.551-0.560), and the learned dynamics and video models we test do no better. When the simulator's state is perturbed, selection degrades long before detection. A world model for safety should thus be judged by within-state ranking, and its state must be accurate in the tail and on handled objects, not only in the median.

</details>

## 123. A ROS2 based Human-In-The-Loop Framework for UAV Search and Rescue Operations

**中文标题**: A ROS2 based Human-In-The-Loop Framework for UAV Search and Rescue Operations

**作者**: Anubhav Pandey, Harshit Nagpal, Aditya Agarwal, Tejasvi Alladi, Fei Richard Yu

**发布日期**: 2026-11-22

**arXiv ID**: [2611.29507v1](http://arxiv.org/abs/2611.29507v1)

**PDF**: [下载链接](http://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.29507v1)

**类别**: cs.RO, cs.CV

<details>
<summary><b>摘要 (Abstract)</b></summary>

Existing Unmanned Aerial Vehicle (UAV) autonomy frameworks lack human contextual awareness, limiting real-time adaptability in unstructured Search and Rescue (SAR) environments. To address data availability, we propose a Robot Operating System (ROS2)-based human-in-the-loop (HITL) framework that translates human directional gestures (left/right) into UAV navigation commands using RGB-Depth (RGB-D) sensing and YOLOv11 (You Only Look Once). The deprecation of ROS1 necessitates migrating to ROS2, prioritising real-time communication, scalability, and long-term maintainability. Unlike approaches optimised for peak accuracy under ideal conditions, the proposed system prioritises robustness and real-time deployment in degraded SAR environments. Our system achieves 89% confidence in depth estimation under occlusion and varying environmental conditions in Gazebo-PX4 simulations. In foggy conditions, RGB-D fusion consistently provides more informative cues than RGB alone, with an average improvement of 46.7% across all scenarios and distances. The framework demonstrates superior occlusion handling (+15%), adaptive RGB-D fusion, temporal consistency modelling, and real-time deployment via a ROS2 data distribution service (DDS), with end-to-end latency below 50 ms. We evaluate the modified YOLOv11 and the depth-weighted pose estimation on a held-out simulated test set and on real people from the public UAV-GESTURE dataset, in clear air and in Koschmieder fog. Without retraining, the framework reads the gestures of real people with 80% accuracy, depth keeps 77% at a visibility of 10 m where RGB drops to 24%, and the modified YOLOv11 reaches 91% in simulation and 99% on held-out real people when trained on real data. The open-source ROS2 implementation eases the transition of SAR-UAV platforms from ROS1, enabling UAVs to act directly on operator gestures without complex path planning.

</details>

<details>
<summary><b>中文摘要</b></summary>

Existing Unmanned Aerial Vehicle (UAV) autonomy frameworks lack human contextual awareness, limiting real-time adaptability in unstructured Search and Rescue (SAR) environments. To address data availability, we propose a Robot Operating System (ROS2)-based human-in-the-loop (HITL) framework that translates human directional gestures (left/right) into UAV navigation commands using RGB-Depth (RGB-D) sensing and YOLOv11 (You Only Look Once). The deprecation of ROS1 necessitates migrating to ROS2, prioritising real-time communication, scalability, and long-term maintainability. The framework demonstrates superior occlusion handling (+15%), adaptive RGB-D fusion, temporal consistency modelling, and real-time deployment via a ROS2 data distribution service (DDS), with end-to-end latency below 50 ms. We evaluate the modified YOLOv11 and the depth-weighted pose estimation on a held-out simulated test set and on real people from the public UAV-GESTURE dataset, in clear air and in Koschmieder fog. Without retraining, the framework reads the gestures of real people with 80% accuracy, depth keeps 77% at a visibility of 10 m where RGB drops to 24%, and the modified YOLOv11 reaches 91% in simulation and 99% on held-out real people when trained on real data. The open-source ROS2 implementation eases the transition of SAR-UAV platforms from ROS1, enabling UAVs to act directly on operator gestures without complex path planning.

</details>
